# Using and maintaining the checking aid

The design, the implementation plan and the evaluation protocol are in the thesis repository (Luuuuuig/Bachelor-End-Project, folder `docs/`).
This manual was written for the state of the code at commit 75207cc of the thesis repository's work branch and updated for Task 25 (the pre-check in the browser) on 8 November 2026; where the code changes, the code is right and this manual must follow. The aid README (`aid/README.md`) has the install and the "Try it out" steps on the made-up set.

## 1. What the aid does

The checking aid is a Python program that checks orders for the buyer. It does two checks.

- **The post-check.** A supplier sends an order confirmation. The aid compares it with the purchase order (PO): every line, the price, the quantity, the unit, the delivery date, missing or extra lines and the required information (the Incoterm).
- **The pre-check.** Before ordering in a webshop, a browser extension sends the cart page the buyer has open to the aid's capture server on the same computer. The aid compares the cart with the PO at once (price, quantity, unit and whether each line is in the cart) and the extension shows the result in a banner above the page, before the buyer presses order. A cart page saved from the browser can still go through the batch command instead (section 2.5).

**What a document is compared with.** Both checks compare a document with the purchase order as the aid knows that Exact holds it: the version of the order that the document answers, as Exact printed or exported it, with the values laid over it that were entered in Exact after the aid filed that version. A value counts as entered once the buyer ticked its change on the tab "In Exact invoeren", or once the aid saw it in a newer PDF or export of the order (section 2.6). So a second confirmation with the price the buyer already entered shows no price difference, and one that returns to the old price shows one, against the entered price. A version or an export that the aid filed after the tick is compared as it is: it is the order after the change. Which version a document answers is chosen as before, among the versions as they were filed. Nothing reads Exact: a tick is the buyer's word, and the aid believes it until a PDF or an export of the order that the aid is given after the tick shows another value for that line, whether that print shows anything new or not. Such a print ends the tick: the change stands on the list again, and from then on documents are compared with the order as it was printed (section 2.6). A value belongs to a line of the order, found by its article, not to a line number: a PDF and an export may number the lines differently. Where one article stands on several lines (two deliveries of it), the line is told by its quantity, its delivery date and its price.

**The flow of a confirmation.**

1. The e-mails for the aid arrive in a folder, the inbox. After a sign-in with the buyer's own Microsoft account on the settings tab of the review page, the runner reads one folder of the work mailbox itself and saves each new e-mail there (section 3.14); without a sign-in, a mailbox rule saves them there, as before (section 3.3). A document that does not come in by itself the buyer gives on the review page's tab "Bestanden", which puts it into the inbox (section 2.7).
2. The runner looks at the inbox every 60 seconds. It files PO PDFs as versions of their PO. For each order confirmation it opens a case. Other documents (packing slips, invoices, quotations) are skipped and listed in the digest. The tab "Bestanden" says what became of each file.
3. The aid reads the confirmation, picks the PO version it answers, lays the values entered in Exact since that version was filed over it, pairs the confirmation's lines with the PO lines and compares the values by fixed rules. It reads with the AI service, Microsoft 365 Copilot, used with the buyer's own work sign-in (section 3.15), or without AI with the template parser (section 3.12). Copilot reads the text layer of a PDF only: a document without one (a scan) is not read, and its case goes to the buyer, listed as failed in the digest.
4. The routing decides per finding: the aid handles the change itself (AUTO), which as installed means that it puts the change on the list "In Exact invoeren" for the buyer to enter (section 2.6), asks the supplier a standard question (ASK), or hands the item to the buyer (REVIEW).
5. The aid prepares the routine changes for the buyer to enter, sends the questions and records everything in the case folder. It writes nothing in Exact itself, and no installation does (section 3.10).
6. Once a day the buyer gets a digest: what the aid did, with "(voorbereid)" behind each change it prepared and the number of changes still to enter (an undo link stands next to a change only where a program made it in Exact, which no installation does), and a link to the review page for each case that waits for him.

**The rules as the buyer experiences them.** The rule codes appear only in the records, never on the page or in the digest. The settings each rule reads are named in brackets.

| Rule | What happens |
|---|---|
| R11 | A new supplier (fewer closed cases than `first_cases_review`) or a supplier the buyer marked (`review_suppliers`): the whole case comes to the buyer, whatever the findings. |
| R09 | The aid could not pick a PO version, or could not compare a value (another currency, a total only, an uncertain match, an unreadable value and similar): the item comes to the buyer. |
| R02 | The delivery date is earlier than the PO date, or later but within the supplier's lead time plus the tolerance (`supplier_lead_times`, `lead_time_tolerance_days`): the aid takes the new date for the PO line and puts it on the list "In Exact invoeren" for the buyer to enter. |
| R03 | The delivery date is later than that, missing on the document, the lead time is unknown, or the line is split: the item comes to the buyer. |
| R04 | The price differs by at most `price_pct` percent of the PO price and at most `price_abs` euro on the line: the aid takes the new price for the PO line and for the article and puts both on the list "In Exact invoeren" for the buyer to enter. |
| R05 | The price differs by more, is missing, the quantity is unknown, or the line is split: the item comes to the buyer. |
| R06 | The quantity or the unit differs: the aid asks the supplier (`messages_off_suppliers`). |
| R07 | A line is missing or extra: the aid asks the supplier. A line that is not in a cart comes to the buyer. |
| R08 | Required information is missing or different: the aid asks the supplier. |
| R10 | The PO is above the authorization limit (`authorization_limit`): the aid prepares its date or price change, as R02 and R04 do, and asks the approver to approve the PO again, with the buyer in copy. The request says why the order is being changed: because the supplier's order confirmation differs from the order, or, for a cart of the pre-check, which has no confirmation yet, because the cart at the supplier differs from the order. When a limit is set and the PO total is unknown, the change is not prepared and the item comes to the buyer. |
| R01 | When nothing waits for the buyer or the supplier, the aid prepares the confirmation to attach to the PO and its status from Besteld to Bevestigd. When a question or a review item is open, it prepares the confirmation to attach only, if it prepared other changes. Neither is a row to tick on the tab "In Exact invoeren": the tab lists the confirmations to attach at its bottom for 7 days after the aid last prepared the attachment or the status (section 2.6), the digest marks both "(zelf in Exact doen)", and the buyer attaches the confirmation and sets the status Bevestigd in Exact himself, as he does today (section 2.1). |
| R00 | A finding that no rule covers comes to the buyer. |

On a cart, every question of R06, R07 and R08 comes to the buyer instead ("cart: for the buyer"), and nothing is attached and no status is set. R04 works on a cart as on a confirmation: a small price difference is prepared for the PO line's price and for the article price, rows on the list "In Exact invoeren", before the order is placed, and the banner says so. For a supplier in `messages_off_suppliers` the questions come to the buyer instead ("messages off for this supplier").

**What the aid never does.**

- It never creates, releases or sends a PO, and never pays anything.
- It changes nothing but the PO line's price, delivery date and quantity, the article's purchase price, the attached confirmation and the confirmation status.
- It sends a supplier only the three standard questions, always with the buyer in copy, and the approver only the request to approve a PO again.
- It only reads the folder of the work mailbox it is connected to: it never moves, flags or deletes a message there. When it sends through that mailbox, each message is first a draft there, which then goes to Sent Items (section 3.14).
- It never says "no difference" when it could not compare a value.
- It never makes a change without a line in the write log that holds the old value, so that the change can be undone.
- In the Bachelor End Project it never writes to the live system: only to the copy of the test account, or as prepared changes.
- It never sends a company document, or anything taken from one, to an AI service outside the company's Microsoft tenant.

## 2. For the buyer

### 2.1 The daily digest

The digest is an e-mail to the buyer, from the aid's mailbox, or from the work mailbox when the aid sends through it (section 3.14). Its subject is "[Inkoopassistent] Dagoverzicht DD-MM-YYYY" (in English "[Inkoopassistent] Daily digest DD-MM-YYYY"). It comes once a day, at the first pass of the runner at or after `digest_time` (16:30 by default), or, when `digest` is `per_case`, after every pass that has a case or a skipped file to report. If the company allows a Power Automate flow, the same text also arrives in Teams (section 3.9).

The digest has up to five blocks. An empty block is left out.

| Block (Dutch) | Block (English) | What it lists |
|---|---|---|
| Behandelde zaken | Handled cases | Every case handled since the last digest, one line each: PO, supplier, status and the number of changes. Under it, a line per change: the field, the line or article, old value → new value. A change made in the system has a link "ongedaan maken" (undo). A prepared change of a price, a delivery date, a quantity or an article's purchase price says "(voorbereid)": it is not in Exact yet and stands on the tab "In Exact invoeren" (section 2.6). The confirmation to attach and the status Bevestigd say "(zelf in Exact doen)" (do it in Exact yourself): the aid prepares them and shows them, without a tick, at the bottom of the tab "In Exact invoeren" for 7 days after the aid last prepared the attachment or the status (section 2.6), and the case's line counts them apart, as "in Exact zelf te doen" ("PO 250101 (S01): afgesloten, 2 in Exact zelf te doen"). A failed one says "(mislukt)". None of these changed anything, so there is nothing to undo. |
| Wacht op u | Waiting for you | The cases in review, each with a link "beoordelen" (review) and a line per item with its reason, field and line. |
| Vraag gesteld aan de leverancier | Question sent to the supplier | The cases that wait for a supplier's answer, each with the PO, the supplier and the kind of each question (for example "aantal of eenheid"). |
| Mislukt | Failed | The cases the aid could not finish, with the first line of the error. Tell the researcher. A confirmation that is a scan, which Copilot cannot read (section 3.15), says "Scan zonder leesbare tekst: de assistent kon dit document niet lezen; bekijk het zelf": handle that document yourself. |
| Overgeslagen | Skipped | Files in the inbox that opened no case, with the reason (for example "invoice", "duplicate", "more than one PO number"). A scan that the aid could not class says "Scan zonder leesbare tekst: bekijk dit document zelf", the words that the tab "Bestanden" uses for it. |

A day without anything says "Er zijn geen zaken behandeld en geen bestanden overgeslagen." Under the title, before the blocks, the first line says "In Exact in te voeren: 2 wijzigingen. Open het tabblad In Exact invoeren: <link>" while changes wait on the review page's tab "In Exact invoeren" (section 2.6); its link opens the page on that tab. When the list cannot be read (a file of it is held by another program, or damaged), that line says instead "De lijst In Exact invoeren kon niet worden gelezen. Open het tabblad om te zien wat er nog in te voeren is: <link>", so that a day with changes waiting never looks like a day with nothing to enter. A line says "Postvak niet verbonden" while the mailbox's sign-in has lapsed (section 5), and a line "Copilot werkt niet (<the error>): <n> zaken wachten erop" while Copilot fails, with the number of cases waiting for it (section 3.15). A pre-check case begins with the word "voorcontrole". Prices use the decimal comma in Dutch.

**The links.** "beoordelen" opens the review page with that case selected. "ongedaan maken" opens the page with that case and that change selected; nothing is undone until you press the undo button on the page. The links work only in a browser on the computer where the review page runs (section 3.8).

**You enter the changes in Exact.** The aid changes nothing in Exact itself (section 3.10): every change of a price, a delivery date, a quantity or an article's purchase price that the aid makes, and every one you confirm on the review page, is a prepared change, marked "(voorbereid)", and stands on the tab "In Exact invoeren" until you have entered it in Exact and ticked it, the aid saw it in a new PDF of the purchase order or in an export from Exact, or you chose not to enter it (section 2.6). The confirmation to attach and the status Bevestigd are no rows to tick: where the digest says "(zelf in Exact doen)", attach the confirmation and set the status Bevestigd in Exact yourself, as you do today; the tab "In Exact invoeren" shows each confirmation at its bottom for 7 days after the aid last prepared the attachment or the status, so that a status prepared at a later close shows there as well. The digest then has no undo links: a prepared change is not in Exact, so there is nothing to undo.

**A try-out in the digest.** A case made from a try-out confirmation (section 3.17) has " (proefdocument)" behind its line, in every block, and each of its rows says "(proefdocument)" in place of "(voorbereid)" and "(zelf in Exact doen)": "PO 250108 (S08): afgesloten, 4 voorbereide wijzigingen (proefdocument)". Do nothing in the real Exact for it: it is made up. The first line's count "In Exact in te voeren" leaves its changes out. So it is in the test mode and after the test mode was switched off, and in the English digest "(try-out document)".

### 2.2 The review page

**Opening it.** Click a link in the digest, or open http://127.0.0.1:7860 in a browser on the computer where the page runs. If that port is taken, the command takes the next free one and prints the address, and `review_url` must then name that port. The page is in Dutch. The switch "Taal" at the top changes it to English ("Nederlands" / "English"). The switch changes the page only; the digest and the questions follow the language of the settings. The page has five tabs: "Beoordelen" (this section), "In Exact invoeren" (section 2.6), "Prijslijst" (section 4.3), "Bestanden" (section 2.7) and "Instellingen" (section 2.8). The link of the digest's line "In Exact in te voeren" opens the page on the tab "In Exact invoeren".

**The case list.** The dropdown "Zaak" (Case) shows the cases in review, and any case with a change made in the system that is not undone yet. Each case reads like "PO 250106 (S06), 2026-11-05 14:18, 3 punten": the PO, the supplier, the time the case was opened and the number of items to decide. A case that is not in review also shows its status. A case made from a try-out confirmation (section 3.17) has "(proefdocument)" behind it, so that it never looks like a supplier's confirmation of the same order. The button "Lijst vernieuwen" (Refresh the list) reloads the list. Cases that wait for a supplier, and closed or failed cases without an undoable change, are not in the list.

**The two documents.** Side by side: "Document van de leverancier" (the supplier's document, page by page) and "Inkooporder" (the PO version the aid chose). A cart saved as a web page, and a PO taken from an ERP export, have no pages to show; their values are in the lists.

**The three lists.**

- "Afwijkingen" (Differences): the differences, and the items the buyer must decide.
- "Overeenkomsten" (Matches): the values that agree.
- "Niet te vergelijken" (Could not compare): the values the aid could not compare, with the reason.

Each list has the columns Punt (item), Veld (field), Regel (line), Inkooporder (the PO value), Leverancier (the supplier's value), Reden (reason), Bron inkooporder and Bron leverancier (where the value stands: page, line and the text of that line). Items to decide are numbered RI1, RI2 and so on.

**Values entered in Exact.** A case is compared with the order as Exact holds it by the tab "In Exact invoeren" (section 2.6). When values were entered in Exact after the order version of the case was filed, a line above the lists names them: "Sinds deze inkooporder is opgenomen, is in Exact ingevoerd: regel 1: prijs 12,70. De assistent vergelijkt met deze waarden." Several values are separated by semicolons, "regel 1: prijs 12,70; regel 2: leverdatum 20-11-2026", and a week reads as "week 48 (2026)". The pane "Inkooporder" still shows the PDF as Exact printed it, with the old value. In the lists the column Inkooporder holds the entered value, with nothing under "Bron inkooporder", and a change you confirm goes from the entered value to the supplier's. An order version or an export that the aid filed after your tick is compared as it is, and its case has no such line. When the list could not be read while the case ran (another program held `prepared_changes.csv` or `marks.jsonl`, section 2.6), the line reads "De lijst In Exact invoeren kon niet worden gelezen: de assistent vergelijkt met de inkooporder zoals zij is opgenomen.": that case was compared with the order as it was filed, so a difference you had entered already may show again. Such a case always comes to you, with that sentence as the reason of its items, or of one item of its own when the order was also found by a guess (section 2.4): the aid did not know what Exact holds, so it prepared no change, requested no approval and asked the supplier nothing. Check such a difference in Exact before you decide.

**The four decisions.** Under "Besluiten" (Decisions) each item has a row with the choice "Besluit". A choice is saved at once ("Besluit over RI1 opgeslagen."); nothing is prepared or written until you close the case. A close prepares its changes, of which a price, a delivery date, a quantity or an article's purchase price then stands on the tab "In Exact invoeren" for you to enter in Exact (section 2.6), and sends its e-mails, a question to the supplier or the request to the approver, in the way set under "Instellingen", "Koppelingen" (section 3.7); nothing goes to Exact. The last choice on an item counts.

| Decision | What the close does |
|---|---|
| Bevestigen: waarde van de leverancier overnemen (Confirm: take the supplier's value) | On a price, delivery date or quantity of one PO line: prepares the supplier's value for the PO line, a row on the tab "In Exact invoeren". On any other item it writes nothing: handle it outside the aid. On an item about the whole case (for example "new supplier"; it names no finding) it means "seen, fine". |
| Waarde van de inkooporder houden (Keep the purchase order's value) | Writes nothing and sends nothing. On an item about the whole case it also means "seen, fine". |
| Leverancier vragen (Ask the supplier) | The standard question for that field, one e-mail per kind of question. The page sends it when the case is closed, in the way set under "Instellingen", "Koppelingen" (through the work mailbox or the aid's own mailbox, section 3.7), with the buyer in copy, as the aid sends its own questions. The case then waits for the answer (section 2.3). When the question cannot be sent, nothing is recorded as asked, the case stays in review with the item "Bericht niet verzonden", and the page says why (below). A question can be asked again: once the question about an item has ended (the supplier answered, the e-mail bounced, or no answer came in time), the item shows no choice, and "Leverancier vragen" chosen on it again sends the question again at the next close. Not offered on an item about the whole case (it has no field, for example "new supplier"), nor on a cart, nor on an item that asks you to look in Exact after a write of a program (section 2.4). |
| Onopgelost (Unresolved) | Nothing happens; the case stays in review. |

**The article tick.** On a price item the tick "Ook de artikelprijs bijwerken" (Also update the article price) appears. With the tick and "Bevestigen", the close also prepares the new price for the article, a row "artikel <code>: inkoopprijs ..." after the rows of the orders on the tab "In Exact invoeren". The tick is set already when the aid had tried that article change itself and it waits for you.

**The close.** The button "Zaak afsluiten" (Close the case) carries out the decisions. The page then says the new status, for example "De zaak is nu: afgesloten." The case closes when every item is "Bevestigen" or "Houden", nothing went wrong and no question to the supplier is open; a question is open while it has no reply of its own (section 2.3). Then the aid prepares the confirmation to attach and the status Bevestigd, which the tab "In Exact invoeren" shows at its bottom, without a tick, for 7 days after the aid last prepared the attachment or the status: attach the confirmation and set the status Bevestigd in Exact yourself (the digest says "(zelf in Exact doen)"). The case waits for the supplier when an item is on "Leverancier vragen" and its question is out, or when another question of the case is open: the order is then not confirmed, and nothing is prepared that the answer was to allow. It stays in review when an item is undecided or "Onopgelost", or when a message could not be sent. An item whose question has ended (answered, bounced, or without an answer in time) shows no choice any more and counts as undecided: its "Leverancier vragen" was carried out and is over. Choose again on it, "Bevestigen", "Houden", or "Leverancier vragen" once more, which the close then sends as a new question with a wait of its own. What the page says after a close stays on the screen until you choose another case or press a button.

What a close writes, for the record: each change in the write log of the case and in the prepared changes (the page always uses the prepared writer, section 3.7); the closes and undos in `steps/09_close.json` and in the audit record; a row per decision in the case's `dispositions.csv`. Your choices themselves are in `decisions.jsonl`. A second close never repeats a change that was made already, and sends a question again only when you chose "Leverancier vragen" anew after the earlier question had ended.

**Messages after a close.**

- "Een schrijfactie is niet gelukt (...): ... De zaak blijft ter beoordeling." (A write did not go through.) The close stopped at that change; the reason is "De schrijfactie is geweigerd" (The write was refused) or the label of the item it became, such as "Voorbereide wijziging: met de hand doorvoeren". Only with a program's write route, which no installation has (section 3.10), the label can also be "Wijziging in Exact gemaakt, maar de controle erna lukte niet: kijk in Exact of de waarde klopt" or "Niet zeker of de wijziging in Exact is gemaakt: kijk in Exact welke waarde er staat": that write did not fail, Exact may hold the change, and the close stopped so that you look in Exact first (section 2.4).
- "Een bericht is niet verzonden; de zaak blijft ter beoordeling." (A message was not sent.) Nothing is recorded as asked, and the case gets the item "Bericht niet verzonden" (or "Geen e-mailadres van de leverancier" when the settings hold no address for the supplier: add it under "Leveranciers" on the settings tab), so that it still says so after you reload the page, and in the digest. The item needs no choice of its own: press "Zaak afsluiten" again once the cause is put right, and when the message is sent the item is settled. After the sentence the page names the kind of cause:
  - "De aanmelding bij Microsoft is verlopen: meld u opnieuw aan onder Instellingen, Koppelingen, en sluit de zaak opnieuw af." (The Microsoft sign-in has lapsed.)
  - "Het postvak of de mailserver was niet bereikbaar: sluit de zaak later opnieuw af." (The mailbox or the mail server could not be reached: no connection, or no answer in time.)
  - "Het postvak of de mailserver weigerde het bericht: geef dit door aan de onderzoeker." (The mailbox or the mail server refused the message.)
  - "Er is geen postvak verbonden: verbind een postvak onder Instellingen, Koppelingen, en sluit de zaak opnieuw af." (No mailbox is connected: the settings name no way to send that is there. Nothing is written as a file in its place.)
  - "Het wachtwoord van de mailserver is niet ingesteld: geef dit door aan de onderzoeker." (The mail server is set, and its password is not in the environment the page was started with, section 3.11.)
  - "De instellingen kunnen nu niet worden gelezen: er is niets verstuurd. Geef dit door aan de onderzoeker." (The settings file cannot be read at this moment, for example after an edit by hand that went wrong: the page sends nothing rather than by the settings it read before.)
  - "Het document van de zaak kan nu niet worden gelezen: er is niets verstuurd." (The case's document cannot be read at this moment, so the page cannot tell whether it is a try-out, about which nothing is ever sent. Close the case again in a moment.)
- "Het bericht is niet verstuurd maar als bestand klaargezet (cases/outbox): de instellingen laten berichten als bestand schrijven." (The message was not sent but written as a file.) The settings say `"send_via": "file"`, the owner's choice for a try-out or a run in which nothing may leave: the question or the request lies as a file in the folder the sentence names, nobody received it, and it counts as asked, so the case waits and comes back after the wait with "Geen antwoord binnen de wachttijd". A folder prepared for the demonstration (`python3 -m aid.demo --prepare-only`) has this setting, so its page always says this.
- "Niet zeker of het bericht is verstuurd: het postvak gaf geen bevestiging. Kijk bij Verzonden items. Staat het er niet, dan komt de zaak na de wachttijd bij u terug en stelt u de vraag opnieuw." (Not sure whether the message was sent.) The mailbox or the mail server took the message and the connection failed before it confirmed. The question counts as asked and the case waits: a reply to it is filed with the case as any other. The aid does not send it a second time, since the supplier would then hold the question twice.
- "Een antwoord bij deze zaak kan nu niet worden gelezen: sluit de zaak zo opnieuw af." (A reply filed with this case cannot be read now.) A reply or a bounce in the case's folder was held by another program at that moment, so the aid could not tell which question it answers. The close did nothing; press "Zaak afsluiten" again in a moment.
- "Vraag aan de leverancier staat open." (A question to the supplier is open.) The PO is not confirmed while a question has no reply of its own: the supplier may still answer it (section 2.3).
- "Deze zaak kan niet worden afgesloten: zij is niet meer ter beoordeling." The case was closed or changed in the meantime. Refresh the list.
- "Dit besluit kan hier niet worden genomen." (This decision cannot be taken here.) For example "Leverancier vragen" on an item about the whole case or on a cart (the page does not offer it there).

**The undo.** Below the close button: choose the change under "Schrijfactie" (Write) and press "Ongedaan maken" (Undo). Only changes a program made in Exact are offered, each named by the value Exact held before it and the new value, as the digest names it ("leeg" where the field was empty, "onbekend" where nothing was read); a prepared change is not in Exact, so it is not offered here, and while every change is prepared the list "Schrijfactie" stays empty. A prepared change you do not want, you drop on the tab "In Exact invoeren" ("Aangevinkte regels niet invoeren", section 2.6). The page says "Ongedaan gemaakt: ..." (Undone) when the undo was made in Exact, or "Niets ongedaan gemaakt: ..." when the change was undone already, or "Ongedaan maken is mislukt." when the undo failed. "Kies eerst een schrijfactie." means no change was chosen. Note for the operator: the review page, as its command builds it, uses the prepared writer, so an undo from the page is a prepared change too, which then stands on the list with "Terugzetten:" before it, and the page says "Nog niet teruggedraaid in Exact. Op de lijst staat nu: ..." instead of "Ongedaan gemaakt" (sections 2.6 and 3.10). A change that is in Exact and must go back, you put back from the tab "In Exact invoeren" with "Terugdraaien in Exact" (section 2.6).

**The re-approval banner.** "Deze wijziging stuurt de inkooporder opnieuw door de fiattering" (This change sends the purchase order through approval again) shows on a PO above the authorization limit. A close that changes a PO line then also asks the approver to approve the PO again, with you in copy.

**A case that waits for its purchase order.** Above the documents of a confirmation that names a purchase order the aid does not know yet, the page says, with the number the confirmation names: "Inkooporder 250101 kent de assistent nog niet. Komt die inkooporder uit Exact binnen, dan controleert de assistent deze bevestiging vanzelf opnieuw; u hoeft hier niets te besluiten. Blijft zij weg, geef dan de pdf van de inkooporder of de e-mail van Exact op het tabblad Bestanden. Is 250101 niet een van uw inkoopordernummers, besluit dan zelf over deze zaak." When the aid knows the order but not the version the confirmation quotes, it says: "Versie 1 van inkooporder 250103 kent de assistent nog niet. Komt die versie uit Exact binnen, dan controleert de assistent deze bevestiging vanzelf opnieuw; u hoeft hier niets te besluiten. Blijft zij weg, geef dan de pdf van die versie of de e-mail van Exact op het tabblad Bestanden." The tab "Bestanden" puts it into the folder the runner reads (section 2.7). The aid checks such a case again when that order, or that version, is filed: it checks the confirmation as a new case, and this case leaves the list (section 3.17). At the first pass after the runner starts every order of the store counts as filed, so a case is checked again then only when its order, or the version it quotes, is in the store. A decision you record on the case ends this: the hint goes at once, the aid no longer checks the case again by itself, and you decide on it as on any case. "Lijst vernieuwen" also hides the hint of a case decided in another browser tab. A confirmation on which the aid found no order number has no hint and waits for nothing: decide on it yourself (section 3.17 says when the aid still links it by itself). A confirmation whose order the aid found otherwise than by the number recognition (the reason of a guessed order, section 2.4) has no hint either and is not counted, as a guess makes no promise. When the aid does not know the version such a confirmation quotes, its case also has "Geen versie van de inkooporder gekozen": nothing was compared, and the order pane ("Inkooporder") stays empty because no version was chosen. Until you decide on such a case, the aid checks it again once that version is filed.

**Other messages.** "Kies eerst een zaak." means no case is chosen. "De besluiten van deze zaak kunnen niet worden gelezen (decisions.jsonl)." means the file with your choices is damaged; tell the researcher.

### 2.3 The supplier questions and the wait

The aid asks only three standard questions: about a quantity or unit ("aantal of eenheid"), about a missing or extra line ("ontbrekende of extra regel"), and about missing information ("ontbrekende gegevens"). Each is an e-mail to the supplier, from the aid's mailbox or from the work mailbox (section 3.14), the buyer in copy, with the subject "Vraag over orderbevestiging PO <number>" and a table of the lines (line, item code, description, our value, your value). It asks for an answer by reply within the wait, three working days by default; you set it on the settings tab ("Wachttijd op een antwoord", section 2.8). A question you ask on the review page ("Leverancier vragen", section 2.2) is the same e-mail, sent in the same way when you close the case.

- **The supplier replies** to the e-mail: the aid files the reply into the case, and the case comes back to you with the item "De leverancier heeft geantwoord: lees het antwoord" (The supplier answered: read the reply), on the page and in the digest. The page does not show the reply itself: read it in your mailbox (you were in copy, so a reply to all reaches you). If the answer needs a change, make it by hand, then choose "Bevestigen" or "Houden" on the item and close the case.
- **The e-mail bounces**: the case comes back at once with "Bericht kwam terug" (Message bounced). Correct the address under "Leveranciers" on the settings tab and choose "Leverancier vragen" on the item again.
- **No answer in time**: after the wait (Saturday and Sunday are skipped; public holidays are not) the case comes back with "Geen antwoord binnen de wachttijd" (No answer within the wait).

**A reply belongs to its question.** A reply answers the question it replies to, and no other: the aid reads from the reply which of its e-mails it answers (the e-mail its `In-Reply-To` header names, else the last one its `References` header names; the first of those that is a question of a case decides, also between two cases of one order). A case can have two questions out, two kinds of question from one check, or one the aid asked and one you asked on the page. It waits until each has a reply of its own. When the supplier answers one of them, the case comes back with the reply's item; when you have read the reply and close the case, the page says "De zaak is nu: wacht op de leverancier. Vraag aan de leverancier staat open.": the order is not confirmed, and the case waits on for the other question. When the wait of that question ends without a reply, the case comes back with "Geen antwoord binnen de wachttijd". With two questions without a reply, the wait runs from the one sent last. A supplier who answers two of the aid's e-mails in one reply answers the one that reply names; the other comes back after its wait as not answered, and you decide it from the reply you have. A bounce belongs to its question in the same way, and ends that question's wait only: when a later question bounces while an earlier one is still within its wait, the bounce comes back to you at once, and after you close the case it waits on for the earlier question, which comes back by itself when its own wait ends. Two questions that both bounce come back as two items. A question that has a reply of its own is answered, whatever non-delivery notice arrives of it besides (one of several recipients, the copy address): the notice is filed and named on the tab "Bestanden", and changes nothing.

**What counts as a reply.** A delivery report of the receiving mail server that names no e-mail in its own headers is filed by the message it returns, and it says itself what it is: a report of a failure is the bounce of its question, and a report of a delay or of a delivery is filed as an automatic message, which ends no wait and answers nothing. An out-of-office reply changes nothing either. An e-mail from your own address, from the aid's address or from the work mailbox that names a question (you are in copy of every question, so your own reply to all does) is no answer of the supplier: the tab "Bestanden" says "Uw eigen bericht bij de zaak gelegd; dit telt niet als antwoord van de leverancier", and the case waits on. An e-mail from any other address that names the question counts as the answer, since a supplier may answer from another address; you read it and decide.

After a question the aid asked itself, the differences stay in the list "Afwijkingen" without a decision row. Handle them outside the aid if needed, then decide on the new item (without a finding) and close. After a question you asked from the page, the item you decided on keeps its row: while its question is out it shows "Leverancier vragen", and once the question has ended (answered, bounced, or without an answer in time) it shows no choice. Choose "Bevestigen" or "Houden" on it, or "Leverancier vragen" to ask once more: the close then sends the question again, and it waits its own three working days. An item on which nothing is chosen keeps the case in review. A case that comes back is listed again under "Wacht op u" in the next digest.

**No case waits without a question.** A case waits only while a question of it is out. Should a case stand on "wacht op de leverancier" while none is (a reply put into the case's folder by hand, or a case left so by an earlier version), the runner brings it back at its next pass: with "De leverancier heeft geantwoord: lees het antwoord" when a reply lies with the case that you were not shown yet, else with "Er staat geen vraag meer open: besluit over deze zaak" (No question is open any more: decide on this case).

### 2.4 What the review reasons mean

The page shows each reason by its label. The digest uses the same labels.

| Reason in the records | Label (Dutch) | Label (English) | In plain words |
|---|---|---|---|
| new supplier | Nieuwe leverancier | New supplier | Until as many cases of this supplier are closed as "Eerste zaken van een leverancier" on the settings tab says, every case of the supplier comes to you, whatever it holds. |
| supplier marked for review | Leverancier gemarkeerd voor beoordeling | Supplier marked for review | You asked to see every case of this supplier. |
| PO found otherwise than by the number recognition | Kijk na of dit de goede inkooporder is: de assistent vond haar niet aan het nummer achter een van de woorden van de nummerherkenning. | Check that this is the right purchase order: the assistant did not find it by the number after a word of the number recognition. | The aid linked the confirmation to this order by a number of the order that stands on the document, by the item codes of its lines, or by choosing among several numbers, not by the one number after a word of the number recognition (section 3.17). That is a guess, so the aid wrote and asked nothing, as for a new supplier: check that the order on the right is the one the confirmation answers, then decide each item as usual. If it is another order, do not close the case: choose "Onopgelost" and handle the confirmation in Exact yourself. When the aid does not know the version the confirmation quotes, the case also has "Geen versie van de inkooporder gekozen": nothing was compared, and the order pane ("Inkooporder") is empty because no version was chosen. Such a case has no hint and is not counted (a guess makes no promise), and until you decide on it the aid checks it again once that version is filed. |
| the list of changes to enter in Exact could not be read | De lijst In Exact invoeren kon niet worden gelezen: de assistent vergelijkt met de inkooporder zoals zij is opgenomen. | The list Enter in Exact could not be read: the assistant compares with the purchase order as it was filed. | While the case ran, another program held `prepared_changes.csv` or `marks.jsonl`, or the first of them could not be understood (section 2.6, "What a tick is"). The aid then does not know which changes you entered in Exact, so it compared the document with the order as it was filed and prepared, requested and asked nothing by itself: every difference is yours to decide, and one you entered already may show again. Check such a difference in Exact before you decide. A document without a difference comes to you with this one item: decide it as any other and close the case. |
| PO chosen by the items in the cart | Kijk na of dit de goede inkooporder is: de assistent koos haar op de artikelen in de winkelwagen. | Check that this is the right purchase order: the assistant chose it by the items in the cart. | A cart of the pre-check (section 2.5) whose page states no order number the aid knows, and of whose order the aid is not sure: by the items it is sure only of the one order whose export was given on the day of the cart, that holds exactly the cart's items and is not sent. The aid compared the cart with the order that fits it best, which is a guess, so it prepared nothing: check that the order on the right is the one you are filling the cart for, then decide each item as usual and close the case, also when the cart equals the order and nothing differs: the case then has one item, this reason itself, and the banner is green once you decided it and closed the case. If it is another order, decide nothing; what helps then depends on that order. An order the aid does not hold yet: give the export of its order lines and reload the cart page; the aid then checks the cart against that order in a new case and finishes this one by itself. An order the aid holds already: another export of it changes nothing, since an export that shows no change files nothing. Where the webshop's cart has a field for your own reference, type the order's number there: the page then states the number, and the aid checks that cart against the order in a new case and finishes this one by itself as well. In both cases the aid finishes this case only while you decided nothing in it: a case in which you decided an item, also "Onopgelost", stays here as you left it, beside the new one, until you close it, which you do by choosing "Waarde van de inkooporder houden" for each item and pressing "Zaak afsluiten" (nothing is written). Where the webshop has no such field, the aid cannot compare the cart with that order: compare the two yourself and decide the items here. |
| no version selected | Geen versie van de inkooporder gekozen | No version of the purchase order chosen | The aid could not tell which PO version the document answers, or does not know the PO. When the confirmation names a PO the aid does not know, or a version of it the aid does not know, the page says so and the aid checks the case again by itself when that order or version comes in (section 2.2; a case whose order the aid guessed has no such hint, see the row above); otherwise decide on it yourself. |
| split line: parts differ | Gesplitste regel: de delen verschillen | Split line: the parts differ | The supplier split one PO line into parts with different values. |
| no delivery date on the document | Geen leverdatum op het document | No delivery date on the document | The supplier gave no date for the line. |
| no lead time known for the supplier | Geen levertijd bekend voor de leverancier | No lead time known for the supplier | The later date cannot be judged: the settings hold no lead time for this supplier. |
| delivery date beyond the lead time plus the tolerance | Leverdatum later dan de levertijd plus de marge | Delivery date later than the lead time plus the margin | The date is later than the aid may accept by itself. |
| no price on the document | Geen prijs op het document | No price on the document | The supplier gave no price for the line. |
| quantity unknown for the price rule | Aantal onbekend voor de prijscontrole | Quantity unknown for the price check | Without a quantity the euro limit cannot be checked. |
| price above threshold | Prijsverschil boven de drempel | Price difference above the threshold | The price differs more than the aid may accept by itself. |
| PO total unknown | Totaalbedrag van de inkooporder onbekend | Purchase order total unknown | The aid cannot tell whether the PO is above the authorization limit, so it changed nothing. |
| cart: for the buyer | Winkelwagen: voor de inkoper | Cart: for the buyer | A difference on a cart: correct the cart or the PO before ordering. |
| messages off for this supplier | De assistent stuurt deze leverancier uit zichzelf geen vragen | The aid sends this supplier no questions by itself | The aid would have asked the supplier, but this supplier has the tick "De assistent stuurt deze leverancier uit zichzelf geen vragen" on the settings tab, so the question comes to you. A question you choose yourself on the review tab is still sent. |
| could not be placed | Kon niet in de winkelwagen worden geplaatst | Could not be placed in the cart | A PO line is not in the cart. |
| not covered by a rule | Voor dit verschil is geen vaste aanpak | No set way of handling this difference | No rule covers this difference. |
| prepared change: apply by hand | Voorbereide wijziging: met de hand doorvoeren | Prepared change: apply it by hand | A program's write to Exact failed; the change is prepared instead. A price, a delivery date, a quantity or an article's purchase price then stands on the tab "In Exact invoeren", for you to enter; a confirmation to attach or a status you do in Exact yourself (sections 2.6 and 3.10). |
| write failed: ... | Schrijfactie mislukt | Write failed | The change could not be made or prepared. |
| write made, not verified: check in Exact | Wijziging in Exact gemaakt, maar de controle erna lukte niet: kijk in Exact of de waarde klopt | Change made in Exact, but the check after it failed: look in Exact whether the value is right | Only with a program's write route, which no installation has (section 3.10). Exact accepted the change, but the aid could not read the new value back, so it does not know whether Exact holds it. The change is logged as made, with the value Exact held before it, and stands among the writes to undo on this tab. Look in Exact, then decide. "Bevestigen": the change is in Exact and stays. The aid counts it as made, prepares nothing more for it and goes on with the rest of the case as after any item; when the order is above the authorization it then asks the approver. "Houden": the old value must stay. At the close the aid puts the value Exact held before back, as "Ongedaan maken" does (a row "Terugzetten: ..." on the tab "In Exact invoeren" while the page only prepares), and what waited behind the change is not carried out: no article price, no confirmation attached, no status, no request to the approver. A status or an attached confirmation is no row of that list: after "Houden" on such a change the page says "De assistent kan deze wijziging niet terugdraaien; zet de oude waarde zelf terug in Exact." and the case stays in review until the next close. "Leverancier vragen" is not offered. |
| write outcome not known: check in Exact | Niet zeker of de wijziging in Exact is gemaakt: kijk in Exact welke waarde er staat | Not sure whether the change was made in Exact: look in Exact which value is there | Only with a program's write route, which no installation has (section 3.10). The write got no answer over a connection that stood, or an answer that says neither made nor refused, and no read settled it afterwards: Exact may or may not hold the change. Nothing is prepared; the change is logged as made, with the value Exact held before it, and stands among the writes to undo. Look in Exact which value is there. The two decisions do what they do in the row above: "Bevestigen" when the new value is there and stays (if the old value is still there and you want the new one, enter it in Exact yourself first: the aid prepares nothing for it); "Houden" when the old value must stay (the put-back then asks for a value that Exact may hold already: tick it). |
| not applied: an earlier write failed ... | Niet uitgevoerd: een eerdere schrijfactie is mislukt | Not applied: an earlier write failed | The aid stopped writing on this PO after a failed change. |
| not applied: an earlier change waits for a look in Exact ... | Niet uitgevoerd: kijk eerst in Exact naar de eerdere wijziging | Not applied: look in Exact at the earlier change first | Only with a program's write route, which no installation has (section 3.10). The aid stopped writing on this PO after a change it could not verify, or whose outcome it does not know (the rows "write made, not verified: check in Exact" and "write outcome not known: check in Exact" above). No write failed: decide that item first. After "Bevestigen" there, this item is decided as any other; after "Houden" there, this change is not carried out, whatever you choose here. |
| no address for the supplier | Geen e-mailadres van de leverancier | No e-mail address of the supplier | The question was not sent: the settings hold no address for this supplier. |
| message not sent | Bericht niet verzonden | Message not sent | A message of the aid's own check was not sent: the mail server or the work mailbox did not take it, or the Microsoft sign-in of the work mailbox has lapsed (section 3.14). Once that is put right: for a question to the supplier, choose "Leverancier vragen" on the item and close the case, and the page sends it; a request to the approver you send yourself. A question or a request of a close on the page that was not sent adds this item too, without a line: the page says why after the close (section 2.2), and the item is settled, without a choice of its own, when a later close sends the message. |
| message bounced | Bericht kwam terug | Message bounced | The supplier's address did not accept the question. Correct the address and ask again (section 2.3). |
| no answer in N working days | Geen antwoord binnen de wachttijd | No answer within the wait | The supplier did not answer in time. |
| supplier answered: read the reply | De leverancier heeft geantwoord: lees het antwoord | The supplier answered: read the reply | The supplier replied to a question: read the reply in your mailbox, then decide the item and close the case. While another question of the case has no reply of its own, the case then waits on (section 2.3). |
| no question is open | Er staat geen vraag meer open: besluit over deze zaak | No question is open any more: decide on this case | The case stood on "wacht op de leverancier" while no question of it was out, so nothing would have brought it back: the runner did (section 2.3). Decide on its items and close the case. |
| run cut off after writes: check the write log | Andere reden | Another reason | The run stopped half way after a change; the researcher checks the write log first. |

The kind of difference, and why a value could not be compared:

| Reason in the records | Label (Dutch) | Label (English) | In plain words |
|---|---|---|---|
| different | Verschil | Different | The values differ. |
| missing on the document | Ontbreekt op het document | Missing on the document | The supplier left the value out. |
| missing line | Regel ontbreekt | Line missing | A PO line is not on the confirmation. |
| extra line | Extra regel | Extra line | The confirmation has a line the PO does not have. |
| not in cart | Niet in de winkelwagen | Not in the cart | A PO line is not in the cart. |
| later | Later dan besteld | Later than ordered | A later delivery date. |
| earlier | Eerder dan besteld | Earlier than ordered | An earlier delivery date. |
| different currency | Andere valuta | Another currency | The document uses another currency. |
| currency missing | Valuta ontbreekt op het document | Currency missing on the document | The PO states a currency and the document none. |
| unknown price basis | Prijseenheid onbekend | Price basis unknown | One side prices per 1, the other per 100 or similar, and the aid cannot tell. |
| unknown unit conversion | Omrekening van de eenheid onbekend | Unit conversion unknown | The units differ and the aid cannot convert them. |
| total only | Alleen een totaalbedrag | A total amount only | The document gives no price per line. |
| match uncertain | Koppeling van de regels onzeker | Line match uncertain | The aid is not sure which document line answers which PO line. |
| unreadable or unverified source | Bron onleesbaar of niet gecontroleerd | Source unreadable or not verified | A value could not be read with certainty. |
| no PO date | Geen leverdatum op de inkooporder | No delivery date on the purchase order | The PO has no date to compare with. |
| merged line | Samengevoegde regel | Merged line | The supplier put several PO lines on one line. |
| no PO price | Geen prijs op de inkooporder | No price on the purchase order | The PO has no price to compare with. |
| no PO quantity | Geen aantal op de inkooporder | No quantity on the purchase order | The PO has no quantity to compare with. |
| no PO unit | Geen eenheid op de inkooporder | No unit on the purchase order | The PO has no unit to compare with. |
| any other | Andere reden | Another reason | A reason without a label. |

### 2.5 The pre-check in the browser

**The extension.** A small extension for Edge or Chrome (`aid/extension`, loaded as its README describes) watches the pages of the webshops listed in its options. When a page holds a cart table (a table with an item column, a quantity column and a price column), it sends a copy of the page to the aid's capture server on the same computer, once per change of the cart: when the page opens, and again about a second after a quantity or a line changes. The server opens a cart case, checks it like any other case and answers at once. The case lands in the case folders like any other: on the review page when it waits for the buyer, and in the next digest under the pre-check ("voorcontrole"), which the runner makes when it runs on the same folder (section 3.13).

**The banner.** The answer appears in one banner at the top of the page. Its title is fixed, in Dutch with the English in brackets; the lines under it come from the aid in the language of the settings.

- **Green**, "Winkelwagen komt overeen met de inkooporder": the cart agrees with its PO, or the only differences were small price differences that the aid settled itself (R04). Those lines are listed under the title. While no route to Exact exists every change is prepared, and the line reads "regel 2 (KM-2102): prijs op de inkooporder: wijziging voorbereid van 4.10 naar 4.15, zie de zaak (prijs binnen de drempel)"; once changes are written to Exact it reads "regel 2 (KM-2102): prijs op de inkooporder bijgewerkt van 4.10 naar 4.15 (prijs binnen de drempel)". A case the buyer closed on the review page is green only when every difference is settled as it stands now: a line the buyer decided counts by the buyer's latest decision (taking the cart's value, with its change prepared or made and not undone, whoever made it; a change left to apply by hand does not count; keeping the PO's value leaves the difference), and a line the buyer did not decide by the change the aid's rules wrote or prepared; a line whose change was undone counts as a difference again ("wijziging ongedaan gemaakt"): undone in Exact by a program, or put back from the tab "In Exact invoeren" and that put-back entered or seen there; while the put-back still waits on that list, Exact holds the change, the line counts it as made and says "terugzetten staat op de lijst In Exact invoeren". The list decides this for every case that asked the change: when the same cart was checked again on another day, its later case is told of a put-back made through the first case's change as well. A cart that agreed with its order through a price you had entered and ticked (section 2.6) is judged by the list again each time it is shown: while that tick stands it is green; while a put-back of it waits on the list its line says "terugzetten staat op de lijst In Exact invoeren"; once the put-back is entered or seen the line is a difference again, "regel 2 (KM-2102): prijs 4.15 in de winkelwagen, 4.10 op de inkooporder (wijziging ongedaan gemaakt)"; and once the tick was taken back or ended, or another value was entered for the line, it is a difference with "(de lijst In Exact invoeren zegt nu iets anders)". A tick that the order as it stands contradicts (a print given after the tick, section 2.6) counts as ended at once, also before the runner's next pass has put the row back on the list. A cart that agreed with its order as it was printed is judged the same way: once you entered and ticked another value for one of its lines, that line is a difference, "regel 2 (KM-2102): prijs 4.10 in de winkelwagen, 4.16 op de inkooporder (de lijst In Exact invoeren zegt nu iets anders)". And a change that was prepared for a cart counts as prepared only while it stands on the list: when a later document named the value Exact holds again and the row left the list (section 2.6), the cart that asked it is a difference again, with the same words. So a cart shown again is never green where a new check of it would not be. A cart that is checked for the first time while the list cannot be read is compared with the order as it was filed and comes to you (section 2.2): amber, with the line "de lijst In Exact invoeren kon niet worden gelezen" under the title. While the list cannot be read (another program holds `prepared_changes.csv` or `marks.jsonl`), no prepared change counts as made, whatever case answers and whether it was put back or not: the line shows the difference between the cart and the order, with "de lijst In Exact invoeren kon niet worden gelezen", and the banner is amber until the list can be read again. That holds for a cart that agreed with its order as it was printed too: no cart is green while the list cannot be read, and the line "de lijst In Exact invoeren kon niet worden gelezen" then stands under the title.
- **Amber**, "Let op: de winkelwagen wijkt af van de inkooporder": one line per difference, in the order of the lines, for example "regel 2 (KM-2002): prijs 41.00 in de winkelwagen, 40.00 op de inkooporder (prijsverschil boven de drempel)", "regel 3 (KM-2003): kon niet in de winkelwagen worden geplaatst" or "regel 4 (KM-2004): extra regel, niet op de inkooporder". A reason about the whole case is a line of its own, for example "eerste zaken van deze leverancier gaan naar de inkoper". A small price difference that the aid wrote or prepared (R04) shows as such, with the text of the green banner, and is not counted as a difference: do not correct it again. A reason the aid has no wording for reads "zie de zaak voor de reden". When the buyer decided on a line on the review page, the line says so ("besluit van de inkoper: waarde van de inkooporder gehouden"). Correct the cart, or the PO, before ordering. For a cart of whose order the aid is not sure (below) the amber banner has another title, "Let op: kijk na of dit de goede inkooporder is", also when nothing differs; the aid sends that title with its answer, and a browser that loaded the extension before the aid sent it keeps the usual amber title until the extension is reloaded (section 3.13).
- **Grey**, "Geen inkooporder gevonden": the cart names no PO the aid knows, and no order the aid holds fits its items (below). The line under the title says what to do: "Geen inkooporder gevonden voor deze winkelwagen. Geef de assistent eerst de export van de orderregels uit Exact (tabblad Bestanden) en herlaad deze pagina na een minuut." Save the export of the order lines from Exact and give it to the aid, on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7), by e-mail to the folder of the mailbox that the aid reads, or in the folder `inbox/` (section 3.13). Once the aid has filed it, within a minute, reload the cart page: the aid checks the cart again, now against its order.
- **Red**, "Voorcontrole mislukt" when the check failed (the case's run failed, or the server met an error, with "the pre-check failed; see capture.log"), "Inkoopassistent niet bereikbaar" when the capture server does not run, "Inkoopassistent weigert de pagina" with the server's reason when it refused the page (a wrong token, "no cart table in ..." for a page the cart reader cannot read, or "Dit is een proefwinkelwagen: de assistent controleert hem alleen in de proefstand." for a try-out cart of the owner's test that is opened while the test mode is off, section 3.13). When the extension was updated while the page was open, the title is "Voorcontrole mislukt" with the line "Herlaad de pagina (reload the page)" under it: no check ran and no case exists; reloading the page is all it needs.

Under the title a sentence sums the case up ("Inkooporder 250202: 1 regel komt overeen, 1 verschil en 1 regel niet in de winkelwagen"); the lines follow, and under them the link "Bekijk de zaak". The link selects the case on the review page only while the case waits for the buyer (in review) or holds a change made in Exact that can be undone; any other case, a green or a failed one, or an amber one the buyer already closed, is read in its case folder.

**What a cart check does not compare.** The last line under a green and under an amber banner is always the same: "Niet vergeleken: levertijd, minimale afname of verpakkingseenheid, verzendkosten en leveringsvoorwaarden." The aid compares the lines of the cart with the lines of the order: the item, the quantity, the unit and the price of each. What the webshop says about the delivery time, a minimum or pack quantity, the delivery costs and the delivery terms it does not read, so a green banner says nothing about those: check them yourself before you order. The grey and the red banner have no such line.

**Which order a cart is checked against.** The number that the cart page states, when the aid knows that order. The cart reader finds the number after a word such as "Uw referentie", "Referentie", "Order", "Bestelling", "Inkooporder" or "PO", when it has six to eight digits and ends there: a number that goes on, as "250301-01", "250301A", "250301/2", "250301.1", "250301_01", "250301,5" and "250301:2" do, is another number, and no part of it is read. So where the webshop's cart has a field for your own reference, type the order's number there: when the page then shows the number after such a word, the aid finds the order by its number, whatever other orders hold the same items. A number the aid does not know as an order counts for nothing, since a webshop prints numbers of its own. Without a number it knows, the aid looks at the cart's items, and it is sure of an order only on what fits your way of working: you make the order in Exact, give its export, and fill the cart with every item of that order.

- **Found by the items.** Exactly one order whose export was given today, the day of the cart, holds exactly the cart's items, no item more and none fewer, and that order is not sent. That order is the cart's: the pre-check works as with a number, the aid settles a small price difference itself, and the sentence under the title says how the order was found: "Winkelwagen komt overeen met inkooporder 250931 (gekozen op de artikelen in de winkelwagen)". An order of one line is found so for a cart of that one item. An order counts as sent when the aid has its PO PDF, or when a supplier's order confirmation of it came in.
- **A guess, the aid is not sure.** Any other order that fits the cart is a guess: an order that shares at least two item codes with the cart, or holds exactly its items, but was exported on an earlier day, holds an item more or fewer than the cart, or is one of two orders of today with the same items. The aid compares the cart with one of them (first those of today, then those with exactly the cart's items, then those that are not sent, then those of the webshop's supplier, then the one filed last), but that is a guess: the banner is amber, the aid prepares nothing, also where it would have settled a small price difference itself, and every difference is yours to decide on the review page. The first line says why: "De assistent weet niet zeker welke inkooporder bij deze winkelwagen hoort. Vergeleken met inkooporder 250922: kijk na of dit de goede is. Is deze winkelwagen voor een nieuwe inkooporder, geef dan eerst de export van de orderregels uit Exact (tabblad Bestanden) en herlaad deze pagina na een minuut. De assistent heeft niets voorbereid."
- **A guess, the order was sent already.** When the order the cart is compared with was sent, the cart is more likely a new order whose export the aid does not have yet, and the first line reads: "De inkooporder die het best bij deze winkelwagen past (250950) is al verstuurd. Is dit een nieuwe bestelling, geef dan eerst de export van de orderregels uit Exact (tabblad Bestanden) en herlaad deze pagina na een minuut. De assistent heeft niets voorbereid."

So make the export of the order right before you fill the cart, on the same day: the aid is then sure of the order, and the banner can be green. On the review page the case of a guess says "Kijk na of dit de goede inkooporder is: de assistent koos haar op de artikelen in de winkelwagen." (section 2.4, which says what helps when the order is the wrong one). When it is the right order, decide each item of the case there and close it, also when the cart equals the order and nothing differs: the case then has one item, the order itself, and after that the banner is green. Until you closed the case the banner repeats the first line, in the words that fit at that moment: a cart that was "not sure" says that the order was sent already once the order's PDF came in.

**A reload opens no new case.** The same page sent again, byte for byte, is answered from its case on any day; the same cart in a page whose invisible parts changed (a time stamp, an advertisement) is answered from the case it opened that day. No second case is opened. A changed cart (another quantity, a line more or less) is a new case. A case that failed is never reused.

**A grey cart is checked again.** There are two exceptions to the rule above, both for a cart whose order the aid did not know for sure. A grey cart is answered from its case only while the aid still finds no order for it: once the export of its order is filed, the same page or the same cart opens a new case for that order, and the grey case is finished as replaced: it leaves the review page and the next digest, and its folder keeps the id of the new case (`replaced_by` in its `capture.json`). A grey case that you closed is left exactly as it is, and the new case answers from then on. In the same way a cart whose order was a guess is answered from its case only while that same order is still the aid's guess: when another order fits better or is found, or the aid is now sure of the order, as after the export of the new order was filed, it opens a new case, which answers from then on. A guess on which you did nothing is then finished as replaced. A guess on which you decided an item, also "Onopgelost", or that you closed, is never replaced and never changed: it stays on the review page as you left it, beside the new case. "Zaak afsluiten" pressed with no item decided closes nothing and counts as nothing done. A new case with an order also takes the place of the earlier cases of the same cart lines that still wait untouched, whatever number their page stated and whatever day they are of: the guess or the grey case of the cart before you typed the order's number into it, and yesterday's guess or grey case of the same cart. The extension sends a page when it opens and when the cart changes, so reload the page after you gave the export.

**The fallback.** A cart page saved from the browser as a web page (HTML) can still go through the batch command with its PO number (section 6), as before the extension existed. The watched folder does not take cart pages. A cart saved as a screenshot (PNG) cannot be read: Copilot, the AI service, reads the text layer of a document only (section 3.15), so save the cart page as HTML. On a cart, every difference comes to the buyer; "Leverancier vragen" is not offered, because the buyer corrects the cart or the PO before ordering.

### 2.6 The tab In Exact invoeren

The aid changes nothing in Exact itself: from your seat no program reaches Exact. Every change the aid makes by its rules, and every change you confirm on the tab "Beoordelen", is prepared, and the tab "In Exact invoeren" (Enter in Exact) of the review page lists what you enter in Exact. Its label counts the changes still to enter, "In Exact invoeren (3)"; without a number nothing waits, and "In Exact invoeren (?)" means that the list could not be read (below). The digest's first line gives the same number with the link to this tab (section 2.1).

**The list.** Under the line "De assistent verandert zelf niets in Exact. Hieronder staat wat u in Exact invoert. Vink aan wat u hebt ingevoerd." each change is one row with a tick box, one under the other. The rows come per purchase order, in the order of the order numbers, by line and then price, delivery date and quantity, and after them the article prices by article code. A row names its order, the line, the article of that line in the version of the order its case was compared with, and the old and the new value: "Inkooporder 250108 (S08), regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,50 → 12,70", "Inkooporder 250114 (S14), regel 1 (KM-1401): leverdatum 16-11-2026 → week 48 (2026)" or "artikel KM-0801: inkoopprijs onbekend → 12,70". A price or a quantity has the decimal comma, a day reads as dd-mm-yyyy and a week as "week 48 (2026)"; "onbekend" is a value the aid does not know, such as the old purchase price of an article it never read. Only the price, the delivery date and the quantity of an order line and the purchase price of an article are rows: attaching the confirmation and setting the status to Bevestigd are not on the list. The aid prepares them too, and the digest marks them "(zelf in Exact doen)": attach the confirmation and set the status Bevestigd in Exact yourself, as you do today. The tab shows them at its bottom, without a tick, under "Bevestigd door de leverancier" (below). With nothing to enter the tab says "Er is niets in te voeren." While the aid is in the test mode (section 3.17), the line under the intro says "Proefstand: voer deze wijzigingen alleen in het test-Exact in.": the rows then come from the test account of Exact, and from try-out confirmations, and go into the test Exact only. That line shows whenever the test mode is on, whatever the authorization limit, until the test mode is switched off on the settings tab. A change that only try-out confirmations asked carries "(proefdocument)" behind its row, in the test mode and out of it: "Inkooporder 250108 (S08), regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,50 → 12,63 (proefdocument)". It is made up: never enter it in the real Exact; once the test is over, tick it and press "Aangevinkte regels niet invoeren".

**One row per change.** The same change asked by several cases (two confirmations of one order, three captures of one cart) is one row, and an article price is one row whichever order or price list asked it. The list keeps one price per article, so two suppliers' prices of one article cannot both stand on it: the tab "Prijslijst" keeps a price off the list when the purchase prices of another supplier hold the article too, and says to enter that price in Exact yourself (section 4.3). When a newer document asks another value for the same line, an older change still open leaves the list and the newer one is the row. A change you chose not to enter is not asked again for the same value, as long as no other value was asked for that line in between, and a change is not asked while your ticks say that Exact holds its value already. When the changes of a case are undone (section 3.10), a change of it that you had not entered goes off the list, since Exact still holds the value from before; for one you had entered, a row asks you to put the old value back. A change that another case asked too stays on the list for that case.

**Ticking.** Enter the change in Exact first. Then tick its row and press "Aangevinkte regels zijn ingevoerd": the page says "1 wijziging genoteerd als ingevoerd in Exact." (or "2 wijzigingen genoteerd als ingevoerd in Exact.") and the row leaves the list. A change you do not want in Exact, tick and press "Aangevinkte regels niet invoeren": "1 wijziging vervalt: de assistent vraagt er niet meer om." The two buttons stand above the rows and again below them, so that they stay in reach of a long list. To tick a whole order at once, choose it under "Blok" (the article prices are the choice "Artikelprijzen") and press "Alles van dit blok is ingevoerd": it notes the rows of that order as the tab showed them when the list was last read, and a change that came in after that stays on the list. While a price list has article prices to enter (section 4.3), the tab shows "Prijslijst" under "Blok" with those price lists, the newest first, each named by its supplier and the time it was put on the list, "S01 (20-11-2026 09:30)": choose the list and press "Alle artikelprijzen van deze prijslijst zijn ingevoerd". It notes the article prices of that list as the tab showed them, and no other row. A row "Terugzetten:" that puts a price of that list back is no row of the list: the press leaves it on the list, for you to tick once the old price is back in Exact, and a list is not offered for such a row alone. An article price that a confirmation asked too is one row with the list's, and is noted with it. With nothing ticked or chosen the page says "Vink eerst aan wat u bedoelt." and notes nothing. When none of the rows you pressed for is still open (another browser tab handled them, or a newer document replaced the value), the page says "Er is niets genoteerd: de aangevinkte regels staan niet meer open (al afgehandeld, of vervangen door een nieuwere waarde). De lijst is bijgewerkt." and notes nothing either. The list is read again when you select the tab, after each button and with "Lijst vernieuwen". A tick or a drop given by mistake is taken back under the list, with "Weer op de lijst zetten" (below).

**Seen by the aid itself.** After you change a sent order, it must be released again in Exact (Verrichten), and on that page of Exact you choose whether the order is sent again. When you have it sent, Exact e-mails a new PDF of the purchase order. The runner files it as a new version of the order, and at each pass, after the cases ran, it compares every open row of an order line with the order as it stands: its newest PDF, or an export from Exact given after it. A row whose new value that look shows leaves the list by itself, marked "(gezien in versie 2 van de inkooporder)", or "(gezien in een export uit Exact)" for an export, and you tick nothing: a price when the line's net price (after its discount, per its own price unit), rounded to the decimals of the new value and at least two, equals it; a delivery date when the new PDF's date lies within the new value, so that a day inside the new week counts; a quantity when the two are equal as numbers. The line is found by its number when its article agrees with the article of that line in the version the case compared with, else by its article when exactly one line holds it. When two lines of the order hold one article (two deliveries of it), the article tells neither from the other, and neither does a line number, since a print may number its lines by their place: such a change belongs to the one line of that article that agrees with the line it was asked for in its quantity, its delivery date and its unit price, the changed value apart. In the very print its case was compared with, it is the line it was asked for. When no line, or more than one, agrees so, the change has no line in that print: nothing is seen or laid over there. Rows that name one line of the order, whether under the PDF's line numbers or an export's, are one row of the list: a tick on it covers both, and neither is asked twice. The changes of two deliveries of one article are rows of their own, and one tick never covers both. A PDF or an export counts only when the aid was given it after the change was asked, and for a row that puts a change back after that row. A print that shows nothing new counts as well: the same export given again, an export with the same lines, and the PDF the aid holds given again are each a look at the order at the time they were given. A file that stays in the watched folder was given once, however often the runner passes it. What counts is when the aid was given it, not when Exact made it: a print that reaches the aid late, because the aid was stopped, the sign-in of the mailbox had lapsed, or a PDF was saved and given to the aid later, counts as new and can tick a change or a put-back off, also when Exact printed it before you entered the change. So when the aid was stopped for a while, or the sign-in of the mailbox had lapsed, check in Exact, once it runs again or the sign-in works again, the put-backs it ticked off as seen. An article price is never ticked off by a purchase order. A later PDF or export never takes back a drop. It does end a tick, or a change the aid saw, that it contradicts (below, "A newer print ends a tick"). When such a look shows neither the old nor the new value, the row stays on the list with a note after it: "Inkooporder 250108 (S08), regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,50 → 12,70. Exact toont nu 12,80." When you do not have Exact send the order again, no new PDF comes and the row stays on the list: tick it yourself. The aid is right both ways (the next paragraph). In the words of the Dutch guide (section 7): "Laat u Exact de inkooporder na de wijziging opnieuw versturen, dan vinkt de assistent de wijziging zelf af. Doet u dat niet, vink de wijziging dan af op het tabblad In Exact invoeren: de assistent vergelijkt latere documenten dan met de waarde die u hebt ingevoerd."

**What the next check is compared with.** Once a change is ticked, or seen by the aid, later documents of that order are compared with the value you entered: a second confirmation with the entered price shows no price difference, one that returns to the old price is shown against the entered price, and the same cart shown again asks nothing. The value is laid over each version of the order that the aid was last given before the tick, on the line of that article (found as for a change the aid saw, above), and the review tab names it above the lists of such a case (section 2.2). A version or an export that the aid was given after the tick is taken as it is: the print that Exact made after your entry is the order after the change. A change that is still open on the list, or that you dropped, counts for nothing: until you tick it, a later document is compared with the order as it was filed and asks the same change again, as the same row. A put-back counts once you ticked it, or the aid saw it: the old value is the order's again. While the put-back is open on the list, the entered value stays. A change that only try-out confirmations asked ("(proefdocument)", section 3.17) counts for the next try-out alone, never for a supplier's own confirmation.
