A change that the supplier takes back leaves the list: when a newer document asked another value for the line, its row stands open, and the newest document names the entered value again, that open row goes off the list, as it did before entered values counted. If the supplier asks that other value once more, the row is back. While the list cannot be read (below, "What a tick is"), a case is compared with the order as it was filed, the review tab says so above its lists, and the case comes to you whole: the aid prepares, requests and asks nothing for it (sections 2.2 and 2.4).

**A newer print ends a tick.** A tick is believed until a newer print shows the order. A print of the order (a PDF version or an export) that the aid is given after the tick, and that shows for that line and field another value than the one ticked, ends the tick: the row stands on the list again as open and says so, "Inkooporder 250108 (S08), regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,50 → 12,70. U vinkte dit af, maar de inkooporder die daarna binnenkwam toont 12,50. Voer de wijziging in Exact in, of vink opnieuw af als zij er al in staat.", whatever value the print shows (for a row that was never ticked, a third value is noted with "Exact toont nu 12,80." as before). The value is laid over no version of the order any more, and the next document is compared with the order as it was printed, so that the difference is found and asked in the ordinary way. No case closes as confirmed on a tick that the newest print contradicts. This holds for a confirmation, which is compared with a PDF, as for a cart: an export given after the tick that shows another value ends the tick for the PDF versions too. It holds for a print that shows nothing new as well: after a tick given without the entry Exact is unchanged, so the next export has the lines of the export before; the aid files nothing for it and still takes it as a look at the order at the time it was given, and the tick is ended. A print that shows the ticked value changes nothing. A print in which the line of the tick cannot be found ends the tick too: its article stands on no line any more (the article of the line was changed in Exact), or on several lines of which none agrees with the line the change was asked for and none shows the ticked value. The row then says "U vinkte dit af, maar in de inkooporder die daarna binnenkwam is deze regel niet terug te vinden. Kijk in Exact of de wijziging er staat en vink dan opnieuw af, of haal de regel van de lijst." When one of several lines of the article shows the ticked value, the print does not contradict the tick and it stands. In doubt the aid asks again and never believes. If the print was an older one that reached the aid late (section "Seen by the aid itself" above), tick the row again once the value is in Exact: the later tick counts, also for that print. A change that only try-outs asked is left as it is. The aid judges this at each pass of the runner, and for the order of a document just before it compares that document.

**Handled changes.** Under the list, the section "Afgehandeld in de laatste 14 dagen" (Handled in the last 14 days) names every change entered, seen or dropped in that time, the newest first, with how the aid knows it: "(door u afgevinkt op 18-11-2026)", "(gezien in versie 2 van de inkooporder)", "(gezien in een export uit Exact)" or "(niet ingevoerd, op 18-11-2026)". Choose one under "Afgehandelde wijziging" and press one of the two buttons beside it.

- "Weer op de lijst zetten" takes back your own tick or drop: "De regel staat weer op de lijst." and the aid asks for the change again. A change the aid saw itself is not taken back: "Deze wijziging heeft de assistent zelf in Exact gezien. Kies Terugdraaien in Exact als de oude waarde terug moet." A change you put back with "Terugdraaien in Exact" does not return either, since you asked for the old value: "Deze wijziging is teruggedraaid; zij komt niet terug op de lijst." The page says "De wijziging komt niet terug op de lijst: er is intussen een andere wijziging voor dezelfde regel of hetzelfde artikel." only when another change for that line or article came in after it. Nothing is written then. With no change chosen under "Afgehandelde wijziging", either button says "Kies eerst een wijziging bij Afgehandelde wijziging." and writes nothing.
- "Terugdraaien in Exact" is for a change that is in Exact (ticked or seen) and must go back. The aid prepares the old value as a new row, which stands on the list with "Terugzetten:" before it, "Inkooporder 250108 (S08), Terugzetten: regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,70 → 12,50", and says "Nog niet teruggedraaid in Exact. Op de lijst staat nu: Inkooporder 250108 (S08), Terugzetten: regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,70 → 12,50. Voer de oude waarde in Exact in en vink de regel af." The page never says "Ongedaan gemaakt" for it. The change stays among the handled ones as it was, next to its put-back, also after you entered the put-back and ticked it (or the aid saw it in a new PDF or an export). A second press says "Het terugzetten staat al op de lijst."; when you had dropped the put-back, it is put on the list again; when the put-back is entered or seen, a press on the change says "Deze wijziging is al teruggedraaid." A put-back is a row of its own on the list: it never joins another row of the same value. When a newer change for the same line came in after the put-back, it replaced the put-back on the list, also one you had dropped, as a newer value replaces any older change; while that newer change is on the list, or is entered or seen and Exact still holds it, a press says "De wijziging komt niet terug op de lijst: er is intussen een andere wijziging voor dezelfde regel of hetzelfde artikel.", and once it no longer stands in the way (you dropped it, it only asked a value Exact holds already, or it was put back itself), a press makes a new put-back. A put-back never replaces a newer change: while a newer change for the same line or article, which another confirmation asked, is open, entered or seen, the page refuses, writes nothing and says how to go on, unless the marks say that Exact holds this change's old value already: then it says "Deze wijziging is al teruggedraaid." While the newer change is on the list: "Terugdraaien kan nu niet: er staat een nieuwere wijziging op de lijst, Inkooporder 250108 (S08), regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,70 → 12,90. Laat die eerst vallen; daarna kunt u deze wijziging terugdraaien." Once it is entered or seen: "Terugdraaien kan nu niet: er is een nieuwere wijziging ingevoerd, Inkooporder 250108 (S08), regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,70 → 12,90. Draai die eerst terug en vink het terugzetten af; daarna kunt u deze wijziging terugdraaien." When the newer change asks this change's old value itself, nothing needs to be put back: while it is on the list, "Terugdraaien is niet nodig: op de lijst staat al een wijziging naar de oude waarde, Inkooporder 250108 (S08), regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,70 → 12,50. Voer die in Exact in en vink die af."; once it is entered or seen, "Deze wijziging is al teruggedraaid." A newer change that the aid can never put back says so, and what to do: "Terugdraaien kan nu niet: er is een nieuwere wijziging ingevoerd, artikel KM-0801: inkoopprijs onbekend → 12,90, die de assistent niet kan terugdraaien. Zet de oude waarde zelf terug in Exact." The newer change stops blocking once you dropped it, or once its own put-back is entered or seen, or once the marks say that Exact no longer holds its value (a row entered after it set another value); a press then makes the put-back of this change, which stands on the list, or says "Deze wijziging is al teruggedraaid." when the marks say that Exact holds its old value already (another put-back or a newer change entered it). Before the page makes a put-back it works out that the put-back will stand on the list; should it ever say "Terugdraaien kan nu niet: het terugzetten zou niet op de lijst komen. Er is niets geschreven; geef dit door aan de onderzoeker.", nothing was written: pass it on to the researcher. A change that can never be put back says so first, before any newer change. An article price that a confirmation asked has no price before it that the aid knows: "Van dit artikel kent de assistent de oude prijs niet; zet die zelf terug in Exact." An article price of a price list has one, and is put back like an order line. A change you did not enter has nothing to put back ("Deze wijziging is niet ingevoerd; in Exact is er niets terug te draaien."), and a put-back is not put back in turn ("De assistent kan deze wijziging niet terugdraaien; zet de oude waarde zelf terug in Exact.").

A put-back names the old value of the row it puts back, which is the order's value as the aid read it when the change was asked. When the aid's own records give another value for what Exact held before that change (an earlier change of that line that was entered or seen, else the order as the aid last saw it), the note of the press adds one sentence: "Let op: 12,50 is de waarde van de inkooporder zoals de assistent die las; volgens de lijst (een eerdere wijziging die is ingevoerd) stond er 12,70. Kijk in Exact welke waarde terug moet.", or with "volgens de inkooporder zoals de assistent haar het laatst zag" when a print shows it. Look in Exact which value goes back; the row itself still names the order's value.

A put-back is the undo of that one change: it is recorded in the case's write log, its `steps/09_close.json` and its audit record, or for a price list in the list's own write log (section 3.10), and one line in `marks.jsonl` links the new row to the change it puts back. A put-back made again, once newer rows came after the first one and no longer ask anything, is a second undo of the same change, recorded the same way.

**Bevestigd door de leverancier.** At the bottom of the tab, "Bevestigd door de leverancier (laatste 7 dagen): voeg in Exact de bevestiging bij en zet de status op Bevestigd" lists the confirmations the aid prepared to attach, one line each and without a tick, for 7 days after the aid last prepared the attachment or the status for that confirmation, the newest first, with the day it last did so: "18-11-2026: inkooporder 250108 (S08), bestand MU08_conf.pdf". Attach that file to the order in Exact and set its status to Bevestigd. "(alleen bijvoegen: er staat nog iets open)" after a line means that a question or an item of that order is still open: attach the confirmation only; the note goes once the case is closed and the aid has prepared the status, and then you set the status too: the line shows again from the day of the close, also when the confirmation came in more than 7 days before. The confirmation of a try-out (section 3.17) carries "(proefdocument)" behind its line: it is made up, so attach nothing and set no status for it in the real Exact. With no confirmation in those 7 days the section is not shown.

**An order above the authorization limit.** Under the line at the top, the tab names each such order with "Let op: na deze wijziging moet de inkooporder opnieuw gefiatteerd worden.": after you enter the change, the order goes through approval in Exact again. Neither the limit nor the order's total is shown.

**What a tick is.** A tick is your word that the change is in Exact: the aid reads Exact back only through the new PDFs it is sent and the exports from Exact it is given. The aid believes a tick: it compares later documents of that order with the ticked value, until a PDF of the order or an export from Exact that the aid is given after the tick shows another value for the line, which ends the tick (above); an export counts whether its lines differ from the export before or not. Until such a print comes, a tick given without the entry hides the difference in the next confirmation: take such a tick back with "Weer op de lijst zetten" (above). It is one line in `cases/prepared/marks.jsonl` per prepared row of the change, with the time and "buyer", appended and never rewritten; a change the aid saw has such a line with "version 2" in place of "buyer", or "export" for one it saw in an export ("(gezien in een export uit Exact)"), and "Weer op de lijst zetten" adds a line "reopened". A tick that a newer print ended has such a line "reopened" with "version 2" or "export" in place of "buyer" and with the value that print showed for the line (none when the line could not be found in it), and a row that left the list because the supplier took its change back has a line "replaced" that names the case whose document said so. A line holds its time with the fraction of the second, so that a tick counts for a print that was filed earlier in the same second. It changes nothing of the case, its write log, its dispositions or `prepared_changes.csv`. "1 regel van de lijst kon niet worden gelezen; vernieuw de lijst over een minuut." means that a line of one of the two files could not be read, usually the last line while the runner is writing it: refresh the list a minute later, and tell the researcher if the line stays. When the list cannot be read at all (another program holds a file of it, for example `prepared_changes.csv` opened in a spreadsheet), the rows stay as they were, the label reads "In Exact invoeren (?)" and the tab says "Dit is niet gelukt (PermissionError)."; close that program and press "Lijst vernieuwen". The same holds when `prepared_changes.csv` can no longer be understood, because its first line lost the columns the aid wrote (the file was saved from a spreadsheet, with semicolons between the fields): the tab says "De lijst In Exact invoeren kan niet worden gelezen: het bestand is buiten de assistent gewijzigd. Geef dit door aan de onderzoeker.", and no tick of the list counts as not made. Tell the researcher: the file must be put back as the aid wrote it. Never save that file from a spreadsheet. On a page opened with `--share`, and on a page without its settings file (the Colab notebook), the tab says only that it is not available.

### 2.7 The tab Bestanden

When a document does not come in by itself (the mailbox is not connected yet, or a purchase order, a confirmation or an export came another way), give it to the aid on the tab "Bestanden" (Files) of the review page: you never need the file explorer for it. At the top stands "Een bestand aan de assistent geven" with the line "Komt een document niet vanzelf uit uw postvak, geef het dan hier: een inkooporder uit Exact (pdf) of een orderbevestiging (pdf of e-mail). De assistent verwerkt het bij de volgende ronde, binnen een minuut." While the columns of the export are set (section 3.17, part 2) the line names the export as well: "een export uit Exact (Excel of csv)".

**Giving a file.** Click the field "Kies een bestand" (or drop the file on it), choose the file and press "Aan de assistent geven", which can be pressed once a file is chosen; one press gives the file once, also when the button is clicked twice. The page puts the file into the watched folder, `inbox/`, under its own name (with _2 before its extension when `inbox/` holds that name already), less a few marks that the page does not take in a name ({ } ! $ * and the backtick, and the marks no Windows folder takes, such as : ? < > and |), and whole: the runner never reads half a file. The page says "Inkooporder 250108.pdf is aangenomen. Hieronder staat binnen een minuut wat ermee is gedaan.", naming the file as it lies in `inbox/`. The runner handles the file at its next pass exactly as a file that the mailbox brought: the same kinds of document, the same check for a document received before, the same refusals. The page itself writes nothing into the purchase orders or the cases. The aid reads a PDF, a PNG, an e-mail saved as `.eml` and, once the columns of the export are set, an export from Exact as `.xlsx` or `.csv`. Any other file is not accepted, and nothing is written: "Inkooporder 250108.docx is niet aangenomen: de assistent leest pdf, png en e-mail (.eml)." (with the columns of the export set, the sentence ends "de assistent leest pdf, png, e-mail (.eml) en een export uit Exact (Excel of csv)."). An empty file: "Inkooporder 250108.pdf is niet aangenomen: het bestand is leeg." A spreadsheet while the columns of the export are not set, which the runner would pass over: "Orderregels.xlsx is niet aangenomen: de assistent leest een export uit Exact pas als de kolommen zijn ingesteld onder Instellingen, Koppeling met Exact." "Kies eerst een bestand." means that no file was chosen. A message dragged out of the classic Outlook program is a `.msg` file, which the aid does not read: save its attachment, or the message as `.eml` where Outlook offers that, and give that file.

**What became of it.** Under "Laatst verwerkt" (Handled last) the table shows the last 20 lines of what the runner did, the newest first: a line for each document, each attachment of an e-mail and each case checked again, whichever way the file came: given on this tab, brought by the mailbox, or saved into `inbox/` at the installation. Its columns are "Tijd" (when the runner handled the file), "Bestand" (the file, and an attachment as `<e-mail>/<attachment>`; a case checked again names its document alone, without its e-mail) and "Wat de assistent deed". "Lijst vernieuwen" reads the table again, and so does selecting the tab. What a line says:

| Wat de assistent deed | What it means |
|---|---|
| "Inkooporder 250108 opgenomen (versie 1)" | A PO PDF, filed as that version of its order. |
| "Export uit Exact opgenomen: 2 inkooporders, waarvan 1 nieuw of gewijzigd" | An export, filed: the orders it holds, and how many of them had new or changed lines (section 3.13). With one order it says "Export uit Exact opgenomen: 1 inkooporder, waarvan 1 nieuw of gewijzigd". |
| "Export uit Exact zonder orderregels: niets gedaan" | An export with the column titles and no order line, such as an overview exported with a filter that matches nothing: nothing is filed (section 3.13). |
| "Zaak geopend voor inkooporder 250101" | A confirmation became a case of that order: on the tab "Beoordelen" when it waits for you, and in the digest. |
| "Zaak geopend, inkooporder onbekend" | A confirmation became a case without an order: decide on it on the tab "Beoordelen". |
| "Zaak geopend voor inkooporder 250101, afgeleid: kijk de zaak na" | The aid found the order otherwise than by its number (the reason of a guessed order, section 2.4): check that it is the right order. |
| "Antwoord van een leverancier bij de zaak gelegd" | A supplier's own answer to a question of the aid, filed with its case, which comes back to you (section 2.3). |
| "Een bericht van de assistent kwam terug als onbestelbaar: bij de zaak gelegd" | A question of the aid bounced: it is filed with its case, which comes back to you with "Bericht kwam terug" (section 2.3). |
| "Automatisch antwoord (bijvoorbeeld een afwezigheidsmelding) bij de zaak gelegd" | An automatic reply to a question of the aid, such as an out-of-office notice, or a mail server's notice that the question is delayed or was delivered: it is filed with its case, which goes on waiting for the supplier's answer (section 2.3). |
| "Uw eigen bericht bij de zaak gelegd; dit telt niet als antwoord van de leverancier" | An e-mail from your own address, from the aid's address or from the work mailbox that names a question of the aid, as your own reply to all does: it is filed with its case, which goes on waiting for the supplier's answer (section 2.3). |
| "Inkooporder 250101 is binnen: de wachtende zaak is opnieuw gecontroleerd" | A confirmation that waited for its order was checked again as a new case once the order came in (section 2.2). |
| "Al eerder ontvangen: niets gedaan" | The aid had this document already: a confirmation that is a case already, a PO PDF or an export it filed before. |
| "Geen inkooporder of orderbevestiging: niets gedaan" | Another kind of document: an invoice, a packing slip, a quotation or anything else. |
| "Dit soort bestand leest de assistent niet" | A file or an attachment of another type, such as a Word file inside an e-mail. |
| "Een rekenblad zonder de kolomtitels van de export: niets gedaan" | A spreadsheet in which no row holds the column titles set for the export (section 3.13). |
| "Proefdocument buiten de proefstand: niets gedaan" | A try-out confirmation while the test mode is off or a limit is set (section 3.17). |
| "Meer dan een inkoopordernummer gevonden: bekijk dit document zelf" | The aid could not tell which order the document answers: handle it yourself. |
| "Scan zonder leesbare tekst: bekijk dit document zelf" | A scan or a picture that the aid cannot read: handle it yourself. |
| "Scan zonder leesbare tekst: de assistent kon dit document niet lezen; bekijk het zelf" | A scan that became a case, which then failed at reading because Copilot reads text only (section 3.15). The line comes after the line "Zaak geopend ..." of the same document, and the digest names the case under "Mislukt" with the same words: handle the document yourself. |
| "E-mail zonder bijlage: niets gedaan" | An e-mail without a document. |
| "Kon niet worden gelezen. Is het een inkooporder uit Exact, controleer dan de opmaak onder Instellingen, Koppeling met Exact." | The file could not be read: a file that cannot be opened or read (a damaged PDF, or a file another program holds), a purchase order that the layout does not fit, or a document the aid could not ask the AI service about (section 3.15). It stays in `inbox/` and the aid tries it again at every pass; once it can be read (a purchase order once the layout of section 3.17 fits it), a line says what it became. |
| "Inkooporder niet opgenomen: de assistent heeft deze versie al uit een ander bestand" | A PO PDF of a version that the aid holds from another file. Either the same version of the order was printed again, which needs nothing, or the order was changed and Exact printed it under the same version number: then the owner of the installation settles it with the layout's version label (section 3.17). The file stays in `inbox/` as any error does. |
| "Export uit Exact niet opgenomen: een rij heeft een artikelcode maar geen inkoopordernummer" | An export that the aid refused for a row with an item code and no order number. It stays in `inbox/` and is tried again at every pass (section 3.13). Refused for its columns, the line says "Export uit Exact niet opgenomen: de kolommen van dit bestand zijn anders dan ingesteld", and for any other reason "Export uit Exact niet opgenomen: controleer de kolommen onder Instellingen, Koppeling met Exact." Three refusals for which the columns are right have a line of their own that says what to do (section 6): for a CSV file in which a row is cut otherwise than the row of column titles, "Export uit Exact niet opgenomen: een rij heeft een ander aantal cellen dan de rij met kolomtitels. Sla het bestand op als Excel-bestand (.xlsx) en geef het opnieuw."; for an Excel file with more than one visible sheet with data, "Export uit Exact niet opgenomen: het Excel-bestand heeft meer dan één werkblad met gegevens. Sla het werkblad met de orderregels op als een eigen bestand en geef dat."; and for an Excel file whose only data stands on a hidden sheet, "Export uit Exact niet opgenomen: het Excel-bestand heeft alleen gegevens op een verborgen werkblad. Maak het werkblad zichtbaar en geef het bestand opnieuw." "Testen" in part 2 of the block "Koppeling met Exact" names the row or the sheets (section 3.17). |
| "Na 30 dagen nog niet gelezen: de assistent probeert dit bestand niet meer" | A file that stayed in `inbox/` as an error for 30 days: the aid moved it to `inbox/processed/` and does not try it again. Put right what kept it from being read, then give it again. |
| "Overgeslagen: niets gedaan" | A file skipped for a reason this table has no line for; the digest names the reason under "Overgeslagen". |

A case made from a try-out confirmation has "(proefdocument)" behind its line. A file that stays in `inbox/` because it could not be read has one line, however often the aid tries it, as long as its name and its content stay the same, also while another program holds it so that it cannot even be opened; a file the aid handled and could not move to `inbox/processed/` keeps the line of what it became; a file given again after it was handled gets a line each time, "Al eerder ontvangen: niets gedaan" when the aid had it already. A file that stays in `inbox/` as an error is not tried for ever: 30 days after its first line, at the next pass that still cannot read it, the aid moves it to `inbox/processed/` with the line "Na 30 dagen nog niet gelezen: de assistent probeert dit bestand niet meer". A file given again under the same name with another content starts its 30 days anew, and a file that cannot be moved then is tried again at the next pass. A file set aside is named in `cases/runner.log` too, with its days, and once in the next digest under "Overgeslagen", as "set aside after 30 days without being read". The lines name files, orders and cases only, never what a document or an error says: the error itself is in the digest under "Overgeslagen" and in `cases/runner.log` (section 3.6). They are kept in `cases/intake.jsonl` (section 3.1). When that file cannot be read, the table stays as it was and the tab says "Dit is niet gelukt (PermissionError)."; refresh the list a minute later. On a page opened with `--share`, and on a page without its settings file (the Colab notebook), the tab says only that it is not available.

### 2.8 Your settings on the page

Your own rules are on the tab "Instellingen" (Settings) of the review page. You never need to open a settings file for them: everything else was set up at the installation. The tab shows what the settings file holds when the page loads and each time you select the tab, also a change someone made in the file itself; what you typed and did not save is then replaced by the file's values. The page is in Dutch, with the switch "Taal" for English. A number takes a decimal comma or a point, and a percentage or an amount at most two decimals: write a thousand as 1000, because "1.000" is refused with "hoogstens twee decimalen (schrijf duizend als 1000, zonder punt of komma)".

From top to bottom:

- **"Wat de assistent zelf mag afhandelen"** (What the aid may settle by itself).
  - "Prijsverschil: hoogstens dit percentage" and "Prijsverschil: hoogstens dit bedrag per regel (euro)" are the threshold for the price ("de drempel"). When a price difference is at most that percentage of the PO's price, and at most that amount on the line (the difference per piece times the quantity), the aid takes the supplier's price over on the PO line and changes the article's price too (a line without an item code has no article to change). A difference above the threshold comes to you.
  - "Leverdatum: dagen bovenop de levertijd" is the margin for the delivery date ("de marge"). A later delivery date is taken over up to and including the supplier's lead time plus these days, counted from the delivery date of the purchase order's line, not from the day the order was placed. An example: the line asks for delivery on 16 November, the supplier's lead time is 10 days and the margin is 7 days. A confirmed date up to and including 3 December (16 November plus 17 days) is taken over, and 4 December comes to you. A date given as a week counts as its Friday. Every later date of a supplier without a known lead time comes to you. An earlier delivery date is taken over, also without a known lead time.
  - "Eerste zaken van een leverancier": until this many cases of a supplier are closed, every case of that supplier comes to you, whatever it holds, with the reason "Nieuwe leverancier" on the review tab; 0 turns this off.
- **"Vragen aan leveranciers"** (Questions to suppliers). "Wachttijd op een antwoord (werkdagen)": the working days the aid waits for a supplier's answer before the case comes to you. "Uw naam onder de berichten": your name under "Inkoop" in the questions and in the request to the approver, or under "Purchasing" when the aid's language is English; empty leaves "Inkoop" or "Purchasing" alone.
- **"Dagoverzicht en taal"** (Digest and language). "Dagoverzicht": "Elke dag op een vaste tijd", with the time under "Tijd van het dagoverzicht (UU:MM)" (for example 16:30), or "Na elke zaak". "Uw e-mailadres": where the digest goes; you are also in copy of every question and of the request to the approver. "Taal van de assistent", "Nederlands" or "English": the language of the digest, of the messages to suppliers and to the approver, and of the banner in the webshop (the switch "Taal" at the top changes the page only).
- **"Fiattering"** (Approval). "E-mailadres van de fiatteur": the person who is asked to approve a PO again after the aid changed it above the authorization limit. The limit itself is set at the installation and is not shown.

Press "Opslaan" under these groups. The save writes only the fields you changed since the form was filled. A field you did not touch is never written, so a value that was changed in the file meanwhile stays as the file has it, and every other line of the file stays as it was. "Opgeslagen. De assistent gebruikt dit vanaf de volgende ronde, binnen een minuut." means it is saved, and the fields then show the values as the file holds them (a time typed as 9:05 shows as 09:05). With nothing changed the page says "Er is niets gewijzigd; er is niets opgeslagen.", leaves the file alone and shows the file's values as they are at that moment. When a value is not right, nothing is saved and the page names each field with what it takes, for example "Niet opgeslagen: Prijsverschil: hoogstens dit percentage: een getal van 0 tot 100." If the page was started again while your browser tab stayed open, the first save is not made: the page fills the form again with the settings as they are and asks you to enter your change once more.

**What a save changes, and what it does not.** The runner uses the new values from its next pass, the pre-check in the browser from its next check, and the review page itself at once. What was checked before a save keeps its outcome: a case that waits for you keeps its reason, and a cart that was checked before is answered from its case, as section 2.5 describes (the same page on any day, the same cart on the same day). So on the same day a cart is checked anew, with the new values, only when the cart itself changes (another quantity, a line more or less).

**"Leveranciers"** (Suppliers). The table lists every supplier you set something for: the e-mail address for the aid's questions, the lead time in days, "Altijd naar u" (every case of the supplier comes to you) and "Geen vragen uit zichzelf" (the aid sends this supplier no questions by itself). The questions the aid would have asked such a supplier come to you. A question you choose yourself on the review tab, "Leverancier vragen", is still sent to the supplier (section 2.2). Without an address the aid asks the supplier nothing either, and the case comes to you. To change a supplier, choose it under "Leverancier": the form below fills with its values and with the two ticks, "Elke zaak van deze leverancier komt naar u" and "De assistent stuurt deze leverancier uit zichzelf geen vragen". To add one, choose "Leverancier toevoegen" and type its name exactly as the purchase order gives it. Then press "Leverancier opslaan"; of a listed supplier only what you changed is written. "Leverancier verwijderen" takes the chosen supplier out of every list. A listed supplier left without an address, a lead time and both ticks is no longer listed. Two saves are refused. A name typed under "Leverancier toevoegen" that is listed already is not saved, so that a listed supplier never loses its values: "Niet opgeslagen: er staat al een leverancier met deze naam in de lijst. Kies die leverancier bij "Leverancier" om hem te wijzigen." A new supplier with a name and nothing else is not saved either: "Niet opgeslagen: een leverancier heeft een e-mailadres, een levertijd of een vinkje nodig." The note of a supplier save stays on the page until you choose another supplier yourself.

**When the settings file cannot be read.** If the file is missing, held by another program, damaged, or holds a value the aid refuses, one line at the top of the tab says so: "Het instellingenbestand kan niet worden gelezen. Hieronder staan de instellingen die de assistent nu gebruikt. Opslaan kan pas weer als het bestand is hersteld." When a value is refused, the line names its field, by its label on the tab, or by the setting's name for a setting the installation sets, never a value. The form then shows the settings the aid works with at that moment, and every save on the tab is refused with the same line until the file is put right. Tell the researcher.

**"Koppeling met Exact (bij de installatie)"**, folded away below the suppliers, holds the connection to Exact: the layout of the purchase orders that Exact e-mails and the columns of the export of order lines, each set from one sample at the installation, and the words and the digits by which the aid finds the order number on a confirmation (section 3.17). Its lines under "Stand van de koppeling" say whether the layout is set, which purchase order the aid filed last, whether the columns of the export are set, when the last export was filed, how many files in the watched folder it could not read and how many cases wait for their purchase order. You do not need it in daily work.

**"Koppelingen: het Microsoft-postvak en de AI-dienst"**, folded away at the bottom of the tab, holds the connection of the mailbox (section 3.14) and of Copilot (section 3.15); a click on its heading opens it. It is set up at the installation. You need it when the digest says "Postvak niet verbonden" or "Copilot werkt niet": the buttons to sign in again are in this part (section 5). At its end stands "Code voor de uitbreiding in de browser": the code to paste into the options of the extension of section 2.5, with a button to copy it; it cannot be changed on the page.

## 3. Running it

### 3.1 The host and the folder layout

The aid reaches the work mailbox through the Microsoft sign-in on the settings tab of the review page (section 3.14), and Exact through the buyer, who enters what the page's tab "In Exact invoeren" lists (section 2.6): no program of the aid reaches Exact, so it needs no Exact token (section 3.10). The first run on documents of the test account needs the company's written permission. What follows is what the code needs, wherever it runs.

The aid runs in one of two ways. From a copy of the repository, with Python 3.11 or newer and its packages installed, as below. Or from a folder that needs no installation at all, built once on a Windows PC and carried to the Windows PC where it runs, where it is started by a double click and at each logon (section 3.16): the way for a PC where nothing can be installed. For the repository, from the folder that holds `aid/`:

```bash
python3 -m pip install -r aid/requirements.txt -r aid/requirements-dev.txt
python3 -m pytest aid/tests -q
```

The suite runs offline and calls no AI service.

The folder layout, with the folder settings of the example (`cases_root` "cases", `po_store_root` "po_store", `watched_folder` "inbox"); relative paths in the settings resolve against the folder that holds the settings file. In the folder of section 3.16, `<root>` is its folder `data`:

```
<root>/
  settings.json          the settings with the real values (kept only here)
  STOP                   create it to stop the runner
  start_state.json       the launcher's state while it runs (section 3.4.1)
  logs/                  the launcher's start.log and the output of each part (section 3.4.1)
  inbox/                 the watched folder, filled by the mailbox rule or the work mailbox, and by the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7)
    processed/           the files the runner has handled, and those it set aside after 30 days (section 2.7)
  po_store/<PO>/v<n>/    po.pdf and po.json, one folder per version of the PO from a PO PDF
  po_store/<PO>/e<n>/    po.json, one folder per look at the PO from an export of the order lines (section 6)
                         in either folder, given.txt (empty) once a print that shows the PO so was given again: its
                         time is when (section 6)
  cases/
    <case>/              one folder per case, named <PO number>_<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS> or unknown_<...>
    captures/            the cart pages the extension sent; nocart_<...> a page without a cart table
    tryout/              the try-out carts made on the settings tab in the test mode, <PO number>_<change>.html (section 3.17)
    prepared/            prepared_changes.csv, the changes to enter in Exact, and marks.jsonl, their ticks (section 2.6)
    exact/               article_prices.json, the purchase prices of the articles from the exports of Exact that the buyer
                         gave on the tab "Prijslijst", per supplier, and pricelist_columns.json, the columns of each
                         supplier's price list as they were last pointed out on that tab (section 4.3)
    outbox/              messages written as .eml files instead of sent
    digests/             <day>.html of each digest
    pricelists/<day>/    the write log of the price lists put on the list that day, from the tab "Prijslijst" or by the
                         command when --settings gives the folder (section 4.3)
    tmp/                 the review page's temporary files: a file uploaded to the page and the page images of a
                         document it shows, removed between one and two hours after they were made and at the
                         page's next start (section 3.7)
      work/              a copy the runner keeps for a moment: the attachments of an e-mail while it handles the
                         inbox, the PDF that Copilot reads; each removed when it is done, and the folder emptied
                         at each start of the runner
    runner.log           the runner's errors
    capture.log          the capture server's errors and refused requests
    digest_state.json    what the next digest reports, and the lines written of a file that stays in inbox/ (section 3.6)
    intake.jsonl         what the tab "Bestanden" shows: a line for each document the runner handled (section 2.7)
    mailbox_state.json   the connection to the work mailbox, when one is made (section 3.14)
    review_items.jsonl   the files the scan skipped that the buyer must look at
```

Never copy `po_store/` to another computer, nor within one. Which look at a PO is the newest, a PDF version or a look from an export, rests on the times of its files. A copy that does not keep those times gives each file the time it was copied, in the order of the copy, and that can make an older look or an older PDF the PO as it stands, so that a cart is checked against an older order. The same times say whether a value ticked on the tab "In Exact invoeren" was entered after a version was last given to the aid (section 2.6): with the time of a copy, a version counts as given after every earlier tick, is compared as it was printed, and ends each of those ticks that it does not show. The hand-over starts with an empty store.

A case folder holds:

```
case.json          the case id, kind, PO number, time of opening, the input files with their sha256
input/             the copy of the supplier's document
steps/             01_po_versions.json, 02_read_document.json, 03_version.json, 04_match.json,
                   05_compare.json, 06_route.json, 07_execute.json, 08_result.json,
                   03_entered.json only when values entered in Exact took the place of the selected version's own
                   (their line, field, old and entered value, the time of the tick and who made it), or the list of
                   them could not be read (section 2.6); a file that an earlier attempt of the case left is removed
                   when the run writes none,
                   and 09_close.json after a close or an undo on the review page, or once the runner or the capture
                   server replaced the case
messages/          sent.jsonl, replies/, bounces/, auto/, escalated.jsonl
write_log.jsonl    one line per change made, prepared or failed, and per undo
dispositions.csv   the decisions in the evaluation's form
decisions.jsonl    the buyer's choices on the review page
audit.json         the audit record
capture.json       a cart case of the capture server: the page's sha256, the digest of its cart, the digest of its lines alone
                   (the cart without the number its page states), the shop's host, the saved page; po_found_by when the order
                   was found or guessed by the cart's items, and replaced_by once a later case of the same page, cart or
                   cart lines replaced the case (section 3.13)
```

A case that the runner replaced when it checked a confirmation again (section 3.17) keeps all its files: its result is closed, its `steps/09_close.json` holds the record "replaced" with the id of the case that replaced it, and its `dispositions.csv` stays. The confirmation is then in two case folders, so whoever counts cases over the cases folder, for the evaluation or otherwise, passes over a case whose `steps/09_close.json` holds the record "replaced". No command of the aid counts over the folder that way; the count of a supplier's closed cases for "Eerste zaken van een leverancier" passes over such a case, which has no supplier.

### 3.2 The settings file

Copy `aid/settings.example.json` to `<root>/settings.json` and fill it in. A key the aid does not know stops it, so a typing error is never silently ignored. An error names the field, never its value. The settings file with the real values never goes into a repository. The mailbox password is not a setting: it is the environment variable `AID_MAIL_PASSWORD`, and never goes into any file. There is no Exact token: the aid writes nothing in Exact, and no command reads `AID_EXACT_TOKEN` (section 3.10). The tokens of the Microsoft sign-in are no settings either: they are kept in a protected store on the PC (section 3.14). The file holds company data (the authorization limit, the suppliers' addresses): keep `<root>` in a folder that only the runner's user can read. On Linux and macOS a save on the settings tab keeps the file's permissions; on Windows the saved file takes the access its folder gives, whatever access the file had before.

| Setting | Default | Meaning | Company-specific |
|---|---|---|---|
| `language` | `"nl"` | The language of the digest and the questions: `"nl"` or `"en"`. | |
| `price_pct` | `"2.0"` | R04: largest price difference the aid accepts, in percent of the PO price. | the company sets it |
| `price_abs` | `"25.00"` | R04: largest price difference the aid accepts on a line, in euro (difference per piece times quantity). | the company sets it |
| `lead_time_tolerance_days` | `7` | R02: calendar days a later date may lie beyond the supplier's lead time, both counted from the delivery date of the PO line. | the company sets it |
| `ask_wait_working_days` | `3` | Working days the aid waits for a supplier's answer. | the company sets it |
| `first_cases_review` | `5` | R11: the number of closed cases of a supplier before the aid acts on its own for that supplier. | the company sets it |
| `precheck_suppliers` | `[]` | Reserved; no part of the aid reads it yet. | |
| `review_suppliers` | `[]` | R11: suppliers whose every case goes to the buyer. | yes |
| `messages_off_suppliers` | `[]` | R06 to R08: suppliers the aid asks nothing by itself; the questions of these rules go to the buyer. A question the buyer chooses on the review page is still sent to the supplier. | yes |
| `digest` | `"daily"` | `"daily"` or `"per_case"`. | |
| `digest_time` | `"16:30"` | `"HH:MM"`, the host's local time from which the daily digest is made. | |
| `authorization_limit` | `null` | R10: the authorization limit; a PO total above it needs the approver again. `null` switches R10 off. | yes, never in a repository |
| `buyer_email` | (required) | The buyer's address: the digest, and the copy of every question. | yes |
| `buyer_name` | `""` | The name printed under the signature of the questions and of the re-approval request ("Inkoop" in Dutch, "Purchasing" in English); empty: the signature alone. | yes |
| `aid_email` | (required) | The aid's own mailbox: the sender of every message sent through SMTP or written as a file. A message sent through the work mailbox (section 3.14) comes from that mailbox instead. | yes |
| `approver_email` | (required) | The approver who gets the request to approve a PO again. | yes |
| `supplier_emails` | `{}` | Supplier (as the PO names it) → the address for the aid's questions. | yes |
| `po_number_pattern` | `(?i:PO\|Inkooporder\|Bestelling\|Bestellung\|Order)[\s:#-]*(\d{6,8})` | The regular expression that finds a PO number in an e-mail or on the first page of a confirmation; its first group is the number, and a number that more digits follow is never taken. Of several numbers it finds, the one the PO store holds with one of its PO's item codes on the page counts; without one, a number the store holds that stands on the page with one of its PO's item codes (section 3.17). The settings tab writes it from the words before the number and the number of digits (section 3.17). | the company sets it |
| `cases_root` | (required) | The folder of the cases, for example `"cases"`. | |
| `po_store_root` | (required) | The folder of the PO versions, for example `"po_store"`. | |
| `watched_folder` | (required) | The inbox, for example `"inbox"`. | |
| `supplier_lead_times` | `{}` | Supplier → lead time in days, from the ERP. A supplier without one gets every later date as a review item. | yes |
| `reader` | `"llm"` | How the documents are read: `"llm"`, by Microsoft 365 Copilot with the work sign-in (section 3.15), which needs `ms_tenant_id` and `ms_client_id` (the runner refuses `"llm"` without them); or `"parser"`, by the template parser, without AI (section 3.12). The settings tab sets it ("Documenten lezen met"). | |
| `model` | the id of the development model | The model of the development AI client on the made-up set. The runner does not use it, as Copilot names no model; the freeze record shows it. | |
| `effort` | `"medium"` | `"low"`, `"medium"` or `"high"`, for the development AI client on the made-up set only. | |
| `exact_base_url` | `null` | Must stay `null` or empty. No command builds a write route into Exact: the aid only prepares its changes, for the tab "In Exact invoeren", and a settings file in which this key is set is refused at the start of every part (section 3.10). | yes |
| `exact_endpoints` | `{}` | Not used by any command (section 3.10). The path of each endpoint of a write route: `po_line`, `article`, `attachment`, `status`. | yes |
| `exact_fields` | `{}` | Not used by any command (section 3.10). The field name per kind of change of a write route, where it differs from the writer's default. | yes |
| `smtp_host` | `null` | The mail server of the aid's mailbox. `null`: messages are written as files. | yes |
| `smtp_port` | `587` | Its port (STARTTLS). | yes |
| `smtp_user` | `null` | The login of the aid's mailbox. | yes |
| `ms_tenant_id` | `null` | The Directory (tenant) ID of the app registration in Microsoft Entra ID (a GUID, or the tenant's domain name), for the Microsoft sign-in (section 3.14). An identifier, no secret. | yes |
| `ms_client_id` | `null` | The Application (client) ID of the app registration (a GUID). An identifier, no secret. | yes |
| `mailbox` | `null` | The address of the mailbox the aid reads and sends from. `null`: the signed-in account's own mailbox. Another mailbox needs the shared permissions and access in Exchange (section 3.14). | yes |
| `mail_folder` | `null` | The id of the folder of that mailbox that the runner reads at every pass, as Microsoft gives it; the settings tab fills it in. `null`: no mailbox is read, and the inbox is filled by a mailbox rule. | yes |
| `mail_folder_name` | `null` | The folder's display name, shown on the settings tab only. | yes |
| `send_via` | `"auto"` | How the aid's messages go out, the runner's and those of a close on the review page (section 3.7): `"microsoft"` (through the mailbox of the sign-in), `"smtp"` (through the SMTP settings above), `"file"` (written as files, sent by no one) or `"auto"`: the mailbox when `mail_folder` is set and an account is signed in at the time of the message, else SMTP when it is set up, else files. While the sign-in has lapsed, `"auto"` and `"microsoft"` send no question at all: it becomes "message not sent" on the review page, and the digest is written into `cases/outbox/` (section 3.14). | |
| `export_columns` | `{}` | For the pre-check: the aid's field name → the column title of the export of PO lines (section 6, "The export of PO lines"). It must name `po_number` and `item_code`, and may name `line_id`, `description`, `quantity`, `unit`, `unit_price`, `price_basis`, `discount_pct`, `net_price`, `delivery_date` and `supplier`. Map `supplier` only to a column that holds the supplier exactly as the supplier lists of these settings name it (the name on the purchase order), and otherwise leave it out: `review_suppliers`, `messages_off_suppliers`, `supplier_emails` and `supplier_lead_times` are keyed by that name. Empty: each column is named after its field. The settings tab sets it, with the two below, from one sample export with a trial read (section 3.17). | yes |
| `export_decimal_comma` | `false` | How the export writes a number as text: `true` for 1.234,50 (decimal comma), `false` for 1,234.50 (decimal point). A number cell of an Excel file is read as the number it holds, whatever this says. The settings tab sets it (section 3.17). | the company sets it |
| `export_fill_down` | `false` | `true` when the export prints the PO number once for each PO, on its first line or on a row of its own above its lines: a line without a PO number then belongs to the PO whose number stands nearest above it. With `false` such a line refuses the whole file. The settings tab sets it (section 3.17). | the company sets it |
| `article_columns` | `{}` | For the price list: the aid's field name → the column title of the export of a supplier's articles from Exact with their purchase prices (section 4.3). It names `item_code` and `price`, and `supplier` only for an export that names the supplier of each row. Empty: the tab "Prijslijst" offers nothing yet. Part 3 of the block "Koppeling met Exact" sets it (section 3.17). While it is set, the review page's `--share` is refused (section 3.7). | the company sets it |
| `article_decimal_comma` | `false` | How that export writes a number as text: `true` for 1.234,50 (decimal comma), `false` for 1,234.50 (decimal point). A number cell of an Excel file is read as the number it holds, whatever this says. Part 3 sets it (section 3.17). | the company sets it |
| `po_layout` | `null` | The layout of the PO PDF that Exact e-mails, in words that stand on its page (section 3.12): the text before the PO number and before the other header values, and the words of the row of column titles with what each column holds. With it every PO PDF is read with this layout, and a PDF that fits it is filed as a PO. `null`: only the made-up layout is read. Fill `po_senders` as well whenever it is set. The settings tab sets both from one sample, with a trial read (section 3.17). | yes, never in a repository |
| `po_senders` | `[]` | The senders of the e-mails with Exact's PO PDFs: one address or one domain (the part after the at sign) to each entry, for example `"inkoop@example.org"` or `"example.org"`. An entry that is neither, such as a name with the address in angle brackets, two addresses in one entry or a trailing comma, is refused by its place (`po_senders.1`). Filled: a PDF in the PO layout that came in an e-mail from another sender is handled as any confirmation is (section 3.12). Empty: any sender. Fill it whenever `po_layout` is set. | yes |
| `review_url` | `"http://localhost:7860"` | The address of the review page, used in the digest's links and in the banner's link. | |
| `capture_token` | `null` | The token the browser extension sends to the capture server (section 3.13); the extension's options hold the same value. Empty or `null`: the server does not start. | yes, never in a repository |
| `test_mode` | `false` | The test mode: the aid works with the test account of Exact. While it is `true`, whatever `authorization_limit` holds, the tab "In Exact invoeren" says to enter its changes in the test Exact only, and only the switch ends it: setting the limit does not. While it is `true` and `authorization_limit` is `null`, the settings tab offers a try-out confirmation made from a purchase order the aid knows, and the watched folder takes such a try-out (section 3.17). The first start of the folder of section 3.16 writes `true`, for the owner's test; the hand-over switches it off on the settings tab, as a step of its own. The switch at the end of the block "Koppeling met Exact (bij de installatie)" writes it. | |

`buyer_email`, `aid_email`, `approver_email`, `cases_root`, `po_store_root` and `watched_folder` have no default and must be given. Numbers that are amounts are written as text: `"2.0"`, `"25.00"`. The six settings of the work mailbox are filled in and saved on the settings tab of the review page (section 3.14); with them empty or `null` the aid works as before. `po_layout` is an object with keys of its own (section 3.12); a layout at fault is named by its key, for example `po_layout.title_row`, and never by a word it holds. Whenever `po_layout` is set, fill `po_senders` too: without it a supplier's document that repeats the PO's page and has no confirmation word in its header is filed as a version of the PO (section 3.12).

**What the settings tab writes, and what only the installation sets.** The buyer changes these on the settings tab of the review page (section 2.8), never in the file: the eleven settings `price_pct`, `price_abs`, `lead_time_tolerance_days`, `first_cases_review`, `ask_wait_working_days`, `buyer_name`, `digest`, `digest_time`, `buyer_email`, `language` and `approver_email`, and the four supplier settings `supplier_emails`, `supplier_lead_times`, `review_suppliers` and `messages_off_suppliers`. The tab's connections also write the six settings of the work mailbox and `reader`, and its block "Koppeling met Exact (bij de installatie)" writes `po_layout` and `po_senders` (its part 1) and `export_columns`, `export_decimal_comma` and `export_fill_down` (its part 2) and `article_columns` and `article_decimal_comma` (its part 3), each after a trial read of a sample, `po_number_pattern`, from the words and the digits of the number recognition at the end of its part 1, and with its switch "Proefstand: de assistent werkt met het test-Exact" `test_mode` and nothing else (section 3.17). The tab "Prijslijst" writes no setting: the columns of a supplier's price list, which it keeps when a list is put on the list "In Exact invoeren", are data and stand in `cases/exact/pricelist_columns.json` (section 4.3), so that no press of that tab changes the settings file. A save of the buyer's part writes only what the buyer changed: the fields whose text differs from the text the form was filled with, and of a supplier the values that were changed. In the file a save replaces the text of the values that changed, where they stand, and adds a key the file lacks at its end; every other key keeps its bytes, and so do the order of the keys, the indentation and the line ends. A save that changes nothing leaves the file and its modification time alone, so that "Opslaan" on an untouched form never changes the hash of the file, which the freeze record keeps (section 4.8). Each save is written through a temporary file with a name of its own and a rename, and one save is made at a time, each on the file as the one before left it, so that two saves pressed at the same moment both land. The buyer's part of the tab is filled from the file when the page loads and each time the tab is selected. When the file cannot be read (it is missing, held by another program, no JSON, or holds a value the aid refuses), the tab shows one line that says so, naming the fields (a field of the tab by its label, any other setting by its name) and never a value, shows the settings in use, and refuses every save until the file is put right. For a file in which `exact_base_url` is set the line is the sentence of section 3.10 in Dutch. Only the installation sets the rest: `authorization_limit`, the folders (`cases_root`, `po_store_root`, `watched_folder`), the SMTP server (`smtp_host`, `smtp_port`, `smtp_user`), `aid_email`, the token (`capture_token`), `review_url`, `model`, `effort` and the reserved `precheck_suppliers`. Nobody sets the three keys of a write route (`exact_base_url`, `exact_endpoints`, `exact_fields`): `exact_base_url` stays empty (section 3.10). Set these in the file before the buyer starts; the page never shows them, except the token, which the settings tab shows read-only at the end of its connections, as "Code voor de uitbreiding in de browser", for the extension's options (section 3.13). The runner reads the settings file again at the start of a pass when the file has changed; a file that is not valid then is logged in `runner.log` (stage `settings`, the fields only) and the settings in use are kept. The capture server does the same before each pre-check (its log is `capture.log`), and the review page when its review tab opens or shows a case and before a close or an undo, so that all three work with the same thresholds.

### 3.3 The mailbox rule

Without the Microsoft sign-in of section 3.14, a mailbox rule fills `inbox/`; it is set up at the installation. It must:

- save the e-mails that carry Exact's PO PDFs, so that the aid knows every PO version;
- save the suppliers' e-mails with their attachments (confirmations, and any other document);
- save the replies to the aid's questions;
- save each as a whole e-mail, an `.eml` file, straight into `inbox/` (not into a subfolder).

Whole e-mails are best: the aid then reads the PO number from the subject and body too, and it can file a reply only by its headers. A `.pdf` or `.png` saved on its own is also handled, and so is an export of the order lines (`.xlsx` or `.csv`) once `export_columns` is set (section 3.13, which also says how another spreadsheet is skipped then); any other file is skipped as "unsupported file type". In the Bachelor End Project the documents of the test-account cases are to be re-sent to the researcher's company mailbox, and the aid reaches that mailbox through the Microsoft sign-in on the settings tab (section 3.14) instead of through a rule.

Instead of this rule, the runner can read a folder of the work mailbox itself after a Microsoft sign-in (section 3.14); it saves the e-mails into `inbox/` in the same form.

**Purchase orders that are already under way.** The aid knows a purchase order from Exact's e-mail with its PDF (or from an export, section 3.13). A confirmation that names an order the aid does not know waits for it: the confirmations of the first weeks, which answer orders sent before the connection, and a webshop's confirmation that comes before Exact's e-mail. Such a case is checked again by itself once the order comes in (section 3.17, "A confirmation that comes before its purchase order"). So at hand-over, drag the e-mails with Exact's PO PDFs of the last weeks into the folder of the mailbox that the aid reads. An e-mail that lay in that folder before the mailbox was connected is left alone (section 3.14): drag it out of the folder and in again. Or give the PDFs, or the e-mails saved as `.eml`, on the tab "Bestanden" of the review page (section 2.7). At its next pass the runner files the orders and checks the cases that wait for them again.

### 3.4 Starting the runner and keeping it running

```bash
python3 -m aid.runner --settings <root>/settings.json [--poll-seconds N] [--max-passes N]
```

- `--settings` (required): the settings file. The STOP file is looked for next to it.
- `--poll-seconds`: seconds between passes, default 60.
- `--max-passes`: stop after this many passes; leave it out in production.

At its start the runner empties `cases/tmp/work` (section 3.1), so that a copy left by a runner that was ended in the middle of it never stays, and removes from the watched folder the copies that a file given on the review page left there when its copy was cut off (names that begin with `.dropin_` and end with `.part`, which the scan never reads), once they are older than ten minutes; a copy that cannot be removed is one line in `runner.log`, and the runner goes on. Each pass: handle the inbox, run every new case, end the waits that are over, and make the digest when it is due. The command runs until the STOP file exists, then prints the number of passes and ends with exit code 0. It ends at once with exit code 2 when the settings file cannot be read or is not valid, when `reader` is `"llm"` without `ms_tenant_id` and `ms_client_id`, or when the writer refuses the Exact settings (for example an address that is not https).

How it is kept running (a scheduled task at start-up, or a service) is the operator's choice on the host. Whatever starts it must:

- run it from the folder that holds `aid/`, with Python 3.11 or newer and the packages installed (or from the folder of section 3.16, whose start files do all of this);
- give the full path of the settings file;
- put `AID_MAIL_PASSWORD` (when `smtp_host` and `smtp_user` are set) in the environment of the process, from the company's secret store, before the start; `AID_EXACT_TOKEN` is not used: no command reads it (section 3.10);
- start it again if it ends with a code other than 0;
- with the work mailbox connected (section 3.14) or with `"reader": "llm"` (Copilot, section 3.15), run it as the same Windows user who signs in on the review page, not as a service account or SYSTEM: the sign-in's tokens are encrypted for that user, and the mailbox and Copilot use the same tokens;
- run one runner per `<root>`, never two.

The runner uses the host's clock, so `digest_time` is the host's local time.

#### 3.4.1 One launcher for the three parts

On the buyer's own PC, the runner, the review page (section 3.7) and the capture server (section 3.13) run side by side. One command starts the three and keeps them running:

```bash
python -m aid.start --settings <root>/settings.json [--testshop] [--poll-seconds N] [--background]
python -m aid.start --settings <root>/settings.json --status
python -m aid.start --settings <root>/settings.json --stop
python -m aid.start --settings <root>/settings.json --resume [--testshop] [--poll-seconds N] [--background]
python -m aid.start --settings <root>/settings.json [--testshop] [--poll-seconds N] --install-autostart
python -m aid.start --settings <root>/settings.json --remove-autostart
```

- **What it starts.** `python -m aid.runner` (with `--poll-seconds` when given), `python -m aid.review` and `python -m aid.capture_server` (with `--testshop` when given), each with the settings file by its full path, from the folder that holds `aid/`, and with the same Python that runs the launcher. Each part is started with `-X utf8 -u` before `-m`, so that its output file holds every line at once and in UTF-8 also under a Python that does not read the environment variables `PYTHONIOENCODING` and `PYTHONUNBUFFERED`, which the launcher sets as well. A launcher that runs isolated (`-I`, as every Python of the folder of section 3.16 does) starts its parts with `-I` too, and so does its start at logon: an isolated Python reads none of Python's environment variables and leaves the user's own folder of packages out. On Windows the word `python` may be a placeholder that does nothing; then call the launcher with the full path of the Python (for example `C:\Users\<you>\AppData\Local\Programs\Python\Python311\python.exe`), and the parts and the start at logon use that path too. The parts open no window. The settings file is read first: when it cannot be read, the launcher names the file and the fields at fault, never their values, starts nothing and ends with exit code 2.
- **In a window or in the background.** A launcher started with the first command lives in the window it was typed in, and its first line says so: closing that window stops the three parts. With `--background` the command first checks, in the window, whether the start is possible (the settings, `STOP`, a launcher that already runs, parts that already answer) and says why when it is not. Otherwise it starts the launcher apart from the window (on Windows with `pythonw.exe`, so without a window of its own), prints where the logs are and returns; the window can then be closed. `--resume --background` does the same after deleting `STOP`. The launcher that Windows starts at logon is such a launcher without a window.
- **The parts end with the launcher.** On Windows the launcher ties the three parts to itself with a job object of Windows: when the launcher ends in any way, also when its window is closed, when it is ended in Task Manager or at a logoff, Windows ends the parts with it. What a part starts itself, such as the browser window that the review page opens for the Microsoft sign-in, is not tied and stays open. If Windows refuses the tie, `start.log` says once "the parts could not be tied to the launcher" and the launcher goes on; only then can parts stay behind when the launcher is ended from outside. On Linux and macOS there is no such tie: the launcher stops its parts on Ctrl-C, on Ctrl-\, on SIGTERM and when its terminal is closed, but not when it is killed outright.
- **One launcher on a PC.** The review page and the capture server always take http://127.0.0.1:7860/ and http://127.0.0.1:7870/health, so only one launcher runs on a PC, whatever its settings folder. Before it starts anything, a launcher asks both addresses. When one of them answers, it starts nothing, ends with exit code 3 and writes "something already answers at <address>: only one launcher runs on a PC; a launcher on other settings is stopped with --stop on its own settings, and parts started by hand are closed". A launcher that runs on another settings folder (the try-out folder `live`, for example) is stopped with `--stop` on that folder's settings file, not by closing its parts, which it would start again; a review page or a capture server started by hand is closed in its own window. When the state file of the last launcher on these settings still names parts as running that were not tied to it (on Linux and macOS, or after a failed tie), a second line gives their names and process ids: close those by the process id (Task Manager, Details). `--stop` on settings where no launcher runs names each address that still answers in the same words, because `STOP` ends a runner only.
- **The logs.** Everything goes to the folder `logs/` next to the settings file: `runner.out`, `review.out` and `capture.out` hold what each part prints (appended, never emptied), and `start.log` holds the launcher's own lines with the time: each start, each end with its exit code, each start again, a part given up, the stop. A fault of the launcher itself is written to `start.log` with its traceback, because a launcher without a window has no other place to show it. The runner's own `cases/runner.log` and the capture server's `cases/capture.log` stay where section 3.6 says.
- **The restart rule.** The launcher looks at the parts every second. A part that ends is started again after 5 seconds, at most 5 times in any 10 minutes; after that it is given up and the other two keep running. A part that ends with exit code 2 had its settings refused (for example the capture server with an empty `capture_token`): `start.log` says so and names its `.out` file, which holds the reason. Such a part is started again as soon as the settings file changes, for example after "Opslaan" on the settings tab, and that start is not one of the five. A part that cannot be started at all is logged with the error itself. When all three have ended or been given up, the launcher ends with exit code 1.
- **The STOP file is respected.** While `STOP` exists next to the settings (section 3.5), the launcher starts nothing, writes "stopped by the STOP file: delete it, or use --resume" and ends with exit code 3; this holds at logon too. When `STOP` appears while it runs, it ends the review page and the capture server at once, lets the runner end by itself at its next pass (and ends it after its poll time plus 30 seconds if it has not), and ends with exit code 0. Ctrl-C in its window, SIGTERM and, on Linux and macOS, Ctrl-\ and a closed terminal stop it the same way, within a second: the launcher then creates `STOP` itself for the time of the stop, with the text "launcher" and the time, so that the runner ends by itself, and deletes the file again when the parts have ended. A `STOP` file that was already there is never changed or deleted by such a stop. A second Ctrl-C, a second or more after the first, ends the parts at once. If such a stop is cut off, for example because the PC is switched off, the launcher's own `STOP` file can stay behind; the next start sees by its text that no person made it, removes it and says so. `--stop` creates `STOP`, says that it waits, and waits up to 90 seconds for the three to end, then says whether they did. `--resume` deletes `STOP` and starts as above. When a launcher is still stopping, `--resume` first waits for it to end, at most 150 seconds, and only then deletes `STOP` and starts; when that launcher has not ended by then, it starts nothing and leaves `STOP` as it is, and when another launcher was started meanwhile (by a second `--resume`), it starts nothing either. A Ctrl-C while `--stop` waits ends that command and leaves `STOP` in place, so the launcher goes on stopping; a Ctrl-C during the watch of a start (below) or the wait of `--resume` ends that command, and nothing is started. Both say so in one line and end with exit code 130.
- **The state file, and a launcher that ended without a stop.** The launcher keeps `start_state.json` next to the settings: its start, a heartbeat written every 5 seconds, the runner's poll time, and per part its start, the number of starts, whether it was given up, whether it was tied to the launcher and, while it runs, its process id. A second launcher on the same settings is refused. A start that finds a heartbeat older than 20 seconds takes the file for the leftover of a launcher that ended without a stop (a crash, a shutdown, a closed window). A younger heartbeat is watched for 7 seconds, and the window says so first. When it moves, a launcher runs: the start says "already running" and ends with exit code 3. When it has not moved, the file is a leftover as well. So after a quick log off and on, when the heartbeat of the session before is still fresh, the launcher at logon starts 7 seconds later instead of not at all. When the leftover names a runner that was not tied to its launcher (Linux and macOS, or a failed tie on Windows), that runner may still be running, and only `STOP` ends it: the start makes the launcher's own `STOP` file, says that it gives that runner its poll time plus 5 seconds to end (65 seconds when the launcher was started without `--poll-seconds`), waits that long, removes the file and goes on. `--background` and `--install-autostart` make this watch and this wait in the window and remove the leftover there ("found the state file (start_state.json) of a launcher that no longer runs: it is removed"), so that the launcher they start does neither again; a launcher in a window or at logon replaces the file itself. A start after a leftover thus takes up to 7 seconds longer, and where the parts were not tied also the runner's poll time plus 5 seconds. `--status` prints one line for the launcher and one per part, and whether the review page (http://127.0.0.1:7860/) and the capture server (http://127.0.0.1:7870/health) answer; it ends with 0 while the launcher runs and with 1 otherwise. For up to 20 seconds after a launcher ended without a stop, it still shows that launcher as running. After that, on Windows, it shows "launcher: not running; it was ended from outside (last heartbeat ...), and its parts ended with it" and each part as "ended with the launcher": Windows ended them, and there is nothing to close. Where the parts were not tied, it shows "it ended without stopping its parts", the runner as "may still run (process N); it obeys the STOP file", and a review page or a capture server as "leftover, process N" while its address answers and as "ended" otherwise. The next start replaces the file. The status lines and the addresses assume the default ports: a review page that moved to another port (section 3.7) shows as not answering.
- **The start at logon on Windows.** `--install-autostart` puts a shortcut `Inkoopassistent.lnk` in the user's own Startup folder (normally `%APPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup`; a Start Menu that the organisation moved elsewhere is followed), which starts the launcher with `pythonw.exe` beside the Python in use, so that no window opens, with the same settings, `--testshop` and `--poll-seconds` as given. It needs no administrator rights. The shortcut is made through PowerShell and read back. When PowerShell cannot be used, or the saved shortcut does not hold the paths it was given (a path with a character that the shortcut cannot keep), a file `Inkoopassistent.cmd` in the same folder does the same, and the command says which of the two it made. When neither can be made, the command says that the start at logon could not be set, prints what the shortcut would run (so that one can be made by hand in the Startup folder), starts nothing and ends with exit code 1. Otherwise it starts the launcher in the background at once, after the same checks as any start, and prints where the logs are. Its last line asks you to log off and on once and to check with `--status` that the launcher started, after `--resume --background` when `STOP` kept the launcher from starting now: that is the only real test of the start at logon. From then on the three parts start by themselves each time this user logs on. The shortcut holds the full paths of the Python, of the folder that holds `aid/` and of the settings file. When the project folder or the settings folder is moved, or the Python is installed elsewhere, run `--install-autostart` again: otherwise nothing starts at logon and nothing is logged. To take it away, run `--remove-autostart`, which deletes the shortcut and the `.cmd` file; a launcher that is running goes on until `--stop`. On another system both options say that they are for Windows and end with exit code 2.
- **Environment variables.** The parts get the environment of the launcher. A variable set in one PowerShell window (`$env:AID_MAIL_PASSWORD = "..."`) reaches a launcher started from that window, also with `--background` or `--install-autostart`, but not the launcher that Windows starts at the next logon. A variable the aid needs (`AID_MAIL_PASSWORD`, section 3.4) must therefore be a user variable of Windows ("Edit environment variables for your account" in the Start menu), so that the launcher at logon has it too. Without the mailbox password the runner writes every message as a file, without a warning, while the review page asks nothing and says after a close that the mail server's password is not set (section 3.11).

The exit codes of the launcher: 0 a clean stop or a done action, also `--install-autostart` once the start at logon is set, whatever became of the start now, and `--stop` whatever became of the stop; 1 every part ended, a fault of the launcher, a start at logon that could not be set, `--status` when no launcher runs, a start in the background that cannot be made, or `--remove-autostart` that cannot delete a file; 2 the settings or an option refused, also a settings file that does not exist; 3 not started (`STOP` exists, a launcher already runs, a part already answers, another launcher was started on the same settings in the same moment, or `--resume` found that the launcher it waited for has not ended or that another one was started meanwhile); 130 a Ctrl-C outside the supervision (the watch of a start, the waits of `--stop` and `--resume`).

### 3.5 The STOP file

Create an empty file `STOP` next to the settings file. The runner ends before its next pass; a pass that is running finishes first, and the runner may take up to the poll interval (60 seconds by default) to notice the file. While `STOP` exists, a new start makes no pass at all. Delete `STOP` before starting the runner again. Documents that arrive meanwhile wait in the inbox and are handled once the runner runs again. `STOP` does not stop a review page started on its own. The launcher of section 3.4.1 obeys `STOP` as well: it starts nothing while the file exists, and when the file appears it stops all three parts, the review page and the capture server included. A `STOP` file whose text begins with the word "launcher" is the launcher's own, made for the time of a stop that it started itself (Ctrl-C, for example), or by a start for the time it gives a runner left behind by an earlier launcher to end, and deleted afterwards; leave such a file alone.

### 3.6 The logs and the audit record

`cases/runner.log` has one line per problem: the time, the case id or the stage (`scan`, `scan <file>`, `recheck`, `recheck <case>` for a confirmation whose check again failed (section 3.17), `list of changes` for the comparison of the list "In Exact invoeren" with the orders as they stand (section 2.6), `intake file` for `intake.jsonl` (below) when its lines cannot be written, with the number of lines that wait, and once more when they are written, `set aside <file>` for a file of `inbox/` that the runner set aside after 30 days ("not read after 30 days; moved to processed/") or cannot move then (it tries again at the next pass), `escalation`, `digest`, `cases root`, `digest_state.json`, `mailbox`, `settings`, `work folder`, `watched folder`), and the error. A case that keeps failing is logged once per run of the loop, and once more when it later succeeds. The pass goes on after an error.

`cases/capture.log` is the capture server's: one line per refused request (the status and the reason; a page without a cart table names its kept copy `captures/nocart_<...>`), per error outside a case's run (with its traceback; the banner shows "Voorcontrole mislukt" with "the pre-check failed; see capture.log") and per connection that ended badly (a client that stalled past the 60-second timeout, a request the server could not parse). A case whose run failed is not in it: its error is in the case folder, as for any failed case (section 5). The token is never in it. The server also prints one line per capture on its console: the time, the shop's host, the case id and the verdict.
