`cases/digest_state.json` holds `last_sent` (the time of the last digest, or null), `reported` (the cases the next digest reports), `skipped` (the skipped files it lists), `mailbox_lapsed` while the Microsoft sign-in has lapsed (section 5, "Postvak niet verbonden"), `copilot` while Copilot fails as a whole (the error and the number of cases waiting for it), `copilot_cases` while Copilot has failed a case (the cases, with their refusals and failures, in the order they are tried last; section 3.15), and `intake` while a file stays in `inbox/` as an error, or because it could not be moved: by its name, its sha256 (none while another program holds it so that it cannot be read), since when it stays and the lines `intake.jsonl` holds of it, so that a file the runner tries again at every pass gets its lines once, and is set aside after 30 days (section 2.7); `skipped` then names it once, as "set aside after 30 days without being read", for the next digest. The lines named are those `intake.jsonl` holds: a line that waits because the file cannot be written is added once it is written. Within a run the runner keeps its own copy of `intake`, which decides, so that a state file that cannot be written repeats no line; the copy in the file is what the next run starts from. When it cannot be read, a fresh one is started and the log says so.

`cases/intake.jsonl` is what the tab "Bestanden" shows (section 2.7): one JSON line per document the runner handled, appended at each pass, `{"time": ..., "file": ..., "outcome": ..., "detail": ...}`. The outcome is `po` (the detail "<PO number> v<version>"), `export` ("<orders> <new or changed>"), `case` or `guess` (the case id), `answer`, `bounce` or `auto` (the id of the case a supplier's answer, a bounce or an automatic reply was filed with), `recheck` (the id of the new case of a confirmation checked again), `skipped` (the reason of the digest's list of skipped files; also `duplicate` for a PO PDF or an export filed before), `error` (`export: <reason>` for an export the aid refused, `version` for a PO PDF of a version it holds from another file, else empty) or `set_aside` (the days a file stayed before the runner set it aside). It holds file names, order numbers, case ids, numbers of days and fixed reasons only, never what a document or an error says. When `intake.jsonl` cannot be written (another program holds it), the runner keeps the lines of the pass, each with the time of its own pass, and writes them before the lines of a later pass, in their order; `runner.log` says how many wait, and when they were written. Lines kept so are lost only when the runner stops before it could write them; a file that still stays then gets its line at the next run.

`cases/<case>/audit.json` is the audit record, for the researcher, IT and the thesis; the buyer never sees it. It holds the inputs by sha256, the model (none in parser mode), the settings with the authorization limit only as "set" or "unset", every step's output with its seconds (the step "entered", after "version", only for a case that has `steps/03_entered.json`, section 3.1), every rule decision with the settings it read, every write and message, the errors, and the timings. The review page adds a list `close`, the mailbox a list `answers` (each filed reply or bounce, with the `message_id` of the question it names), and the escalation a list `escalations`.

### 3.7 The review page on the host

```bash
python3 -m aid.review --settings <root>/settings.json [--share]
```

It runs on its own, beside the runner, and serves the page on 127.0.0.1 of the host only: open it in a browser on that machine. The page answers its own host only: a request whose Host header is not 127.0.0.1 or localhost, alone or with the page's port, gets "Invalid host header" (status 400) and reaches nothing of the page, so that a web page whose domain name points at this computer cannot press the page's buttons. Open the page by one of those two names. It uses port 7860 when that port is free. If it is taken (for example by a review page that is still running), the page starts on the next free port, up to 7959, and prints its address. Stop the old page first, or set `review_url` to the printed address, so that the digest's links work. `--share` opens a public link and is for the made-up set only: with company settings (an Exact address, endpoints or fields, an authorization limit, an SMTP server or login, or an address outside example.com and example.org) it is refused with exit code 2. It is refused as well while the columns of an export are set, those of the export of order lines (`export_columns`, part 2 of the block "Koppeling met Exact") or those of the export of articles (`article_columns`, its part 3), since those column titles were chosen from the company's own files (section 3.17). An unreadable settings file also gives exit code 2. The page has five tabs: "Beoordelen", the review of the cases, "In Exact invoeren", the changes to enter in Exact (section 2.6), "Prijslijst", a supplier's price list compared with the purchase prices of an export from Exact (section 4.3), "Bestanden", the files the buyer gives the aid and what became of each file (section 2.7), and "Instellingen", the buyer's settings and suppliers (section 2.8) and, folded away at its bottom under "Koppelingen: het Microsoft-postvak en de AI-dienst", the connection of the work mailbox (section 3.14) and of the AI service (section 3.15), which writes into the settings file the page was started with. With `--share` the host of the public link passes the check of the Host header as well. On a page opened with `--share` the tabs "In Exact invoeren", "Prijslijst" and "Bestanden" and the settings tab say only "Niet beschikbaar op een openbare link" and offer nothing, and `--share` is refused altogether while the settings name a tenant (`ms_tenant_id`) or a folder of the work mailbox (`mail_folder`), since the cases may then come from company e-mails.

The page keeps a file uploaded to it and the page images of a document it shows as temporary files in `cases/tmp/` under `cases_root` (the page library's temporary folder, `GRADIO_TEMP_DIR`, which the command sets before it loads the library). While it runs, it removes a temporary file between one and two hours after it was made: once an hour it removes those older than an hour. It removes them all at its next start, when it empties that folder, all but its folder `work`, where the runner keeps a copy for a moment (section 3.1). When it ends, it removes them only at a stop with Ctrl-C in its own window: a page that is ended from outside, as the launcher of section 3.4.1 ends it at a stop, leaves them until its next start. So no document of a case lies outside the aid's own folders. The page also tells the browser to keep none of these files (`Cache-Control: no-store` on each), so that the page images of a document do not stay in the browser's cache, which lies outside them too. When the environment names a folder in `GRADIO_TEMP_DIR` already, the page uses that folder and does not empty it.

As the command builds the page, its writer is the prepared writer: a close prepares its changes in `cases/prepared/prepared_changes.csv`, where the tab "In Exact invoeren" lists them for the buyer (section 2.6).

**The page sends as the runner does.** For each close the page makes the mailer that `send_via` names (section 3.2), with the function the runner makes its own with (`mailer_of` of `aid/messaging/microsoft_mailer.py`), from the settings file as it is at that moment and the sign-in as it is then. So a sign-in or a save on the settings tab counts for the next close, without a restart of the page. A question or a request of a close goes out through the work mailbox while its sign-in works (section 3.14), and through the SMTP server when that is set up and `AID_MAIL_PASSWORD` is in the environment the page was started with (section 3.11).

**When a message of a close is written as a file, and when it is not sent.** By the state of the settings and the connections at the close:

| State | What the close does | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| `send_via` is `"file"` | The message is written as an `.eml` file into `cases/outbox/`, sent by no one, and counts as asked: the case waits. The page says "Het bericht is niet verstuurd maar als bestand klaargezet (cases/outbox): de instellingen laten berichten als bestand schrijven." | Nothing: this is the owner's choice, for a try-out or a run in which nothing may leave. Set `send_via` to another value to send. |
| `send_via` is `"auto"` and nothing can send: no folder of the work mailbox is chosen (with or without a sign-in), or a folder is chosen and nobody is signed in, and no SMTP server is set | Nothing is sent and nothing written. The case stays in review with the item "Bericht niet verzonden", and the page says "Er is geen postvak verbonden: verbind een postvak onder Instellingen, Koppelingen, en sluit de zaak opnieuw af." | Sign in and choose the folder under "Koppelingen" (section 3.14), then close the case again: the question goes out. |
| `send_via` is `"smtp"` and no SMTP server is set | The same, with the same sentence. | Set `smtp_host` and `smtp_user` (section 3.11), or choose another `send_via`. |
| The SMTP server is set and `AID_MAIL_PASSWORD` is not in the environment the page was started with | The same, with "Het wachtwoord van de mailserver is niet ingesteld: geef dit door aan de onderzoeker." | Make `AID_MAIL_PASSWORD` a user variable of Windows and start the page again (section 3.4.1); the tab "Koppelingen" cannot put this right. |
| The mailbox or the server is there and the sending fails (no connection, a lapsed sign-in, a refusal) | The message is recorded "not sent (<the class of the error>)" in the case's result, with the error's text in `steps/09_close.json`; nothing is recorded as asked; the case stays in review with the item "Bericht niet verzonden", and the page names the kind of cause (section 2.2). | What the page's sentence says; the next close sends the message. |
| The failure comes at the hand-over itself (the send of the draft in the work mailbox gets no answer; the SMTP connection drops after the message was given) | The message is recorded as sent, with `"unconfirmed": true`, under the id it carries, and the case waits: a reply to it is filed. The page says "Niet zeker of het bericht is verstuurd: ..." (section 2.2). | Look in Sent items. Nothing is sent a second time. |
| The settings file cannot be read at the close | Nothing is sent and nothing written: the page does not send by the settings it read before. The case stays in review, and the page says "De instellingen kunnen nu niet worden gelezen: er is niets verstuurd. Geef dit door aan de onderzoeker." A close without a message goes on as before. | Put the settings file right (section 3.2); the page reads it again by itself. |

The runner's own messages are not held back in this way: with nothing to send through, the runner writes each of its messages as a file into `cases/outbox/`, as before (section 3.8 for the digest). A page opened with `--share` never makes a mailer and writes files, and no message about a case made from a try-out confirmation is sent or written, on any page (section 3.17); nor is one about a case whose document cannot be read at the moment of the close, since the page cannot tell then whether it is a try-out.

### 3.8 Where the digest goes

The runner always writes the HTML version to `cases/digests/<YYYY-MM-DD>.html` (a second digest on the same day replaces it). The text goes to `buyer_email` the way `send_via` says (section 3.2): through the work mailbox while its sign-in works (section 3.14), through the aid's mailbox when SMTP is set up, and otherwise as an `.eml` file into `cases/outbox/`. A digest that the work mailbox cannot send because the sign-in has lapsed is written into `cases/outbox/` too, and counts as made.

### 3.9 Teams via Power Automate

A Power Automate flow outside the code can relay the digest to the buyer in Teams, so that no AI service is connected to Teams. The runner sets up no flow.

- Trigger: "When a new email arrives" (Office 365 Outlook connector) in the mailbox that receives the digest, with a subject that starts with `[Inkoopassistent]` and the sender the digest really has: `aid_email` when the aid sends through SMTP, the address of the work mailbox when it sends through that mailbox (section 3.14; with `mailbox` empty, the signed-in account's own address). The prefix is the same in both languages. A digest written into `cases/outbox/` is not sent, and reaches no flow.
- Action: "Post message in a chat or channel" (Microsoft Teams connector, by the Flow bot in a chat with the buyer) with the e-mail's text, or "Post card in a chat or channel" with a card built from the HTML version in `cases/digests/<day>.html`. The e-mail itself carries only the text.

If the flow is not allowed, the e-mail is the notification.

### 3.10 How changes reach Exact

**The list.** The aid writes nothing in Exact. The buyer opens Exact Globe+ as a virtual application, which no program of the aid reaches, so every change is prepared: the prepared writer adds a row to `cases/prepared/prepared_changes.csv` (columns `entry_id`, `case_id`, `po_number`, `version`, `line_id`, `article_code`, `kind`, `old_value`, `new_value`, `document`, `rule_id`, `timestamp`), and the buyer enters the change in Exact from the tab "In Exact invoeren" of the review page, which keeps his ticks in `cases/prepared/marks.jsonl` beside it (section 2.6). Only a price, a delivery date or a quantity of an order line and an article's purchase price are rows of the list. The aid prepares the confirmation to attach and the status Bevestigd too, which are no rows to tick: the tab shows each confirmation at its bottom for 7 days after the aid last prepared the attachment or the status, the digest marks them "(zelf in Exact doen)", and the buyer attaches the confirmation and sets the status Bevestigd in Exact himself, as he does today. The runner, the review page and the batch prepare into that folder; give the price-list import the same folder (section 4.3). Nobody applies the rows from the file itself. A tick given by mistake is taken back on the tab with "Weer op de lijst zetten" (section 2.6). By hand, the same is `reopen(Path("<root>/cases/prepared"), ["<entry id>", ...], datetime.now())` of `aid/execution/by_hand.py`, the entry ids being those of the change's rows in `prepared_changes.csv`: it writes the mark "reopened" only when that puts the change on the list again, and returns the state the change would have otherwise; for a change that was put back, by the page or with the recipe below once its undo is entered or seen, it writes nothing and returns "undone". Do not use `mark(..., "reopened")` for this: it writes the mark whatever the state, so that while a put-back of the change waits on the list, the change and its put-back cancel each other and both leave the list, while Exact still holds the entered value.

**`exact_base_url` stays empty.** So do `exact_endpoints` and `exact_fields`. No command builds a write route into Exact: the runner, the review page and the capture server all work with the prepared writer, and none of them reads `AID_EXACT_TOKEN`. A settings file in which `exact_base_url` is set is refused. The runner, the review page, the capture server and the launcher end at their start with exit code 2 and this sentence, on the console and in the launcher's log (`logs/start.log`):

    exact_base_url is set, but this version of the aid writes nothing in Exact: every change is prepared for the tab "In Exact invoeren". Remove exact_base_url from the settings file.

A part that is running when the key is set keeps the settings in use and writes the sentence in its log (`runner.log`, `capture.log`); the settings tab shows it in Dutch ("exact_base_url staat ingesteld, maar deze versie van de assistent schrijft niets in Exact: elke wijziging wordt klaargezet op het tabblad "In Exact invoeren". Haal exact_base_url uit het instellingenbestand."), shows the settings in use and refuses every save until the key is taken out. `--status` and `--stop` of the launcher do not read the settings and work as before. A file that holds the key as `null` or empty, as an older file does, loads as before.

**What the module `aid/execution/exact_api.py` is.** The writer behind the three keys is no part of what runs. It was written against a stand-in of the tests, a made-up server on the PC itself, and was never run against Exact: it reads a field through a web API, writes the new value and reads it back, and the web service that Exact offers has another shape, so it fits no door of Exact Globe+. It is kept, with its tests, as the place where a writer for a real door would plug in (below). A case made from a try-out confirmation (section 3.17) writes through the prepared writer only, whatever writer a part is given.

**What a program door would need.** Of the three ways in that Exact names for a program, the one a writer of the aid could use is the Entity Services of Exact. The company would have to open: the Entity Services of Exact on a company server that the aid's PC reaches; an Exact user for the aid with rights on purchase orders (and on articles, for a purchase price); the name of that server and of the database (the administration); and one point is open, whether the Entity Services let a line of an existing purchase order be updated at all.

**Where it would plug in.** A writer for that door implements the Writer protocol of `aid/execution/writer.py` and stands in front of the prepared writer, as `RetryingWriter` puts the writer of `aid/execution/exact_api.py` in front of it in the tests: `build_deps` of the runner, which today always gives the prepared writer, would make `RetryingWriter(<the new writer>, PreparedWriter(<cases_root>/prepared))`, and the refusal of `exact_base_url` in `aid/settings.py` would go. What follows describes what the code does with such a writer, as its tests run it; no installation shows any of it. A change that writer makes is an entry made in the system (status "done"): it is in the case's write log with its old value, has an undo link in the digest and is offered for undo on the tab "Beoordelen", and it never reaches `prepared_changes.csv`, so it never reaches the list. A change it cannot make falls back to the prepared writer: it lands on the list, and the case comes to the review page with "prepared change: apply by hand". What the writer learns about a write decides the rest. Refused (a 4xx answer), not sent at all (no token, or a connection that was never made: refused, a connect time-out, a TLS or a proxy failure), ignored (answered with success while the read after it shows the value from before): that attempt failed as any other, and after the last one the change is prepared. Accepted (answered with success) with a check that then failed, because the read after it failed or shows a value that is neither the new one nor the one read before: the system may hold the change, so the writer raises `UnverifiedWrite` (a `WriterError` that carries the entry of the attempt), and when no attempt verifies the change `RetryingWriter` returns that entry: it is logged as made (status "done"), with the value read before the first attempt as its previous value and the failed check in `error`, it is never prepared, and the case comes to the review page with "write made, not verified: check in Exact". No answer over a connection that stood (a timeout, a lost connection) or an answer that is neither a success nor a refusal (a gateway's 502 can follow a write that was made): the outcome is not known, and the writer raises `UnknownWrite` after that attempt and after every later one that fails without settling it. A read settles it: the new value read means made and checked; the value from before, read after a write that was answered, means not made; after a write that got no answer the value from before settles nothing, because that write can still land. When the last write is still in doubt, `RetryingWriter` returns the entry of the first such attempt with what happened in `error` and `"outcome": "not known"` in its evidence: logged as made too, never prepared, and the review reason is "write outcome not known: check in Exact". An attachment is a POST, which a repeat would make a second time, and this API has no read of the attachments: an attachment in doubt is not sent again and ends as not sure; a detachment in doubt is sent again and only a success settles it. In both cases the later changes of the case are "not applied: an earlier change waits for a look in Exact", and the digest and the tab "Beoordelen" offer the undo. An attempt that reads, before it writes, the value that an earlier attempt of the same change sent takes that read as the check and writes nothing again, so a write whose answer was lost is found there, when that read works, and logged as made and checked. What the buyer's decision on such an item does is in section 2.4: after "Bevestigen" the close counts the entry as made (`_made` of `aid/review/close.py`), writes and prepares nothing for it, and includes it in the request to the approver of an order above the authorization, which the run did not send for it; after "Houden" the close undoes the entry through its writer, as the tab's undo does, and makes none of the changes that waited behind it, the attachment and the status included. A status or an attached file that the page can only prepare to put back is no row of the list: the close then says so with the sentence of the tab's undo ("De assistent kan deze wijziging niet terugdraaien; zet de oude waarde zelf terug in Exact."), notes the kind under `put_back_by_hand` in its record, and keeps the case in review until the next close.

**What a builder of a route must still settle.** The checks of the module found these four, and they are not put right, since nothing builds the route. A doubt that outlives its operation: the writer remembers a write whose outcome is not known for as long as the writer lives, and the runner's writer lives across cases, so a later case's call for the same change is logged as made and not sure, with the first case's error and its previous value, although nothing was sent in that case. The article price that waits behind a look: when the price of an order line is made and not verified, the article price of the same rule is not applied and is a review item of its own; "Bevestigen" on it writes the article price only with the article box ticked, which the page ticks for no such item, and nothing on the page names the article. A close's own write that asks for a look: on a review page whose writer is a route, a write the close makes itself that ends not verified or not sure stops the close, and the next close counts it as made, with no review item decided on it and, above the authorization, no request to the approver. A read that lags behind the write: when the system made the change and its read still shows the value from before, the writer takes the write as ignored, and the change is tried again and then prepared while the system holds it.

**The undo.** The undo applies the reverse change (the old value back; an attached file removed) and logs it with `undo_of` naming the change. For a change made in a system the old value is the value read before the write (the entry's `previous`), also where the purchase order as the aid read it said another value; the tab "Beoordelen" and the digest name the change by that value too, and by "leeg" where the field was empty before the write and "onbekend" where nothing was read, as its put-back does. An undo never writes a value the system did not hold: when the field was empty before the write (a null or a blank text was read, which the entry's evidence notes under `read_before`) or nothing was read, a writer into a system is not called and `undo_entry` raises a `WriterError` that names the kind, the place and which of the two it is; through the page, whose writer prepares, the put-back goes on the list without a value, "... → leeg" with "Maak het veld in Exact leeg en vink de regel af." for a field that was empty and "... → onbekend" where nothing was read. For a prepared change the old value is the one its row showed. The put-back of a change made in a system is judged against that change: the row it puts back is no row of the list, so a tick made on the list before that write never makes the put-back "already put back" (`_of_place` of `aid/execution/by_hand.py`); a tick on the put-back itself, or a newer row of its place, ends it as any other. Each change is undone once, but for "Terugdraaien in Exact" on the tab, which undoes a change a second time once its first put-back was replaced or dropped and nothing newer stands in the way (section 2.6). A change that was only prepared was not made, so the review page offers no undo for it: the buyer drops it on the tab "In Exact invoeren" instead. From the review page an undo is prepared too (section 3.7), and so lands on the list; the page then says "Nog niet teruggedraaid in Exact. Op de lijst staat nu: ...", never "Ongedaan gemaakt". To undo every change of a case through the runner's writer, newest first:

```python
import os
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from aid.settings import load_settings
from aid.runner import build_deps
from aid.execution.writer import undo_case

settings = load_settings(Path("<root>/settings.json"))
deps = build_deps(settings, os.environ)
print(undo_case(settings.cases_root / "<case id>", deps.writer, datetime.now()))
```

On the list (section 2.6) an undo counts as follows. It belongs to the last change of its place (the order line, or the article) whose new value is the value it takes away, and it takes away only its own case's request in that change. When that request was never ticked as entered, the undo takes it off the list, and itself too, as if neither had been asked: Exact still holds the value from before. When another case asked the same value, the change stays on the list for that case alone, and ticking it later brings back nothing of what the undo took away. When the request was ticked as entered, the undo is a change to enter of its own, the old value back: "Inkooporder 250108 (S08), regel 1 (KM-0801): prijs 12,70 → 12,50", or, for an article price whose price before it the aid never read, "artikel KM-0801: inkoopprijs 12,70 terugzetten naar de prijs van daarvoor", where the buyer puts back the price that Exact held before. An undo whose case asked nothing in that change takes nothing away: it is a change to enter when that change was ticked as entered, and is left out otherwise. The recipe has its limits: an undo made with it that a later case's request for the same value joins, or whose change's tick is taken back, may be passed over by the list; prefer "Terugdraaien in Exact" on the tab (section 2.6).

**Seen in a new purchase order.** No program reads Exact, but Exact e-mails a new PDF of the purchase order when a changed order is released again and the buyer has it sent then (process record, steps 5 and 6; the owner's check A7 gave the answer: on the release page of Exact, Verrichten, sending the order again is a choice, so the print comes on some days and not on others). At each pass, after the cases ran, the runner compares every open change of an order line with the order as the store was last given it (`reconcile` of `aid/execution/by_hand.py`: `PoStore.given_last`, the newest PDF version or the newest look from an export, whichever came last, also when it came again without showing anything new) and marks a change whose new value that look shows as seen, with a line in `marks.jsonl` whose "by" is "version <n>", or "export" for an export; the rules are in section 2.6. The look counts only when the store was given it after the newest row of the change was prepared (`PoStore.given_at` against the row's `timestamp` in `prepared_changes.csv`; `given_at` is when the look was filed, `PoStore.filed_at`, or later, when a print that shows the order so came again): the version the case compared with never ticks a change off, and a put-back is ticked off only by a print or an export given after the put-back itself. The time that counts is when the store was given the look, not when Exact made it: a print that reaches the aid late (the runner was stopped, the sign-in of the work mailbox had lapsed, or a PDF was saved and put in the watched folder later) counts as new, and can tick a change or a put-back off although Exact printed it before the buyer entered the change; section 2.6 asks the buyer to check those put-backs in Exact. An article price is never seen in a purchase order; a change that is dropped stays so whatever a later PDF shows, and a second pass over the same documents marks nothing more. After the changes seen, the same pass ends each tick that the order as it stands contradicts (`contradict`): at each place, the last change that is entered or seen, when the store was given the order as it stands after its mark and it shows another value for its line, or its line cannot be found there and no line of its article shows its value; its marks are taken away with a mark "reopened" by "version <n>" or "export", which keeps the value the print showed. A try-out's change is left out, and so is a change whose article no case says. A mark "seen" carries the time of marking, the runner's clock at that moment and not the time its pass began, so that the value counts for an older print that the same pass filed before the look. An error of this step is logged in `runner.log` as "list of changes", once in a run of the runner, and the pass goes on. The digest counts the changes still open after it.

**The order as Exact holds it.** Since no print is sure to come, the marks themselves count in the next comparison. After the pipeline has selected the version a document answers, from the versions as the store holds them, it lays the values that the marks say Exact holds over that version (`as_entered` of `aid/execution/by_hand.py`, from `entered_values`): at each place of the list the new value of the last change that is entered or seen, when its mark was made after the store was last given that version (`PoStore.given_at`: when it was filed, or later, when the same PDF, the look's own export or an export with its rows came again, which files nothing and leaves `given.txt` in the folder). Before it does, it brings the list in line with the order as it stands (`reconcile` for that PO alone: the changes seen, then the ticks that a newer print contradicts), so that a tick ended by a print counts no more. A print contradicts a tick when it was given after the tick and shows another value on the tick's line, or when that line cannot be found in it and no line of its article shows the ticked value; the mark "reopened" that ends the tick keeps the value the print showed, for the note on the list. A place is a line of the order: rows of one PO and kind are of one place when they carry one line id and their item codes agree (step 01 of each row's case says the item code), or, whatever their line ids, when they carry an item code that one line holds. An item code that several lines of a version hold tells no line from another, and neither does a line id across prints (a print may number its lines by their place). The line of such a change in another print is the one line of that article that agrees with the line it was asked for in its quantity, its delivery date and its unit price, the changed field apart; in the very print its case was compared with (the same file, by its sha256) it is the line it was asked for; when no line, or more than one, agrees, the change has no line there. Such rows are places by those values where they tell the line from the other lines of its article, else by their own print and line id, so that the changes of two deliveries are never one row. A row whose case says no item code is taken with the rows of its line id when those name one line. Each value is first given its line of the version; of those that name one line there, the one marked last decides. A put-back that is entered or seen is such a change, so the old value is back; an open, dropped, replaced or undone change never counts; the marks of a try-out's changes count only in a case made from a try-out. The line is found as for a change seen (section 2.6); a value whose line the version lacks is not applied, nor is a price whose line there states another price basis than the line the change was asked for. A price is written over the net price of a line that states a net price or a discount, else over its unit price; the PO's total and whether it is above the authorization limit are worked out again. The case's `steps/03_entered.json` and the step "entered" of its audit record hold what was replaced, only when something was (section 3.1); `steps/01_po_versions.json` keeps the versions as the store gave them. While `prepared_changes.csv` or `marks.jsonl` cannot be read, or the first cannot be understood (its first line lacks the writer's columns: `ListUnread`), the case is compared with the version as it is, that step holds `"unread": true`, and one REVIEW action of the pipeline's own rule L01 takes the place of the routing's actions, as G01 does for a guessed order: nothing is prepared, requested or sent, and every difference is the buyer's. When a case's document names again the value that the marks say Exact holds for a line, the open changes of that line get the mark "replaced" (`withdraw`), by "case <id>": the supplier took the other value back. The pre-check judges a case it already holds by the same list each time it answers (`held_now`, with the ticks that the order as it stands contradicts counted as ended in reading only: it writes no mark): every line and field that matched, the printed ones too, and a prepared write only while its change stands on the list (section 2.5). A try-out is made from the order as a try-out's case is compared with it (section 3.17), so a second try-out starts from what the owner entered for the first.

**Put back from the tab.** "Terugdraaien in Exact" (section 2.6) is the undo of one change that is in Exact, for the buyer: for a change of a case it calls the undo of the review page's close (`undo` of `aid/review/close.py`, as above), so that the case's write log, `steps/09_close.json` and audit record hold it; for an article price of a price list it calls `undo_entry` on the list's write-log folder, the folder under `<cases_root>/pricelists/` whose `write_log.jsonl` holds the change (the import's default folder with `--settings`). A list imported with `--write-log` elsewhere, an article price whose price before it the aid never read, and an undo itself cannot be put back from the tab, and the page says so. The undo is prepared through the page's writer, so it is a change to enter, the old value back, as above; one more line of `marks.jsonl`, `{"entry_id": <the new row>, "mark": "puts_back", "of": <the change's entry>, "at": ...}`, links it to the change, so that it shows with "Terugzetten:" before it and a second press does not prepare it again while that put-back is on the list, entered or seen. A press undoes a change a second time once its first put-back was replaced or dropped and nothing newer stands in the way: a second undo entry with the same `undo_of` in the write log, and a second "undo" record in `steps/09_close.json`. A change undone before with the recipe above is linked to that undo instead.

### 3.11 The SMTP mailer

Set `smtp_host`, `smtp_port` (587) and `smtp_user`, and put the password in `AID_MAIL_PASSWORD` before the runner starts. The runner checks for the variable once, at the start: without it, it writes every message as a file into `cases/outbox/` and sends nothing, without a warning. The review page looks for it at each close, in the environment it was started with: without it, the page sends nothing and writes nothing, keeps the case in review and says that the mail server's password is not set (section 3.7). When the server refuses the supplier's address, the message goes to nobody, the buyer's copy included; when it refuses only the buyer's copy, the supplier has the question and the case's record counts the refused copy. The mailer uses STARTTLS and checks the server's certificate. It sends from `aid_email`. An error names its kind, never the password, the login or a recipient. Each message carries a Message-ID, by which a reply is filed. In the Bachelor End Project the questions go to the researcher's address only, never to a real supplier: `supplier_emails` holds that address.

### 3.12 Reading without AI: the parser

The runner reads with Microsoft 365 Copilot (`"reader": "llm"`, section 3.15) or with the template parser (`"reader": "parser"`). Company documents never go to another AI service. In parser mode:

- a document without a text layer (a scan, a photo) cannot be classified: it is skipped as "no text layer; class unknown" and listed in the digest;
- a document with text but no known keyword is skipped as "other";
- a confirmation is read only if a parser template fits its layout (section 4.1); otherwise the case fails.

Not built yet: the templates of the real suppliers' layouts (Task 5b).

**The PO PDF.** A PO PDF is read without AI under either reader. Without a layout in the settings the PO reader knows only the made-up layout. The layout of the PO PDF that Exact e-mails is the setting `po_layout`: it is written on the company side, from one of the company's own POs, in words that stand on the page, so no PO of the company is ever needed in the repository. The settings tab of the review page writes it from one sample PO, after a trial read of that sample (section 3.17). Its keys:

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `po_label` | The text that stands before the PO number, on the same line: one word, or several words matched as a phrase ("Inkooporder nr."). Required. |
| `date_label`, `version_label`, `currency_label`, `incoterm_label` | The text before the PO's date, its version number, its currency and its delivery terms. Leave a key out, or give `null`, when the PDF does not print the value. Without a version label the versions of a PO are numbered in the order they arrive. |
| `supplier_label` or `supplier_line` | The text before the supplier's name; or, when the name stands alone on a line, the number of that line on page 1, counted from 1 from the top. One of the two, or neither. |
| `title_row` | The words of the row of column titles above the order lines, from left to right, as printed, one word per entry: a title of two words is two entries ("Gewenste", "levering"). |
| `columns` | What each of those words is the title of, one entry per word: `line_id`, `item_code`, `description`, `quantity`, `unit`, `unit_price`, `price_basis`, `discount_pct`, `net_price` or `delivery_date`; `same` for a word that belongs to the title on its left (the second word of "Gewenste levering"); `skip` for a column the aid does not read. One column must be `item_code`, and one must be `line_id` or `quantity`. |
| `table_end` | Optional: a text that stands only on the row after the last order line, best the whole label of the total row ("Totaal exclusief btw"). It is matched as whole words, case not counting, and only on a row that is no order line. That row ends the table on its page, and a later page is read only when it shows the row of column titles again. A page subtotal that holds the text ends that page's table. |
| `decimal_comma` | `true`, the default, for numbers written as 1.234,50; `false` for 1,234.50. |
| `prices_per_piece` | `true` when the PDF has no column for what a price is per and every price is per one unit. The lines then still hold no price basis, so that a confirmation that prints none is compared with them, and only the PO's total, which is held against the authorization limit, counts each line that prints no basis as per one. With `false`, the default, the total of a PO without price bases is unknown, and so it is when a basis is printed and cannot be read. The total is worked out when the PO is filed. |

A layout is refused as any setting at fault is (section 3.2), by its key and never by a word it holds, when: `po_label` is empty; `title_row` and `columns` differ in length or have fewer than two entries; an entry of `title_row` is empty or holds a space (each printed word is an entry of its own, and the second word of a title gets `same`); the first column is `same`; no column is `item_code`; a field stands twice in `columns` (`same` and `skip` may stand as often as needed); there is neither a `line_id` nor a `quantity` column; both `supplier_label` and `supplier_line` are given; or a key is not one of the keys above.

The made-up PO `aid/madeup/tryout/ANDERS_po_v1.pdf` is in a second made-up layout, for trying this out. It is read with:

```json
"po_layout": {
  "po_label": "Bestelnummer",
  "date_label": "Besteldatum",
  "supplier_line": 3,
  "currency_label": "Valuta",
  "incoterm_label": "Leveringsconditie",
  "title_row": ["Hoeveelheid", "Eenh.", "Artikelcode", "Benaming", "Gewenste", "levering", "Stuksprijs", "Bedrag"],
  "columns": ["quantity", "unit", "item_code", "description", "delivery_date", "same", "unit_price", "skip"],
  "decimal_comma": true,
  "prices_per_piece": true
}
```

**How a PO PDF is read with the layout.** A label is found as a word or a phrase of its own, in any case: "Inkooporder" is not found in "Inkoopordernummer", and a label of several words is matched as a whole. A label that stands inside a longer label of the layout at the same place is passed by: with the labels "Order" and "Order date", the line "Order date: 03-11-2026" holds the date label and no PO label. A header value is the text after its label, without the colon, hash or full stop after the label, and up to the next label of the layout on that line, so two labels may share a line. The PO number is the first word of that text when it holds a digit, the version when it is a whole number, and the date when it reads as a date; the PO number and the version lose a comma, semicolon, colon or full stop printed right after them ("Inkooporder 250101," gives 250101). A line where that word is none of these gives nothing, and the next line that holds the label is tried. So a title "Inkooporder  Pagina 1 van 1" above "Inkooporder: 26001234" gives the number 26001234. The supplier, the currency and the delivery terms are the whole text after the first line that holds their label.

Below the row of column titles a row is an order line when, with a line number column, its line number is a whole number and its quantity or its price reads as a number (either is enough), or, in a layout without line numbers, when it has an item code and its quantity is a number; the lines are then numbered 1, 2, 3. So a total row, the second row of a long description and a footer that begins with a postal code are no lines and are not read. A page that does not repeat the column titles continues the table; a page that repeats them is cut by its own titles, and its rows above them are no lines. A number is read with its column also when it is printed flush right under a short title: the word that stands under the title takes the column, and a first word left of the title goes with it when the two are one number printed one space apart, with a space before its thousands ("1 234,50") or with a currency sign before it ("€ 4,20"). A number with a space as its thousands mark whose first part stands under the title on its left is read short: "12 345,00" with its "12" under the end of the title "Eenh." gives the price 345,00 and the unit "st 12". The trial read on the page (section 3.17) shows it. Two cells that the PDF prints close together, as a price that ends 2 points before its basis, are read as two cells: a word of the text layer is cut where the gap between two of its characters is wider than the spacing of its letters. A PDF in which the PO label is followed by two different PO numbers, as a batch print of two POs, is refused; a page header that repeats the same number on every page is fine.

**Which PDF is a PO.** With `po_layout` set, a PDF fits the layout when its first page holds the whole row of column titles, word for word, and the PO label with a value after it. A PDF that fits is filed as a PO whatever else its first page says below its column titles, so a PO that asks its supplier for an "orderbevestiging" under its lines is still a PO, and it is filed also when a case already holds the same file. Two exceptions keep a supplier's document out of the PO store. A PDF whose header, the lines of its first page above the column titles, holds a confirmation word anywhere ("Orderbevestiging", "Order confirmation", "Purchase order confirmation", "Auftragsbestätigung" and the like) is classed by the keywords as before, although it fits: so is a confirmation whose title stands below the supplier's letterhead, and so is a PO of the company whose header holds such a word, which is then taken for a confirmation. And when `po_senders` is filled, a PDF that fits and came in an e-mail from a sender that is not on the list is handled as any confirmation is, also when its title says it is a PO: a supplier may answer with the PO itself. A sender is on the list by its address or by its domain; a file put into `inbox/` on its own, as one given on the tab "Bestanden" is (section 2.7), has no sender and passes. A PDF that does not fit is classed by the keywords as before. What is left: without `po_senders`, or from a listed sender, a supplier's document that repeats the PO's column titles and its label and has no confirmation word in its header is filed as a version of the PO, unless it prints a version number that the store already holds from another file, which the store refuses. So fill `po_senders` whenever `po_layout` is set.

With a layout set the made-up layout is no longer read: a PO PDF is read with the layout or refused. A PO PDF that is refused stays in `inbox/` and is listed in the digest under "Overgeslagen" with its reason: "does not fit the purchase order layout" (the first page lacks the row of column titles, or the PO label with a value after it), "no PO number found" (on no line is the PO label followed by a word with a digit), "more than one PO number" (two different numbers after the PO label), "cannot be the name of a folder" (a PO number that cannot name a folder of the PO store on every computer: a slash or another sign a folder name cannot hold, more than 100 characters, a device name of Windows; section 6 gives the rule, which holds for a PO PDF as for an export), "is stored for its PO from another file" (a version number that the store holds from another PDF) or "no order line found". The reasons name the setting and never a value of the PDF. It is tried again at every pass for 30 days, so once the layout is right within that time it is filed by itself; after 30 days it moves to `inbox/processed/` and must be given again on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7). A PDF that was skipped as "other" has moved to `inbox/processed/`: give it again on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7).

What a layout cannot describe: values that stand under their labels and not beside them, a table without a row of column titles, and a PDF that is a picture (no text layer). Two cells of two characters in all, such as a "1" and a "5" printed close together, stay one word.

**Check a reading before the layout is used.** A layout can read a PO without refusing it and still read it wrong, so compare the reading of a sample PO with the PO itself. The trial read on the settings tab does so (section 3.17): "Testen" shows the header values and the order lines as the layout reads them, with a line for each problem and each warning of the reader, and writes nothing; "Opslaan" refuses a layout that cannot file the sample. Without the page, the reading of a filed PO is in `po_store/<PO>/v<n>/po.json`: the header values with the line each was read from, and each order line with the printed text and the value of every cell. To try a layout that way without touching the real store:

1. Copy `settings.json` into an empty scratch folder. In the copy set `po_store_root` to `"po_store"` and `cases_root` to `"cases"` (they then lie in the scratch folder) and `po_layout` to the layout to try.
2. Put one sample PO into a folder `sample` there, under a name that ends in `_po_v1.pdf`, for example `sample/sample_po_v1.pdf`.
3. Write a file `cases.csv` there that holds only its header row: `case_id,document,po_number,kind`.
4. In the scratch folder run `python -m aid.batch --cases cases.csv --po-dir sample --settings settings.json --reader parser --out summary.csv`. A refused PO stops the command with its reason on the last line; otherwise read `po_store/<PO>/v1/po.json`.

Look first at the PO number. It is the word right after the PO label, on the first line where that word holds a digit, so watch for two things. An earlier line that holds the label too: it is passed by when the word after the label holds no digit (a title "Inkooporder  Pagina 1 van 1", an address "PO Box 61"); when that word holds a digit (a title "Inkooporder 1/2" with the page right after it), the PDF is refused as holding more than one PO number, and a longer label that the title does not hold, such as "Inkooporder:", reads it. And a word between the label and the number: "Inkooporder nr. 26001234" needs the label "Inkooporder nr.", or no PO number is found.

### 3.13 The capture server

The capture server answers the browser extension (section 2.5). It runs beside the runner and the review page, on the computer where the buyer's browser runs. It reads every cart with the cart reader and asks no model, also with `"reader": "llm"`: the pre-check stays fast, needs no Copilot sign-in, and what the rules cannot settle goes to the buyer.

```bash
python3 -m aid.capture_server --settings <root>/settings.json [--host 127.0.0.1] [--port 7870] [--testshop]
```

- `--settings` (required): the settings file, the same one the runner uses, so that the cases land under the same `cases_root`.
- `--host`: the address it listens on, default 127.0.0.1 (this computer only). Leave it so.
- `--port`: default 7870.
- `--testshop`: also serve the made-up cart pages of `aid/madeup/out` under `/testshop/` (for example `http://127.0.0.1:7870/testshop/CART02.html`), for trying it out with the demo's settings. Leave it out on the company side.

It prints the address it listens on and runs until Ctrl-C. How it is kept running is the operator's choice, as for the runner (section 3.4).

**The PO side.** The server looks for a cart's PO only in the PO store, and it files nothing itself. For a cart the PO side is an export of the order lines, because no PO PDF exists before ordering. Save the export from Exact and give it on the tab "Bestanden" of the review page (section 2.7), or e-mail it so that it reaches the folder of the work mailbox that the runner reads (section 3.14): with `export_columns` set (sections 3.2 and 3.17) the runner files it at its next pass, within a minute, before the PO PDFs and the confirmations of that pass, and moves it to `inbox/processed/`. Each PO of the export becomes a look at that PO, kept beside its PDF versions (section 6, "The export of PO lines"). A cart is checked against its PO as it stands: its latest look when that look was filed after the latest PO PDF, else the PDF. An export whose rows for a PO are those of the PO's latest look files nothing for it, and so does not count as later: the PDF filed after that look keeps standing. An export file is filed once: the same file again, or a file that stays in `inbox/` and is handled again at every pass (one held open in Excel, or an e-mail with another attachment the aid cannot handle yet), files nothing more, also after a newer export. Of several exports that wait for the same pass, the one whose file was saved last (for an attachment, its e-mail) is filed last and stands, whatever their names. While no stored PO fits the cart (the next paragraph), the banner is grey ("Geen inkooporder gevonden", with the line that says to give the export and to reload the page) and the case opens as `unknown_<...>`; while the cart's own PO is not in the store yet and another stored PO fits its items, the cart is compared with that PO, as a guess with nothing prepared, unless that PO is the one order filed that day with exactly the cart's items (section 4.9 names that state).

**Which PO a cart is for.** The number that the page states, when the store knows that PO, from a PDF or from an export, whatever items it shares with the cart; the case is then opened as for any cart. A number the page states that the store does not know decides nothing. Otherwise the items decide, by three words. A PO is *sent* when the store holds a PDF version of it, or when a supplier's confirmation of it came in: a case that is no cart's and no try-out's holds its number, found by the number recognition (no `po_found_by` in its `case.json`), and is not failed; such a case is looked for by the PO number in the name of its folder. A PO was *filed today* when the PO as it stands was filed on the calendar day of the capture, by the server's clock (the time of its `po.json` in the store). A PO *holds exactly the cart's items* when the set of its item codes equals the cart's and neither has a line without an item code. The server is sure by the items when exactly one stored PO that was filed today holds exactly the cart's items, and that PO is not sent: the case is opened for it as for a stated number, its `capture.json` holds `"po_found_by": "cart items"`, and the banner's summary names the order with "(gekozen op de artikelen in de winkelwagen)". One item code is enough for that. Every other choice is a guess, when at least one stored PO fits: the candidates are the POs that share at least two item codes with the cart and those that hold exactly the cart's items, each as it stands. The cart is compared with one of them, chosen in this order: first those filed today, when there are any; then those that hold exactly the cart's items; then those that are not sent; then those of the page's supplier (the first heading of the page against the supplier of the PO); then the one whose PO as it stands was filed last, the later number among orders filed at the same time. The guess is `"cart items of an order already sent"` when that PO is sent and `"cart items, not sure"` when it is not. The case holds which in its `case.json` (`po_found_by`), as a confirmation whose order was guessed does, so the pipeline prepares and asks nothing and every difference is a review item with the reason "PO chosen by the items in the cart" (rule G01 in the audit record; section 2.4); the banner is amber and its first line says which of the two it was (section 2.5). A cart that no stored PO fits has no order. The supplier never makes the server sure: a webshop's heading seldom equals the supplier's name in Exact.

**A cart that is checked again.** An earlier case answers a page that comes again: a case that holds the same page, of any day, or a capture case of the same day that holds the same cart. A case that holds the page itself and stands answers at once: the cart is not read, and no later case of the same cart is looked at. Otherwise, of the cases of the page and of the cart together, the one opened last decides. A case stands when its order was found (by its number, or by the items as the one order of today), and when it is no case of the capture server. For a case without a PO, and for a case whose order was a guess, whatever the buyer did on it, the rule of the paragraph above is asked again, and the case answers only while the order found now is its own: a case without a PO while no order is found, a guess while no order is found or the same order is still the guess. A guess that answers so gives the first line of the kind found now, "not sure" or "already sent", not of the kind saved with the case, and nothing of the case is written. When another order is found or guessed now, or the guess's own order is now found for sure, the server opens a new case, at least one second after the earlier one so that the new case is the later of the two, runs it, and then finishes as replaced the earlier cases that may be: a guess on which nothing was done, and a case without a PO while its result is in review, whatever was decided in it. A guess on which something was done is never replaced and never changed: it stays on the review page as the buyer left it, and the new case answers from then on. Something was done on a case when it holds a decision of the buyer, a line in its write log, a message sent, an undo, or a close that closed it or carried something out; a press of the close button that left the case in review and carried nothing out is no act. A case without a PO that the buyer closed is left exactly as it is.

When the new case has a PO, the cases of the same lines go with them. `capture.json` keeps two digests: that of the cart, which holds the number the page states, and `lines_digest`, that of the lines alone (and of the mark of a try-out cart, so that a try-out cart and a webshop's cart of the same lines are never mixed). The server reads the `capture.json` of every capture case once and finishes as replaced the cases of any day that hold the same lines digest, may still be replaced as above, and were not opened after the new case, by the times in their ids. A case whose run failed is never among them: it keeps its failed result. So the guess of a cart before the buyer typed the order's number into it leaves the review page when the cart with the number finds its order, and so does the case without a PO of that cart, and yesterday's untouched guess or case without a PO when the same cart opens a case today. A `capture.json` written before the lines digest was kept is matched by its cart digest. This search is made only when a new case with a PO is opened, not at every capture; which case answers a page or a cart is not changed by it.

A replacement has two steps: first `replaced_by`, the id of the new case, is written into the earlier case's `capture.json`, and then its result is closed (without a supplier) and the replacement recorded in its `steps/09_close.json` and its audit record, as the runner does for a confirmation that it checks again (section 3.17). From the mark on the case answers no capture, is in no digest and is not offered on the review page, and a decision, a close and an undo on it are refused. Right after the mark the earlier case is read again: when the buyer acted on it just before the mark, the mark is taken back and the case is left as it is. The review page is another process and the two share no lock, so each side looks twice: a decision is appended and the mark looked at again, and the line is taken out and the decision refused when the case was marked meanwhile; a close looks at the mark again right before it writes its result, and writes nothing when it is there. A new case whose own run failed replaces nothing. A replacement that was cut off at any point after the mark is finished, never undone: the earlier case stays off the review page and out of every digest until the next reading finishes it, read again first in the same way, by the next capture that meets it, which is the same cart in a page of other bytes on that day or the earlier case's own page, and at the latest when the server starts. That reading also finishes a case whose closed result was written while its record was not (a marked case without a PO whose result is closed while no close of the buyer closed it is such a cut-off, not a case the buyer closed; a press of the close button that left the case in review closed nothing), and finishes again a case whose result a close of the review page wrote over in the instant of the mark. The page of the capture that was cut off does not finish the case when that page came in other bytes than the earlier case's own page: the new case holds those bytes and answers at once. A replacement cut off before the mark is made by the next capture that meets the earlier case: its own page, the same cart on its day, or the next new case with a PO of the same lines, as on a later day. Until then the case stays on the review page, where the buyer can close it.

**The try-out shop.** While the test mode is on and no authorization limit is set, the server serves the try-out carts that the settings tab makes (section 3.17) under `/tryout/`, for example `http://127.0.0.1:7870/tryout/250108_price_small.html`, with or without `--testshop`: the files of `cases/tryout/`, by their name alone, and to this PC's own host only (a request whose `Host` is 127.0.0.1 or localhost, with any port, as the review page asks; any other host gets 404). It reads the settings file again first, so the switch of the test mode counts at once. Outside the test mode, or with a limit set, the address answers 404, and a try-out cart page that the extension still sends (a tab left open) is refused: the red banner "Inkoopassistent weigert de pagina" shows "Dit is een proefwinkelwagen: de assistent controleert hem alleen in de proefstand.", in the language of the settings, and `capture.log` gets the line "403 POST /capture: try-out document outside the test mode". No case is opened and no copy of the page is kept, as the runner skips a try-out confirmation then.

**What the export may look like.** An overview of the order lines saved from the ERP as an Excel workbook (.xlsx) or as CSV, with one row per order line. Its columns need not have the aid's names or order: `export_columns` says which column title holds the PO number, the item code and the other values, `export_decimal_comma` how the file writes its numbers, and `export_fill_down` whether the PO number stands only once for each PO (section 3.2); part 2 of the block "Koppeling met Exact (bij de installatie)" on the settings tab sets the three from one sample export, with a trial read (section 3.17). A CSV file may be cut at semicolons, commas or tabs: the aid takes the mark under which it finds the row of column titles. Rows that are no order line are passed over: rows above the column titles, blank rows, the column titles repeated lower down, rows without an item code (totals, group rows, text lines) and, when the map names the quantity column, rows with an item code and no quantity, such as a total that prints its word in the item code column. With `export_fill_down` a line without a PO number belongs to the PO whose number stands nearest above it, also when that number stands on a group row or a text line. Section 6, "The export of PO lines", gives the rules and what makes the aid refuse a file. The runner takes a `.xlsx` or `.csv` file as an export when `export_columns` is set and one of the file's rows holds every column title of the map. While `export_columns` is set, any other `.xlsx` or `.csv` file is skipped as "spreadsheet without the export's column titles" and moved to `inbox/processed/`: a price list, an export with a column title the map does not know or lacks, and a file the aid cannot read as a table (a damaged workbook, a CSV file in an encoding the aid does not read). The digest lists it under "Overgeslagen" with that reason, and the tab "Bestanden" as "Een rekenblad zonder de kolomtitels van de export: niets gedaan"; check the column titles of the map against the file's (section 3.2) and give the file again on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7). With `export_columns` empty every spreadsheet is skipped as "unsupported file type". A file that the runner takes as an export and the aid then refuses stays in `inbox/`, is listed in the digest under "Overgeslagen" with its reason, which names the row or the column and never a value, and is tried again at every pass, for 30 days: after that the runner moves it to `inbox/processed/` and tries it no more (section 2.7). The refusals: a title of the map that stands twice in the title row, and the refusals of the rows of section 6 (a row with an item code and no PO number without `export_fill_down`, a PO number that is a date, holds no digit or cannot be a folder name, a line number that is blank or stands twice). An export that holds the column titles and no order line (an overview exported with a filter that matches nothing) files nothing: the digest lists it under "Overgeslagen" as "export without order lines", the tab "Bestanden" says "Export uit Exact zonder orderregels: niets gedaan", and it moves to `inbox/processed/`.

**The digest.** The server runs its cases itself and never writes the runner's digest state. The runner, at each pass on the same `cases_root`, reports every case of the capture server that has its result once, so that it appears in the next digest under "voorcontrole". A capture case that was replaced (its `capture.json` holds `replaced_by`, or, while that file cannot be read, its close records hold the replacement) is not reported, and leaves the cases of the next digest when it was among them: the digest names the case that replaced it. Without a runner on that folder, capture cases are in no digest.

**A case in progress.** While the server runs a case, a new one or one it resumes because an earlier run of it was cut off, the case folder holds a file `.running` (the process id and the start). The runner leaves such a case alone, checking with the time of the check itself, and a start up to ten minutes ahead of its own clock counts as running too (the two processes' clocks may differ); a `.running` file older than ten minutes is a run that crashed, and the runner resumes the case as it resumes any case without a result.

**The token.** Set `capture_token` in `settings.json` to a long random value and put the same value in the extension's options. The extension sends it with every page; a request without it, or with another value, is refused. The value is company-specific and never goes into a repository. The extension keeps it in the browser profile's local extension storage on that computer. The settings tab of the review page shows it, read-only and with a button to copy it, at the end of the part "Koppelingen" as "Code voor de uitbreiding in de browser", on the page of this PC only: a page opened with `--share`, or without its settings file, does not show it. The first start of the folder of section 3.16 writes a random token of 43 characters.

**The extension's options.** The address of the server (default `http://127.0.0.1:7870`; change it only with `--host` or `--port`), the token, and the webshops, one address per line. Saving asks the browser for access to those shops; a shop taken off the list loses that access. An address on `http://127.0.0.1` or `http://localhost` needs no entry: the test shop is always on, and the options page says so. Open tabs of a shop just added need a reload. After an update of the aid, reload the extension on `chrome://extensions` (or `edge://extensions`, its reload button) and then the open tabs of the shops: a browser keeps the extension's script as it was loaded until then, and shows what an older script shows, for a cart of whose order the aid is not sure the usual amber title.

**What the process needs.** The server builds its writer and mailer as the runner does: its writer is the prepared writer, as every command's is, so it needs no Exact token (`AID_EXACT_TOKEN` is not used, and a settings file in which `exact_base_url` is set stops the server at its start, section 3.10), and when the settings set SMTP, `AID_MAIL_PASSWORD` must be in its environment, exactly as for the runner. The banner's link is `review_url` with the case, and the review page listens on 127.0.0.1 of its own computer only: start the review page (section 3.7) on the computer where the browser and the capture server run and, when it printed another port, set `review_url` to that address.

**What it logs and keeps.** Each page sent is saved under `cases/captures/` (a page answered by an earlier case is not kept twice, and a page whose pre-check failed before a case was opened is removed). The extension empties in the copy every password field and every field whose name, id or autocomplete holds, in any case and also with its punctuation removed, one of: pass, wachtwoord, card, kaart, cc-, ccnum, cardnumber, creditcard, cvc, cvv, csc, securitycode, iban, rekening. A select so matched has no option chosen in the copy. A field named otherwise keeps its typed value in the copy. Errors go to `cases/capture.log`, one line per capture to the console (section 3.6). A page larger than 5 MB is refused.

**Exit code 2.** The server does not start, and ends with exit code 2 and a line "aid.capture_server: ...", when the settings file cannot be read or is not valid, when `capture_token` is empty, when the writer refuses the Exact settings, when the port is outside 0 to 65535, or when the address cannot be used (the port is taken by another program, for example a capture server that still runs).

### 3.14 Connecting the work mailbox

Instead of a mailbox rule (section 3.3), the aid can read the work mailbox itself. After a sign-in with the buyer's own Microsoft work account on the settings tab of the review page, the runner reads one folder of that mailbox at every pass, through Microsoft Graph, and saves each new e-mail as an `.eml` file into `inbox/`, before it handles the inbox: a confirmation that arrives opens its case in the same pass. The messages that are in the folder when it is connected are left alone; every message that comes into the folder after that is read, also one that an Outlook rule or a drag moves there, whenever it was received. One limit: when the reading was broken for a while (the runner stopped for days, so that Microsoft no longer knows where the aid left off), the aid reads again what was received since its last read, and an older message moved into the folder while the reading was broken is not read; move it out of the folder and in again. The runner only reads the folder: it never moves, flags or deletes a message. The aid's own e-mails that come back into the folder (the digest, the buyer's copy of a question) are passed over. Without a sign-in everything works as before.

**What must reach the folder.** The folder takes the place of the rule of section 3.3, so it must receive the same mail: the e-mails with Exact's PO PDFs, the suppliers' e-mails with their confirmations, and the replies to the aid's questions. A reply arrives in the inbox of the mailbox the question was sent from, so give that mailbox an Outlook rule that moves it into the folder, for example on the subject words "Vraag over orderbevestiging PO" (in English "Question about order confirmation PO"), which a reply keeps after its "RE:". With "Verzenden via" on the SMTP server, the questions go out from the aid's own mailbox (`aid_email`) and the replies arrive there; an Outlook rule cannot move mail into another mailbox, so let that mailbox forward the replies to the work mailbox, or keep the mailbox rule of section 3.3 for it. For the rest, use rules on the sender of Exact's PO e-mails and on the suppliers' sender addresses or domains, or choose "Postvak IN" itself as the folder. Subject words such as "orderbevestiging" or "order confirmation" are examples only: suppliers also write "Opdrachtbevestiging", "Auftragsbestätigung" or "Order acknowledgement", or answer the PO e-mail with the confirmation attached. An e-mail that never reaches the folder is never checked, and nothing reports it; a reply that never reaches it is not filed, and its case waits until its wait ends.

**The app registration.** The sign-in needs an app registration in Microsoft Entra ID. A user can make one in a few minutes when the company allows users to register applications:

1. Open entra.microsoft.com and sign in with the work account.
2. Open App registrations and choose New registration.
3. Name it "Inkoopassistent", choose "Accounts in this organizational directory only" and register it.
4. Under Authentication, add the platform "Mobile and desktop applications" with the redirect URI `http://localhost`, and set "Allow public client flows" to Yes. Save.
5. Under API permissions, add Microsoft Graph, Delegated permissions: `Mail.Read`, `Mail.ReadWrite`, `Mail.Send` and `User.Read`, and, to read and send from another mailbox than your own (below), also `Mail.Read.Shared`, `Mail.ReadWrite.Shared` and `Mail.Send.Shared`. `Mail.ReadWrite` lets the aid create the draft that each of its messages is sent from (Microsoft does not say that a message sent at once keeps the aid's Message-ID, by which a reply is filed); it changes no other message.
6. On the Overview, copy the Directory (tenant) ID and the Application (client) ID into the settings tab. A portal in Dutch calls them "Map-id (tenant)" and "Toepassings-id (client)", as the tab does.

No client secret and no certificate is made: the app is a public client, and the person signs in with their own account. The two IDs are identifiers, not secrets.

**When the portal refuses.** If New registration is greyed out or refused, the account may not register applications: the company decides that, with a setting of Entra ID. Ask IT to make the registration with the settings above, or keep to the mailbox rule of section 3.3. If the sign-in asks for an administrator's approval of the permissions, the company does not let users approve apps themselves: an administrator approves them once.

**Connecting.** Start the review page on the PC where the runner runs (section 3.7), as the same Windows user as the runner (below), and open the tab "Instellingen". The fields and buttons of the connection are at the bottom of that tab, folded away under "Koppelingen: het Microsoft-postvak en de AI-dienst": a click on that heading opens the part. Everything this section names on the tab is inside it.

1. Fill in the Map-id (tenant) and the Toepassings-id (client).
2. Press "Aanmelden bij Microsoft". A browser window with Microsoft's sign-in page opens on the PC where the page runs: sign in with the work account and accept the permissions. The tab waits for it for at most five minutes. If no window opens, press "Aanmelden met een code": the tab shows an address and a code; open the address on any device, enter the code and sign in, and the tab waits until that is done.
3. The tab shows "Aangemeld als" with the account, and the folders of the mailbox. Only the folders at the top of the mailbox are listed, beside "Postvak IN", not the folders inside them.
4. Leave "Postvak" empty for your own mailbox.
5. Choose the folder and press "Verbinding testen": the tab shows how many messages the folder holds and when the newest arrived, never a subject or a sender.
6. Leave "Verzenden via" on "Automatisch", which sends each message through the mailbox while you are signed in and a folder is chosen, and through the SMTP server or as files when no folder is chosen or no one is signed in (after "Afmelden"); or choose another way. While the sign-in has lapsed, nothing is sent (see "Sending").
7. Press "Opslaan". The six settings are written into the settings file, every other setting kept as it was, and the runner takes them over at its next pass: "Opgeslagen. De assistent gebruikt dit vanaf de volgende ronde, binnen een minuut." Under "Stand van de controle" the tab shows "Nog niet verbonden" until the runner's next pass has connected the folder, then since when the folder is connected, when it was last read and how many messages were saved, or "nog niet gelezen" when that pass could not read the folder (a sign-in to repeat, or a folder the account may not read yet). The line under the sign-in buttons shows who is signed in, read from the PC without asking Microsoft; whether the sign-in still works shows when you press "Verbinding testen" or "Mappen ophalen": "Aanmelding van ... verlopen" if it has lapsed.

**Sending.** With "Automatisch" (once signed in with a folder chosen) or "Het postvak van de aanmelding", the runner's supplier questions, re-approval requests and digest go out from that mailbox, From its address. Each message is first made as a draft in the mailbox and then sent, so that it ends in Sent Items; a draft whose sending failed may stay in the Drafts folder. Who is signed in is looked at for each message, so a sign-in on the tab counts from the next message on, without a restart. A question asked on the review page, and the request to the approver that a close makes, go out in the same way when the case is closed: the page makes its mailer from the same settings and the same sign-in (section 3.7), so the sign-in on this tab is all it takes, and the reply reaches the folder as a reply to the runner's own question does. While the sign-in has lapsed (the account is still on the PC, but Microsoft no longer accepts it), the supplier questions and re-approval requests are not sent: each case of the runner comes to you on the review page with "Bericht niet verzonden", and a close on the page says "Een bericht is niet verzonden; de zaak blijft ter beoordeling." and records nothing as asked; sign in again and close that case again. No other way is tried, since a reply to another sender would never reach the folder and so never its case. The digest is then written into `cases/outbox/` (section 3.8). Only after "Afmelden" do the messages under "Automatisch" go through SMTP or as files.

**A test with your own mailbox.** Make a folder for the test at the top of your own mailbox (beside "Postvak IN", not inside it), choose it on the tab and save. After the next pass, drag a made-up confirmation e-mail into the folder, or let an Outlook rule move it there. At the next pass (within a minute) it is saved into `inbox/` and its case opens. Use another made-up confirmation for each test: a document the aid has handled once is skipped as "duplicate". To test a reply, answer a question the aid sent to your own address and let the reply reach the folder (see "What must reach the folder").

**Another mailbox.** To read another mailbox, such as the purchasing department's, fill in its address under "Postvak", press "Mappen ophalen", choose its folder and save; the tab refuses a folder listed for another mailbox than the one in the field. Another mailbox or folder starts a new connection from that moment, and the aid sends from that mailbox too. This needs two things: the app registration's shared permissions of step 5, which the aid asks for when a mailbox is filled in (sign in again after filling it in), and the signed-in account's access in Exchange to read that mailbox and to send from it, which IT grants.

**Where the tokens are kept.** The sign-in's tokens are kept on the PC only: on Windows in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Inkoopassistent`, encrypted with Windows' data protection (DPAPI), so that only this Windows user on this PC can read them; elsewhere in `~/.config/inkoopassistent`, encrypted where the system offers a keychain, else in files that only their owner can read. The review page and the runner share them, so both must run as the same Windows user: a runner started as a service account or as SYSTEM looks in another folder, cannot read the page's tokens, and keeps reporting "Postvak niet verbonden" however often the buyer signs in. Copilot (section 3.15) uses the same tokens, one store per Windows user: such a runner cannot ask Copilot either, every case waits with `SignInNeeded`, and the digest says "Copilot werkt niet".
