Choose "Inkooporder" for the purchase order number, "(staat niet in het bestand)" for "Regelnummer", "Artikel", "Omschrijving", "Aantal", "Eenheid", "Prijs" and "Per" for the next six fields, "(staat niet in het bestand)" for "Korting in procenten" and "Nettoprijs", "Leverdatum" for the delivery date and "Leverancier" for the supplier; then "1.234,50 (komma voor de decimalen)" and the tick. "Testen" says "Gelezen: 2 inkooporders met samen 5 regels; 1 rij zonder artikelcode overgeslagen. Hieronder de eerste en de laatste inkooporder: vergelijk ze met Exact." and shows the three lines of purchase order 250301 of supplier S21, the first with "1000 (1.000)", "pc (st)", "12.5 (12,50)", "100" and "2026-11-24 (24-11-2026)", and the two lines of purchase order 250302 of supplier S22; the row passed over is the total row. With "1,234.50 (punt voor de decimalen)" instead, that line shows "1 (1.000)" and the test names the column "Prijs" with the choice that fits; without the tick it says "Rij 5: een artikelcode zonder inkoopordernummer. ...". "Opslaan" writes the map that section 6 gives for this file. Then copy `aid/madeup/tryout/EXPORT_nl.csv` into `live/inbox/` and let the runner make one pass, `python3 -m aid.runner --settings live/settings.json --max-passes 1`: the file moves to `live/inbox/processed/`, the two purchase orders are filed as looks in `live/po_store/250301/e1/` and `live/po_store/250302/e1/`, and after "Stand vernieuwen" the state says "Laatste export opgenomen:" with the time.

**A test walk of part 2 on the test account.** On the work laptop, enter an order in the test Exact without sending it, make the export of the order lines from the overview of check A11, and save it where the laptop's file window can open it (check A13). Choose it as the sample, set the columns as above and press "Testen". Compare three lines of the first and the last order with Exact's screen, and report in words every line the block shows above the table: the line "Gelezen: ...", the line on rows with an item code but no quantity, each line "Niet gelezen" and each problem (check B4). If all values agree, press "Opslaan"; give the same export on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7), or e-mail it to the folder the runner reads, and report the line of the tab "Bestanden" and the line "Laatste export opgenomen" under "Stand van de koppeling" in words (check B4).

**Part 3, "3. Inkoopprijzen van artikelen als export uit Exact (voor de prijslijst)".** "Kies een voorbeeld van de export van artikelen uit Exact en wijs de kolommen aan. Heeft de export geen kolom met de leverancier, dan kiest u de leverancier wanneer u de export op het tabblad Prijslijst geeft." A supplier's price list is compared with the purchase prices of that supplier's articles in Exact, which the buyer gives as an export on the tab "Prijslijst" (section 4.3). Part 3 writes the two settings of that export, `article_columns` and `article_decimal_comma` (section 3.2), from one sample: an export of one supplier's articles that you save from Exact (checks A15 and A16), an `.xlsx` or `.csv` file. It stores no price: the export itself is given on the tab "Prijslijst", each time a price list comes in.

1. Choose the file under "Voorbeeld: een export van artikelen uit Exact". As in part 2, the block shows "De eerste rijen van het voorbeeld" and fills "Rij met de kolomnamen"; a file of another type, a damaged file and one that cannot be cut into columns are named at once, by the sentences of part 2. Of an Excel file part 3 reads one sheet, the one you see, as the tab "Prijslijst" reads the export itself: a hidden sheet is never read, the one visible sheet that holds data is read wherever it stands, and a file with more than one such sheet is refused, with the names of those sheets: "Dit Excel-bestand heeft meer dan één werkblad met gegevens (Slangen, Klemmen). De assistent leest één werkblad: sla het werkblad dat u bedoelt op als een eigen bestand en geef dat."
2. Choose the column for "Artikelcode (verplicht)" and for "Inkoopprijs (verplicht)", the purchase price per one unit. Choose a column for "Leverancier" only when the export names the supplier of each row; with "(staat niet in het bestand)" an export holds the articles of one supplier, whom you choose when you give it on the tab "Prijslijst".
3. Under "Getallen die als tekst in het bestand staan", choose how the file writes a number as text, as in part 2.
4. Press "Testen". It writes nothing. The block says what it read: "12 artikelen gelezen; 3 rijen overgeslagen (zonder artikelcode: 2, prijs niet te lezen: 1).", and for one of each "1 artikel gelezen; 1 rij overgeslagen (prijs niet te lezen: 1)."; with no row passed over there are no brackets. The brackets name each reason for which rows were passed over, with how many: "zonder artikelcode" (a total or a text line), "zonder leverancier" (with a supplier column, a row whose supplier cell is empty), "prijs niet te lezen" (an empty price, or a text that is no number) and "artikel dubbel" (an article that two rows list for one supplier: both rows are passed over, since it is not clear which price holds). Under the line a table shows the first 15 articles as read, under "Rij", "Artikelcode", "Inkoopprijs" and, with a supplier column, "Leverancier". A price stands as the aid will keep it, in plain digits with a point for the decimals, with the text of the cell in brackets when the two differ: "12.5 (12,50)".
5. Compare a few rows with Exact's screen, and press "Opslaan": "Opgeslagen."

A problem refuses "Opslaan" ("Niet opgeslagen: ..."): each problem is named in the page's language and nothing is written. The problems are: no sample ("Kies eerst een voorbeeld."); no column for the item code or for the purchase price ("Kies de kolom met de artikelcode.", "Kies de kolom met de inkoopprijs."); one column chosen for more than one field; a file that the reader refuses, by the sentences of part 2, a workbook with more than one visible sheet with data (the sentence quoted under step 1) a CSV row that is cut otherwise than the row of column titles and a workbook whose only data stands on a hidden sheet among them; a price column whose text shows the other decimal mark than the one chosen, named with the choice that fits, as in part 2; and a sample that gives no article ("Het voorbeeld gaf geen artikel: geen rij onder de kolomnamen heeft een artikelcode en een prijs die te lezen is."). A save from a form that another browser tab overtook is refused as in part 2. "Opslaan" writes `article_columns` and `article_decimal_comma` and nothing else, and the tab "Prijslijst" opens the next time it is selected. While the columns are set, the review page's `--share` is refused, as while the columns of part 2 are set: after the walk below, the folder `live` no longer opens with `--share`. The sample stays on this PC as the samples of parts 1 and 2 do, and it shows real articles and prices of the company: set the columns up at the PC itself, not on a shared screen.

**A test walk of the price list with two made-up files.** On the own PC, in the folder `live` of the worked example above (`python3 -m aid.demo --out live --prepare-only`, then `python3 -m aid.review --settings live/settings.json`), make two files in a text editor and save them as `.csv`. The export of the articles of supplier S01, `artikelen S01.csv`:

```
Artikelen van leverancier S01 (verzonnen voorbeeld)

Artikel;Omschrijving;Inkoopprijs
KM-1001;Slang 10 mm;12,50
KM-1002;Slangklem;8,00
KM-1003;O-ring;0,45
KM-1004;Koppeling;op aanvraag
```

The price list of that supplier, `prijslijst S01.csv`:

```
Artikelnummer;Omschrijving;Nettoprijs;Per;Geldig tot
KM-1001;Slang 10 mm;12,70;1;
KM-1002;Slangklem;8,00;1;
KM-1003;O-ring;0,48;1;
KM-1003;O-ring;40,00;100;
KM-9999;Nieuw artikel;4,10;1;
KM-1002;Slangklem;7,90;1;30-06-2026
```

1. In part 3, choose `artikelen S01.csv`: "Rij met de kolomnamen" shows 3. Choose "Artikel" for "Artikelcode (verplicht)", "Inkoopprijs" for "Inkoopprijs (verplicht)", "(staat niet in het bestand)" for "Leverancier" and "1.234,50 (komma voor de decimalen)". "Testen" says "3 artikelen gelezen; 1 rij overgeslagen (prijs niet te lezen: 1)."; press "Opslaan".
2. Select the tab "Prijslijst", type S01 under "Leverancier" and press Enter: "Van S01 zijn nog geen inkoopprijzen opgenomen. Geef eerst de export van de artikelen van deze leverancier uit Exact." Choose `artikelen S01.csv` under "Export van de artikelen uit Exact (Excel of csv)" and press "Inkoopprijzen opnemen": "Inkoopprijzen van S01 opgenomen: 3 artikelen; 1 rij overgeslagen."
3. Choose `prijslijst S01.csv` under "Prijslijst (Excel of csv)". Choose "Artikelnummer" under "Kolom met de artikelcode", "Nettoprijs" under "Kolom met de prijs", "Per" under "Kolom met de prijs per (mag leeg)" and "Geldig tot" under "Geldig tot (mag leeg)", and press "Bekijken". The table shows six rows: KM-1001 with "prijs verandert" (12,50 and 12,70), KM-1002 with "ongewijzigd", KM-1003 with "prijs verandert" (0,45 and 0,48), KM-1003 with "prijs per ander aantal: niet vergeleken", KM-9999 with "artikel niet bekend in Exact" and KM-1002 with "verlopen". Under it: "2 prijzen veranderen, 1 blijft gelijk, 3 rijen overgeslagen. Sommige rijen zijn verlopen: vraag de leverancier om een actuele lijst."
4. Press "Op de lijst In Exact invoeren zetten": "2 prijswijzigingen staan op de lijst In Exact invoeren." The tab "In Exact invoeren" shows "artikel KM-1001: inkoopprijs 12,50 → 12,70" and "artikel KM-1003: inkoopprijs 0,45 → 0,48". Tick the first and press "Aangevinkte regels zijn ingevoerd". Back on the tab "Prijslijst", "Bekijken" now shows KM-1001 with "ongewijzigd" and 12,70 as its purchase price in Exact: "1 prijs verandert, 2 blijven gelijk, 3 rijen overgeslagen." On the tab "In Exact invoeren", choose the list under "Prijslijst" and press "Alle artikelprijzen van deze prijslijst zijn ingevoerd" for the rest.

Report in words the line of "Testen", the line after "Inkoopprijzen opnemen" and the count line under the table. Then try it on the laptop with one export of a supplier's articles from the test Exact and a price list that you make yourself from five of its articles: whether Exact exports a supplier's articles with their purchase price, and whether that export has a column with the supplier, is what this walk finds out (checks A15 and A16).

**A test walk with a try-out.** On the work laptop, with the test account of Exact, the test mode on (the first start of the folder sets it, section 3.16) and no authorization limit in the settings file (check B3):

1. Send one purchase order from the test Exact to your own mailbox, or give its PDF on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7). After the next pass that tab says "Inkooporder ... opgenomen (versie 1)" with its number, and "Stand van de koppeling" names it: "Laatst opgenomen: inkooporder" with its number.
2. In "Proefdraaien met een inkooporder uit Exact", choose it under "Inkooporder": the list is filled when the tab "Instellingen" is selected and with "Stand vernieuwen", so press "Stand vernieuwen" once the state names the order. Choose "De prijs van de eerste regel is 1 procent hoger" under "Wat wijkt af?" and press "Proefbevestiging direct aan de assistent geven". To try the mailbox as well, press "Proefbevestiging maken (pdf)" and send the PDF to your own mailbox as an attachment: every try-out is a document of its own, so this opens a case of its own too.
3. Within a minute the case is on the tab "Beoordelen" with the order's lines and the one change. While "Eerste zaken van een leverancier" is above 0, the case comes to you as one of a new supplier's first cases: choose "Bevestigen: waarde van de leverancier overnemen" and close the case. Otherwise the aid takes the price over by itself, within the thresholds.
4. The change stands on the tab "In Exact invoeren" with "(proefdocument)" behind its row, under the line "Proefstand: voer deze wijzigingen alleen in het test-Exact in."; the digest marks it "(proefdocument)" too and leaves it out of its count (section 2.1). Enter the price in the test Exact and tick the row.
5. Give a second try-out of the same order, with "Niets: alles komt overeen". It prints the price you entered, and within a minute its case is closed without a price item: on the tab "Beoordelen" nothing waits, and no new row stands on the tab "In Exact invoeren". (While "Eerste zaken van een leverancier" is above 0, the case comes to you as in step 3: "Afwijkingen" then holds the one item "Nieuwe leverancier" and no price, and above the lists stands "Sinds deze inkooporder is opgenomen, is in Exact ingevoerd: regel 1: prijs ...".)

Report in words whether the case showed the order's lines with the one change, whether its row stood on the list, whether the second try-out closed without a price item, and the lines under "Stand van de koppeling". Try it first on the own PC with a made-up order: prepare a folder with `python3 -m aid.demo --out live --prepare-only`, start the assistant on that folder with the launcher of section 3.4.1 and that settings file (`python3 -m aid.start --settings live/settings.json`), and on its settings tab switch the test mode on in the block and set "Eerste zaken van een leverancier" to 5 (section 2.8) first, so that the case comes to the tab "Beoordelen" in step 3. The demo folder knows the made-up orders already: skip step 1 there and choose an order such as 250108 under "Inkooporder". The demo folder holds 0 there: with 0 the aid takes the price over by itself and closes the case, and only its rows on the tab "In Exact invoeren" show the change. Then try it on the laptop with one order of the test Exact. The try-out proves the chain from the mailbox to the list, not the reading of a real supplier's confirmation, which needs Copilot.

**A test walk of the pre-check with a try-out cart.** On the work laptop, with the test account of Exact, the test mode on, no authorization limit in the settings file, the columns of the export set (part 2) and the extension loaded in the browser with the code of the settings tab in its options (section 3.13; checks A3 and B4):

1. Enter an order in the test Exact without sending it, make the export of the order lines and give it to the aid: on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7) or in `inbox/`. After the next pass "Stand van de koppeling" names it: "Laatst opgenomen: inkooporder" with its number and "uit een export".
2. In "Proefdraaien met een inkooporder uit Exact", press "Stand vernieuwen" and choose the order under "Inkooporder". Under "Wat wijkt af in de proefwinkelwagen?", the question above "Proefwinkelwagen maken", choose "De prijs van de eerste regel is 1 procent hoger" and press "Proefwinkelwagen maken".
3. Open the link of the note in the browser that holds the extension. Within about a second the bar appears at the top of the page. What it shows depends on the first line of the order and on three settings of the tab "Instellingen" (section 2.8):
   - Green, "Winkelwagen komt overeen met de inkooporder", with the sentence "Winkelwagen komt overeen met inkooporder" and the order's number, "; 1 regel op de inkooporder voorbereid", and the line of the price under it, such as "regel 1 (KM-2101): prijs op de inkooporder: wijziging voorbereid van 0.95 naar 0.96, zie de zaak (prijs binnen de drempel)": the higher price is within the thresholds "Prijsverschil: hoogstens dit percentage" and "Prijsverschil: hoogstens dit bedrag per regel (euro)", and "Eerste zaken van een leverancier" is 0, or the aid closed that many cases of the order's supplier already.
   - Amber, with the price as a difference and "prijsverschil boven de drempel" behind it: the higher price is above a threshold. The new price is at least one step of the price's decimals higher, so that 1 percent can come out as more: with the thresholds as installed, 2 percent and 25 euro, a first price below 0,50 gives this bar (0,49 becomes 0,50, which is more than 2 percent), and so does a first line worth more than about 2.400 euro (1 percent of it, rounded up to the price's decimals, is more than 25 euro). For the green walk pick an order whose first line has a price of 0,50 or more and is worth less than that.
   - Amber, with "eerste zaken van deze leverancier gaan naar de inkoper" and the price as a difference: "Eerste zaken van een leverancier" is above 0 and the aid has not closed that many cases of the order's supplier. A try-out is no supplier's case, and an order known from an export without a supplier column names no supplier at all, so the try-out cart of such an order gives this bar for as long as that setting is above 0. Choose "Bevestigen: waarde van de leverancier overnemen" on the tab "Beoordelen" and close the case; the bar of the same page is green after that.
4. The change stands on the tab "In Exact invoeren" with "(proefdocument)" behind its row, such as "Inkooporder 250201, regel 1 (KM-2101): prijs 0,95 → 0,96 (proefdocument)"; an order that names its supplier has the supplier in brackets behind its number.

Report in words the sentence of the bar and the row on the list (check B4). On a real webshop (check A20): open the cart of an order whose export the aid does not have yet, read the grey bar, give the export, wait a minute, reload the page and report what the bar says then, and whether the order was found by its number or by its items ("gekozen op de artikelen in de winkelwagen"). Try the try-out cart first on the own PC: in the folder `live` of the walk above, started with the launcher, which starts the capture server as well, switch the test mode on, choose order 250201, which the demo folder knows from a made-up export without a supplier column (its first line: 25 pieces at 0,95), make the try-out cart with the higher price and open the link: the bar is green while "Eerste zaken van een leverancier" is 0, as the demo folder has it. Without the extension the page itself shows, with its heading "Proefwinkel (verzonnen pagina)"; with the extension loaded and the code of `live/settings.json` in its options (`aid/extension/README.md`), the bar shows above it.

## 4. Maintaining it

### 4.1 Adding a supplier

1. On the settings tab of the review page, under "Leveranciers", choose "Leverancier toevoegen", type the supplier's name as the PO prints it, its address for the questions and its lead time in days, and press "Leverancier opslaan" (section 2.8). This writes `supplier_emails` and `supplier_lead_times`; editing those two in `settings.json` is the other way. A supplier that is listed already is not added a second time: choose it under "Leverancier" and change its values there. A new supplier needs an address, a lead time or a tick besides its name.
2. If the supplier's confirmations have a layout no template reads, add a parser template.

A template is a JSON file `aid/readers/templates/<name>.json`; the file name is the template's `name`. Files whose name starts with `po_` are PO templates and are not used for confirmations. Templates are tried in the order of their file names; the first whose `detect` expressions all match the document's text is used. Look at `table_nl.json` (a table) and `text_en.json` (one sentence per line) as examples. The fields:

- `name`, `language` (`"nl"`, `"de"` or `"en"`), `decimal_comma` (true for "1.234,50");
- `detect`: regular expressions that must all match somewhere in the text;
- `header`: per field (`po_reference`, `version_hint`, `supplier`, `document_date`, `currency`, `incoterm`) an expression with exactly one group, the value;
- `table` with `"strategy": "lines"`: `header_row_contains` (text on the title row), `columns` (each a `title` word and the `field` it fills: `line_id`, `item_code`, `description`, `quantity`, `unit`, `unit_price`, `price_basis`, `discount_pct`, `net_price`, `delivery_date`; `line_id` is required), and optionally `line_id_pattern`, `basis_mark` and `item_code_regex`;
- or `table` with `"strategy": "text"`: `line_regex` with named groups after those fields (`line_id` required), and optionally `line_start` and `item_code_regex`.

A misspelled key or a bad expression is refused when the template loads. Test a template on one document:

```bash
python3 -B -c "
from pathlib import Path
from aid.readers.supplier_parser import load_templates, detect_template, read_with_template
pdf = Path('aid/madeup/out/MU01_conf.pdf')
t = detect_template(pdf, load_templates())
print(t.name if t else 'no template fits')
doc = read_with_template(pdf, t)
for line in doc.lines: print(line.line_id, line.item_code.value, line.quantity.value, line.unit_price.value, line.delivery_date.value)
"
```

Or run the batch (section 6) with a one-line `cases.csv` and look at `steps/02_read_document.json` of the case. Add a test beside those of `aid/tests/test_supplier_parser.py` and run the suite. A template for a real supplier is company material: it stays on the company side and never goes into a repository; only the made-up templates are in the repository.

### 4.2 Changing thresholds

Change them on the settings tab of the review page and press "Opslaan" (section 2.8); editing `settings.json` is the other way, and the tab shows such a change the next time it is selected. The runner reads the file again at the start of its next pass when the file has changed (a file that is not valid is logged in `runner.log` and the settings in use are kept); the capture server reads it before its next pre-check and the review page before its next close or undo, so neither needs a restart. A case that was handled before the change keeps its outcome, and on the same day a cart that was checked before is checked anew only when the cart itself changes (section 2.8). The thresholds are `price_pct`, `price_abs`, `lead_time_tolerance_days`, `ask_wait_working_days` and `first_cases_review`; the supplier lists are `review_suppliers` and `messages_off_suppliers`. Each case's audit record keeps the settings it ran with. During the evaluation the settings must not change (section 4.8).

### 4.3 The price-list import and its undo

A supplier's price list is compared with the purchase prices in Exact on the tab "Prijslijst" (Price list) of the review page. The command at the end of this section does the same from a terminal, with a CSV file of the current prices made by hand. Either way the aid only prepares the changes: they stand on the tab "In Exact invoeren" (section 2.6), and the buyer enters them in Exact.

**The tab "Prijslijst".** While the columns of the export of articles are not set, the tab shows one sentence and nothing else: "De kolommen van de export van de artikelen zijn nog niet ingesteld. Dat gebeurt bij de installatie: Instellingen, Koppeling met Exact, deel 3." (section 3.17, part 3). Once they are set, it opens with "Een prijslijst van een leverancier vergelijkt u hier met de inkoopprijzen in Exact. De assistent heeft daarvoor de export van de artikelen van die leverancier uit Exact nodig. Wat verandert, zet u met een druk op de knop op de lijst In Exact invoeren; in Exact verandert de assistent niets." and, top to bottom:

1. "Leverancier", with the line "Kies de leverancier, of typ de naam bij een nieuwe leverancier.": the suppliers whose purchase prices the aid holds; for a new supplier, type the name and press Enter. The spaces around a name do not count, and a name that differs from a listed one in capitals or spaces only is that supplier. Name a supplier as the purchase order names it, so that an article price that a confirmation of that supplier asked counts for it (below). A name of more than 100 characters, or with a line break or another character that no name holds, is not taken: "De naam van de leverancier is te lang of bevat tekens die niet in een naam horen." Under it the tab says what it holds: "Inkoopprijzen van S01 opgenomen op 2026-11-20 09:15 uit artikelen S01.csv: 12 artikelen.", or "Van S01 zijn nog geen inkoopprijzen opgenomen. Geef eerst de export van de artikelen van deze leverancier uit Exact.", or, with no supplier chosen, "Kies eerst een leverancier." Beside the field stands "Inkoopprijzen van deze leverancier weghalen", for a supplier that was given under two spellings (below).
2. "Export van de artikelen uit Exact (Excel of csv)": the export of that supplier's articles with their purchase prices, saved from Exact. Choose the file and press "Inkoopprijzen opnemen", which can be pressed once a file is chosen: "Inkoopprijzen van S01 opgenomen: 12 artikelen; 3 rijen overgeslagen." The rows passed over are those that the test of part 3 names (section 3.17): no item code, a price that cannot be read, an article listed twice. The export takes the place of all the prices the aid held for that supplier, and leaves every other supplier's prices as they are. An export that gives no article stores nothing and leaves what the aid held: "Deze export gaf geen artikel: geen rij heeft een artikelcode en een prijs die te lezen is. Er is niets opgenomen." Check the columns of part 3 then. When the export has a column with the supplier (set in part 3), no supplier needs to be chosen: each row counts for the supplier it names, a row without one is passed over, the tab says "Inkoopprijzen opgenomen van 2 leveranciers: 12 artikelen; 3 rijen overgeslagen.", and the suppliers it named are in the list from then on. Give a new export whenever prices were changed in Exact otherwise than through the list "In Exact invoeren": the newest export is what the aid takes for the prices in Exact. One press stores one export, also when the button is clicked twice.
3. The price list, offered once the supplier has purchase prices: choose the file under "Prijslijst (Excel of csv)", an `.xlsx` or `.csv` file with its column titles in its first row. A file with a heading above its column titles is not taken (below). A CSV file is read as the export is: in UTF-8, UTF-16 with its byte order mark or Windows-1252, cut at semicolons, commas or tabs, whichever cuts the row of column titles into the most columns, so that a comma inside a description never decides. Of an Excel file one sheet is read, the one you see (below). Rows and columns hidden in Excel (a filter, a hidden column) are read as the others are. Point out its columns: "Kolom met de artikelcode", "Kolom met de prijs", "Kolom met de prijs per (mag leeg)", "Geldig vanaf (mag leeg)" and "Geldig tot (mag leeg)". Each field offers the titles of the list, and "(staat niet in het bestand)". The fields come filled: with the columns of the supplier's last list that was put on the list "In Exact invoeren", else with the titles of the list that are the aid's own (`item_code`, `price`, `basis`, `valid_from`, `valid_to`). A column of the list that has one of the aid's own titles is read under that title also when its field is left on "(staat niet in het bestand)". Under the fields stands: "Heeft de lijst een kolom voor waar de prijs per geldt (per 100, per doos), wijs die dan aan: anders leest de assistent elke prijs als een prijs per stuk."
4. "Bekijken" compares the list with the purchase prices in Exact and writes nothing anywhere. The table has a row for each row of the list, in the order of the file, under "Artikel", "Inkoopprijs in Exact", "Prijs op de lijst" and "Uitkomst". Under it stands the count: "2 prijzen veranderen, 1 blijft gelijk, 3 rijen overgeslagen.", followed, when a row of the list is past its last day, by "Sommige rijen zijn verlopen: vraag de leverancier om een actuele lijst.", and, when a price differs strongly (below), by "Een prijs die meer dan drie keer zo hoog of zo laag is, zet de assistent niet op de lijst: controleer de lijst, en voer een echte wijziging zelf in Exact in.", and, when a row that is kept off the list leaves an older price of this supplier on the list, by a warning that names the articles (below, "An older price that stays on the list").
5. "Op de lijst In Exact invoeren zetten", which can be pressed after "Bekijken", prepares the prices that change: "2 prijswijzigingen staan op de lijst In Exact invoeren." The table then shows again what became of each row. Enter them in Exact and tick them on that tab, one by one, or all of one price list with "Alle artikelprijzen van deze prijslijst zijn ingevoerd" (section 2.6).

**The outcomes.** The column "Uitkomst" says what became of each row of the list:

| Uitkomst | What it means |
|---|---|
| "prijs verandert" | The price on the list differs from the purchase price in Exact: the press puts it on the list "In Exact invoeren". |
| "ongewijzigd" | The two prices are equal as numbers. |
| "artikel niet bekend in Exact" | The supplier's export does not hold the item code: the article is not that supplier's in Exact, or its row of the export was passed over. |
| "prijs niet te lezen" | The price cell of the list is empty or holds no number. |
| "prijs per ander aantal: niet vergeleken" | The row states a price per another number than 1, per 100 for example, in the column pointed out under "Kolom met de prijs per (mag leeg)". The purchase prices are per one unit, so the row is passed over, never compared as a price a hundred times too high. When that column is not pointed out, the aid cannot know what the price is per: the price then differs strongly, and the row is kept off the list all the same (the next row). |
| "wijkt sterk af: controleer de kolom "prijs per"" | The price on the list is more than three times the purchase price in Exact, or less than a third of it, which is what a price per 100 or per box looks like when it is read as a price per piece. The row is not put on the list. Check whether the list has a column for what a price is per, and point it out; a real change of that size, and any price of an article whose purchase price in Exact is 0, you enter in Exact yourself. |
| "ook bij leverancier S02: voer deze prijs zelf in Exact in" | Another supplier holds this article too, by its purchase prices or by an open change on the list "In Exact invoeren", and the outcome names that supplier as the aid holds its name; with more than two suppliers it names the first two and how many more, as in "ook bij leverancier S02, S03 en nog 2: voer deze prijs zelf in Exact in". The row is not put on the list: enter the price in Exact yourself, for the supplier whose list this is (below). |
| "al afgevinkt op de lijst In Exact invoeren" | The list "In Exact invoeren" holds this price as ticked after the supplier's newest export, for a confirmation of another supplier for example. As far as the aid knows Exact holds it, and no press can put it on the list: the row is passed over. |
| "van de lijst gehaald: zet de regel daar terug als de prijs toch moet worden ingevoerd" | You chose before not to enter this price ("Aangevinkte regels niet invoeren", section 2.6), and a change that you chose not to enter is not asked again for the same value. The row is passed over: put the row back on the list there with "Weer op de lijst zetten" when the price must be entered after all. |
| "nog niet geldig" | The row's first day lies after today. |
| "verlopen" | The row's last day lies before today. |
| "dubbele rij" | A later row for an article that an earlier row of the list settled. |
| "geen artikelcode" | A row without an item code, named by its row in the file, "rij 7". |
| "geldig vanaf niet te lezen" | The row's first day cannot be read as one day. With "geldig tot niet te lezen", the same for its last day. |
| "niet gelukt" | Shown after the press: the change could not be prepared, because the file of the prepared changes could not be written. The note under the table names those articles, "Niet op de lijst gezet: KM-1002. Bekijk de lijst opnieuw om het nog eens te proberen.": press "Bekijken" and the press again. |

**What the press carries out.** The press carries out the view that the page showed, once: a second press without a new view changes nothing. When the list's file, the supplier, the columns or the supplier's purchase prices changed since that view (a newer export, an article price ticked on the list), it carries nothing out and says "De gegevens zijn veranderd sinds u de lijst bekeek. Bekijk de lijst opnieuw." Each price that changes is a row of `cases/prepared/prepared_changes.csv`, as every change the aid prepares, and an entry of the list's own write log, `cases/pricelists/<day>/write_log.jsonl`, so that an article price of a list that is in Exact is put back from the tab "In Exact invoeren" with "Terugdraaien in Exact" (section 2.6). The columns pointed out are kept for the supplier in `cases/exact/pricelist_columns.json`, beside the purchase prices and written whole or not at all, like them: no press of the tab changes the settings file, whose hash the freeze keeps (section 4.8). When the columns could not be kept, the changes stand on the list all the same and the note says so after the count: "De kolommen van deze lijst konden niet worden bewaard: wijs ze de volgende keer opnieuw aan." When the list's own write log could not be written, the changes stand on the list as well, the table is shown and the columns are kept, and the note says after the count: "De assistent kon deze wijzigingen niet in zijn eigen logboek van de prijslijst schrijven en kan ze daardoor later niet zelf terugzetten: geef dit door aan de onderzoeker." "Terugdraaien in Exact" then has no entry to put such a price back from. The same list put on the list twice adds no row: the list shows one row per article and value. What the table shows as "prijs verandert", the press puts on the list. A price that no press can put there is never shown as changing: its row reads "al afgevinkt op de lijst In Exact invoeren" or "van de lijst gehaald: zet de regel daar terug als de prijs toch moet worden ingevoerd" (the table above) and counts among the rows passed over. So "0 prijswijzigingen staan op de lijst In Exact invoeren." follows a view in which no price changed, or a press none of whose changes could be prepared, whose rows then read "niet gelukt".

**A price that Exact does not hold after all.** What the tab shows as changing, the press can put on the list. When you ticked a price as entered and a newer export of the supplier then shows another price (Exact was changed outside the aid, the price was typed wrong in Exact, or the tick came before the price was entered), "Bekijken" compares with the newer export and shows the price as changing, and the press puts that change back on the list, as "Weer op de lijst zetten" does (section 2.6), noted in `marks.jsonl` as done by the assistant, and counts it with the others, also when the tick is older than the 14 days of the handled changes. So when a purchase price in Exact is not what the list asked, give the export of that supplier's articles again and put the list on the list again. A tick made after the newest export stays: as far as the aid knows, Exact holds that price. Its row reads "ongewijzigd" when the tick counts for this supplier, and "al afgevinkt op de lijst In Exact invoeren" when the price was ticked for another supplier; either way the press does not ask it again.

**The purchase prices in Exact, as far as the aid knows.** The aid keeps the prices of each supplier's last export in `cases/exact/article_prices.json`, a file it writes whole or not at all. The price it compares a list with is that export's price or, for an article whose price was entered since, the price last ticked as entered on the tab "In Exact invoeren" after the export was given. So a price that you entered yesterday is the purchase price in Exact today, and the list does not ask it twice. An article price of a price list counts for the supplier of that list. One that a confirmation asked counts for the supplier of its case, as the purchase order names that supplier, and one whose case names no supplier for every supplier whose export holds the article. A newer export of the supplier is taken as it is: what was ticked before it no longer counts. The aid never ticks an article price off by itself, also not after a newer export (section 4.9). When `article_prices.json` cannot be read, the tab says "De opgenomen inkoopprijzen kunnen niet worden gelezen. Geef de export van de artikelen opnieuw." and holds no supplier until an export is given again.

**A file the tab does not take.** Both fields take any file, and the tab says in a sentence why it does not take one; nothing is stored or prepared then. Another type than .xlsx and .csv: "artikelen.xls is niet aangenomen: de assistent leest hier Excel (.xlsx) en csv." An empty file: "artikelen.csv is niet aangenomen: het bestand is leeg." An Excel file with more than one sheet to read: "Dit Excel-bestand heeft meer dan één werkblad met gegevens (Slangen, Klemmen). De assistent leest één werkblad: sla het werkblad dat u bedoelt op als een eigen bestand en geef dat." Of an Excel file, the export and the price list alike, the aid reads one sheet, the one you see: a hidden sheet is never read; when one visible sheet holds data, that sheet is read, wherever it stands; when more than one does, the file is refused with the names of those sheets, so that no sheet is compared or left out without a word. An Excel file whose only data stands on a hidden sheet: "Dit Excel-bestand heeft alleen gegevens op een verborgen werkblad. De assistent leest geen verborgen werkbladen: maak het werkblad zichtbaar, of sla het op als een eigen bestand, en geef het opnieuw." In a CSV file cut at commas every filled row below the column titles must have exactly their number of cells, the empty ones counted, since a price of 12,70 without quotation marks is cut in two there and would be read as 12: a row with more or with fewer cells refuses the file whole, with that row: "Rij 3 van dit bestand heeft een ander aantal cellen dan de rij met kolomtitels. In een bestand met komma's als scheidingsteken staat er dan meestal een komma in een getal of in een omschrijving zonder aanhalingstekens. Sla het bestand op als Excel-bestand (.xlsx) of met puntkomma's als scheidingsteken, en geef het opnieuw." In a file cut at semicolons or tabs a decimal comma cannot cut a row, so there only a filled cell beyond the last column title refuses the file: "Rij 3 van dit bestand heeft meer cellen dan de rij met kolomtitels. Meestal staat er dan een komma in een getal of in een omschrijving zonder aanhalingstekens. Sla het bestand op als Excel-bestand (.xlsx) of met puntkomma's als scheidingsteken, en geef het opnieuw." Empty cells beyond the titles and short rows are read there. What cannot be told is a comma file whose writer leaves out cells so that a cut row has, by chance, the titles' number of cells: save a list or an export as .xlsx, or with semicolons, whenever its numbers have decimal commas. A price list with a heading above its column titles (one filled cell in its first filled row, and more in a later row): "De kolomtitels moeten in de eerste rij van het bestand staan: haal de regels erboven weg en geef het bestand opnieuw." An export that names, in a row it would keep, a supplier whose name is no name: "In rij 6 van de export staat bij de leverancier een naam die te lang is of tekens bevat die niet in een naam horen. Er is niets opgenomen." A CSV file that cannot be cut into columns (another separator than a semicolon, a comma or a tab, UTF-16 without its byte order mark, or no text at all) is named by the sentence that the connection block has for one. An export in no row of which the column titles of part 3 stand: "De kolommen van dit bestand zijn anders dan ingesteld: controleer Instellingen, Koppeling met Exact, deel 3." An export without a supplier column while no supplier is chosen: "Kies eerst de leverancier van wie deze artikelen zijn." A damaged file is named by the sentence that the connection block has for one, no file by "Kies eerst een bestand.", a price list without a column for the item code or for the price by "Kies de kolom met de artikelcode." or "Kies de kolom met de prijs.", and anything else by its class, as in "Dit is niet gelukt (PermissionError).".

**Two suppliers of one article.** The list "In Exact invoeren" keeps one price per article, whichever supplier's list asked it, and a row names no supplier (section 2.6). So two suppliers' prices of one article cannot both stand on it. The tab knows another supplier of an article in two ways: by an export given on the tab, when that supplier's purchase prices hold the article, or by an open change on the list, when a price list of that supplier or a confirmation whose case names that supplier asked a price of the article that is still to enter, also when no export of that supplier was given. Until the list keeps an article price per supplier, a row of a price list for such an article is not put on the list: the table shows it with its price on the list and "ook bij leverancier S02: voer deze prijs zelf in Exact in", it counts among the rows passed over, and the press leaves it out. Enter that price in Exact yourself, for the supplier whose list it is, and give that supplier's export again afterwards: the row then reads "ongewijzigd". So no price list of one supplier takes an open change of another supplier off the list, or joins it, and a tick counts for the supplier whose list or case asked it. An article price that a confirmation asked keeps counting for the supplier of its case, as above; a confirmation whose case names no supplier is no other supplier.

**An older price that stays on the list.** A row that is kept off the list, as shared or as differing strongly, does not replace what an earlier price list of the same supplier put on the list for its article. While that older change is still open and asks another price than this list, the count under the table and the note after the press end with: "Let op: voor KM-1001 staat op de lijst In Exact invoeren nog de prijs van een eerdere prijslijst van deze leverancier. Deze lijst vraagt een andere prijs, die de assistent niet op de lijst zet: haal die regel daar van de lijst en voer de goede prijs zelf in Exact in." Take that row off the list on the tab "In Exact invoeren" with "Aangevinkte regels niet invoeren" (section 2.6) and enter the right price in Exact yourself.

**A supplier given under two spellings.** A name that differs from a listed one in more than capitals and spaces is another supplier to the aid: S01 B.V. and S01 BV are two. When the export of one supplier was given under two such names, the two share every article and no row of the supplier's price list goes on the list; the outcome of each row names the other spelling. Choose the wrong spelling under "Leverancier" and press "Inkoopprijzen van deze leverancier weghalen", beside the field. The aid removes the purchase prices and the kept columns that it holds under that name, both or neither, and nothing else: "De opgenomen inkoopprijzen van S01 B.V. zijn weggehaald. Wat al op de lijst In Exact invoeren staat, blijft staan." No row of the list "In Exact invoeren" changes. A change that a price list under the wrong spelling put there stays open and is still that name's, so the row of the right spelling keeps reading "ook bij leverancier S01 B.V.: voer deze prijs zelf in Exact in" until you enter and tick that change or take it off the list there. For a name of which the aid holds no purchase prices the press changes nothing, and the tab says so by the sentence quoted under step 1.

**A price that differs strongly.** A list that states its prices per 100 or per box, read as prices per piece, would ask prices a hundred times too high. Point out the column for what a price is per whenever the list has one. Whatever the columns, a price that is more than three times the purchase price in Exact, or less than a third of it, is never put on the list: the table shows "wijkt sterk af: controleer de kolom "prijs per"", the row counts among the rows passed over, and the count ends with the sentence quoted under step 4. The command below has no such check.

On a page opened with `--share`, and on a page without its settings file (the Colab notebook), the tab says only that it is not available. While the columns of the export of articles are set (`article_columns`, part 3), `--share` itself is refused (section 3.7). In the test mode (section 3.17) a price list is handled as any other: it only prepares rows for the buyer.

**The command.**

```bash
python3 -m aid.pricelist --file <list.csv or list.xlsx> --supplier <supplier> --current <current_prices.csv> --prepared <folder> (--write-log <folder> | --settings <root>/settings.json)
```

- `--file`: the price list, a CSV file (comma or semicolon) or an `.xlsx` file, of which the one sheet you see is read, with the columns `item_code` and `price`, and optionally `basis`, `unit`, `min_quantity`, `valid_from`, `valid_to`.
- `--supplier`: the supplier, recorded with every change.
- `--current`: the current prices, a CSV file with the columns `item_code` and `price`.
- `--prepared`: the folder for `prepared_changes.csv`; give `<root>/cases/prepared`, so that the changes stand on the tab "In Exact invoeren" (section 2.6).
- `--write-log`: the folder whose `write_log.jsonl` gets every change; without it, `--settings` gives `<cases_root>/pricelists/<YYYY-MM-DD>`. One of the two is required.

Of an Excel file the command reads one sheet, the one you see, as the tab and every other part of the aid that reads an Excel file does: a hidden sheet is never read, the one visible sheet that holds data is read wherever it stands, and a file with more than one such sheet is refused with the names of those sheets (exit code 1). A CSV file it reads in UTF-8 or Windows-1252, cut at commas or semicolons. It looks up its columns first, so that a file whose first row does not hold them is refused with the missing column named; then a row that the aid does not read (above: in a comma file another number of cells than the row of column titles, in any CSV file a filled cell beyond the last column title) refuses the file with that row, as on the tab; the same holds for the file of `--current`. A workbook whose only data stands on a hidden sheet is refused in words that say so. The command has none of the tab's own checks: an article that another supplier holds too, a price that differs strongly, and a price that the list holds as ticked or as taken off the list. The import always prepares its changes; it writes nothing to Exact. It prints a line per row (updated, skipped with the reason, or error) and a summary, and warns when rows are past `valid_to`. Exit code 0 when it ran, 1 when a file cannot be read or a write failed, 2 for a wrong command line. To undo an import, reverse its write-log folder with `undo_case` (as in section 3.10, with that folder in place of the case folder and a `PreparedWriter` or the runner's writer). Prepared into `<root>/cases/prepared`, the undo takes the import's article prices that were not ticked as entered off the list, and asks to put the old price back for those that were (section 3.10). One article price of a list that is in Exact is put back from the tab with "Terugdraaien in Exact" (section 2.6), when the import kept its write log in a folder under `<cases_root>/pricelists/` (the default with `--settings`).

### 4.4 Re-running a case

A case is run once. The runner never runs a case again that holds `steps/08_result.json`, because a second run would make its changes again and send its questions again. A case whose run was cut off before any change or message is simply run at the next pass. A case cut off after a change or a message is not run again: it goes to review with the item "run cut off after writes: check the write log". Then the researcher compares its `write_log.jsonl` (and `messages/sent.jsonl`) with Exact and the mailbox, finishes by hand what is missing, and the buyer decides on the page.

A confirmation that waits for its purchase order is checked again by the runner itself, never run again in its own folder: when that order, or the version the confirmation quotes, is filed, a new case is opened from the same document and runs, and the first case is closed as replaced, with the new case's id in its `steps/09_close.json` and its audit record (section 3.17). At the first pass after the runner starts every order of the store counts as filed, so a waiting case is checked again then only when its order, or the version it quotes, is in the store. Both folders stay, the first one for the audit (section 3.1). This happens only while nothing was done on the first case: a case on which the buyer decided, which was closed, or which wrote or sent anything is never checked again. A changed number recognition counts for documents that come in after the change; on a case that came in before it, the buyer decides.

To run a case again on purpose (after a fix, and only when its changes were undone or never made), through Python:

```python
import os
from pathlib import Path
from aid.settings import load_settings
from aid.runner import build_deps
from aid.case import load_case
from aid.pipeline import run_case, record_dispositions

settings = load_settings(Path("<root>/settings.json"))
deps = build_deps(settings, os.environ)
case = load_case(settings.cases_root, "<case id>")
result = run_case(case, deps, rerun=True)
record_dispositions(case, result)
```

This makes the changes and sends the questions again, and adds rows to `dispositions.csv`. A safer way to try a document again is the batch (section 6): it opens a new case folder, prepares its changes and sends nothing. Sending the same file to the inbox again does not work: a document seen before is skipped as "duplicate". For a confirmation that waits for its order, give the order instead (section 3.3).

### 4.5 Cleaning old cases

During the evaluation, delete nothing under `cases/` and `po_store/`. The evaluation needs each case folder whole (`case.json`, `input/`, `steps/`, `audit.json`, `write_log.jsonl`, `dispositions.csv`, `decisions.jsonl`, `messages/`), and `cases/prepared/` (`prepared_changes.csv` and the buyer's ticks in `marks.jsonl`). Deleting a case folder also loses its undo, makes a re-sent copy of its document open a new case, and lowers the count of closed cases that rule R11 uses for its supplier. The list "In Exact invoeren" reads the folder of each case whose changes it holds (section 2.6): without the folder a row shows its article as "onbekend", a value entered for it is laid over another version of the order by its line number alone, and the rows of a case made from a try-out count as a supplier's own, so that a value entered for a try-out would count for a supplier's confirmation.

What may go after it has been read: the files in `inbox/processed/` (each case keeps its own copy in `input/`, and each PO its copy in `po_store/`), old files in `cases/digests/`, the `.eml` files in `cases/outbox/` once handled, and an old `runner.log` or `intake.jsonl` while the runner is stopped (the tab "Bestanden" then starts a new list). After the evaluation, archive the case folders to the company's restricted storage rather than deleting them.

A runner that is stopped in the middle of a pass, as when the PC is switched off, can leave a document of that pass without its line in `intake.jsonl` (section 2.7), with "Al eerder ontvangen: niets gedaan" as its only line, or with a line twice: its error line, a skip line or "Al eerder ontvangen: niets gedaan". The purchase order or the case exists all the same: the digest lists the case, and "Stand van de koppeling" on the settings tab counts the order (section 3.17). A document that the aid left for you in such a pass ("bekijk dit document zelf") is named only in `cases/review_items.jsonl`. A runner stopped with `STOP` ends between two passes (section 3.5); it loses only the lines that still waited because `intake.jsonl` could not be written (section 3.6), and a file that still stays in `inbox/` gets its line at the next run. While `digest_state.json` cannot be written, a confirmation checked again as a new case (section 3.17) leaves its old case in review beside the new one, without the line of the check again, until its order is filed again or the runner starts anew.

### 4.6 Backups

Back up the whole `<root>` folder, at a moment when the runner is stopped or between passes. It holds company data (documents, addresses, the authorization limit in `settings.json`), so the backup stays on the company side, in storage the company names. The credentials are not in the folder; they stay in the secret store.

### 4.7 Updating the code

1. Create `STOP` and wait for the runner to end; stop the review page.
2. In the copy of the thesis repository: `git pull`.
3. Install the packages again and run the suite:

```bash
python3 -m pip install -r aid/requirements.txt -r aid/requirements-dev.txt
python3 -m pytest aid/tests -q
```

4. Only when every test passes: delete `STOP` and start the runner and the review page again.

Development happens in the thesis repository. The aid's own repository, Luuuuuig/Checking_aid_BEP, is a copy of `aid/` and of the two templates its tests read (`evaluation/templates/dispositions.csv` and `references.csv`). A sync copies these again by hand: copy them, run the suite in the copy, commit and push there. Nothing does this automatically.

### 4.8 The freeze

```bash
python3 -m aid.freeze --settings <root>/settings.json [--out <file>]
```

It writes the freeze record (default `freeze_<date>.json` in the current folder): the git commit and whether the working tree is clean, the sha256 of the settings file and the settings themselves (the authorization limit only as "set" or "unset"), the prompt files with their versions and hashes, the model, the rules R01 to R11 with the settings they read and their values, the aid version, the Python version and the installed packages. The record holds the settings' addresses and supplier names, so it stays on the company side.

During the evaluation nothing of this may change: no `git pull`, no change to `settings.json` (its hash is recorded), no new template or prompt, no package update. The tab "Prijslijst" changes none of it: the exports given there, the columns it keeps for a supplier's list and the changes it prepares are data under `cases/` (section 4.3). A save of a part of the block "Koppeling met Exact" does change the settings file. Freeze from a clean working tree. If something must change, record a new freeze and note the deviation as the evaluation protocol says.

### 4.9 Known limitations of the pre-check in the browser

- **A tick without an entry is believed.** Nothing reads Exact. A cart, like a confirmation, is checked against the order with the values laid over it that were ticked on the tab "In Exact invoeren" after that look at the order was filed (section 2.6), so a cart shown again after a ticked price difference asks nothing more. A tick given without the entry in Exact is believed until a PDF of the order, or an export, is given to the aid after the tick and shows another value for that line: that print ends the tick, the change stands on the list again, and from then on documents are compared with the order as printed (section 2.6). Until such a print comes, the aid compares with the ticked value and a case can close as confirmed on it. An export whose rows for the order are those of the look before files nothing (section 3.13), and still counts as such a print: it is a look at the order at the time it was given. A print that reaches the aid after the tick but was made before the entry ends a tick rightly given too, and the row says so; tick it again. A change that is not ticked counts for nothing: a cart changed after a small price difference is still checked against the look as it was filed and asks the same change again, as the same row of the list.
- **The order's number is read from the page only in one form.** The cart reader finds a number of six to eight digits that stands after one of the words "PO", "Inkooporder", "Bestelling", "Bestellung", "Order", "Referentie" or "Reference" on the same line of the page, with nothing but spaces, a colon, a hash or a dash between them, and that ends there. A number that goes on is another number, of which no part is read: one with a letter directly behind it, or with a separator (a hyphen, an en dash, a slash, a full stop, an underscore, a comma or a colon) that a letter or a digit follows ("250301A", "250301-01", "250301/2", "250301.1", "250301_01", "250301,5", "250301:2"); "250301.", "250301," and "250301:" at the end of a text or before a space are read. A number of another form, or one that the page shows in another cell than its word, is not read: the order is then found by the cart's items, or guessed (section 2.5). Whether a real webshop shows the order's number so is check A20.
- **A number the page states that the aid does not know as an order counts for nothing.** A webshop prints numbers of its own, so such a number neither finds an order nor casts doubt on one: the items of the cart decide. When it is the number of a new order whose export the aid does not have yet, the cart is checked by its items until that export is filed; a reload then finds the order by its number, unless a case whose order was found already answers that cart (below).
- **By its items the aid is sure of an order in one case only.** The order's export was given on the day of the cart, the order holds exactly the cart's items, it is the only such order, and it is not sent. A cart of an order that was exported on an earlier day and not changed since is a guess, which the buyer confirms on the review page: giving the same export again does not help, since an export that changes nothing files nothing. A cart with an item more or fewer than its order is a guess too. An order counts as not sent until its PDF or a supplier's confirmation of it comes in: sending an order from Exact is optional, so an order placed in a webshop may never count as sent. Two orders with the same items filed on one day cannot be told apart: the cart is a guess, compared with the one filed last.
- **A case whose order was found keeps that order for the same cart of that day.** Found by its number or by its items, the case answers its page on any day and its cart that day, whatever is filed later, and the changes the aid prepared in it stay: a case in which the aid prepared changes is not replaced behind the buyer. It matters in one state: two orders with the same items are filed on one day, and the cart of the second was checked while the aid held only the first. The bar names the number of the order the cart was compared with. When that is not the cart's order, drop the prepared change on the tab "In Exact invoeren" (section 2.6) and check the cart yourself.
- **A grey cart is checked again only when the page is sent again.** The extension sends a page when it opens and when the cart changes. A cart page left open while the export of its order is filed keeps its grey banner until it is reloaded. A guess on which the buyer decided an item is never replaced: when another order is found for its cart later, a case of that order is opened beside it and answers from then on, and the guess stays on the review page as he left it until he closes it. A changed cart is checked anew.
- **One cart can be answered by two cases.** A case that holds the page itself and stands answers before any later case of the same cart is looked at. So the page of one day keeps its case, while the same cart in the bytes of a later day has a case of that day, and the two may name the order in different ways: one found, the other a guess.
- **A replaced case stays in its folder.** The case that a later case replaced is closed and keeps its files, its copy of the page under `cases/captures/` included; only its `capture.json` (`replaced_by`) and its close record say what became of it. A replacement that the end of the server cut off between its two steps leaves the earlier case marked and not yet closed: it is off the review page and out of the digests, and it is finished when the same cart comes in a page of other bytes that day, when its own page comes, or when the server starts; the page of the capture that was cut off does not finish it when that page came in other bytes than the earlier case's own page (section 3.13).
- **A cart check compares the lines of the cart and nothing else of the page.** The item, the quantity, the unit and the price of each line are compared with the order. The delivery time, a minimum or pack quantity, the delivery costs and the delivery terms that a webshop shows are not read and not compared. The last line of the green and of the amber banner says so ("Niet vergeleken: levertijd, minimale afname of verpakkingseenheid, verzendkosten en leveringsvoorwaarden."), and the buyer checks them before ordering.
- **An article price is ticked by hand.** The aid never sees a purchase price change in Exact. A newer export of a supplier's articles does not tick the open article prices of that supplier off, as a new PDF does for a line of a purchase order (section 2.6): tick them on the tab "In Exact invoeren", one by one or all of one price list with one press. A newer export is then the newer look at that supplier's prices (section 4.3). Entering the prices in Exact stays work by hand: whether Exact's own import of items can take it over is the owner's check A17, and nothing is built for it.
- **Closed cart cases count for the first cases of a supplier.** A cart case that closes counts toward `first_cases_review` of its supplier, like a closed confirmation, so a few pre-checks can end the first-cases review of that supplier. The case of a try-out cart does not count, and nor does a replaced case, which has no supplier. The try-out cart of an order that names no supplier has no supplier either, so with `first_cases_review` above 0 it always comes to the buyer (section 3.17).
- **The capture server and the runner share the cases.** Both may use the same `cases_root` on one computer. The runner never runs a case again that has a result, and it leaves a case alone while the server's `.running` file in it is younger than ten minutes. A run of the server that takes longer than that (a slow Exact route, for example) can therefore be run a second time by the runner, and a case whose run crashed waits up to ten minutes before the runner finishes it.

### 4.10 Known limitations of the questions and their replies

- **A reply is tied to its question by the e-mail it names** (section 2.3). A reply written as a new e-mail, or sent by a program that drops the reply headers, names no question: it is not filed, and its question comes back after the wait as not answered (section 5). One reply to two of the aid's e-mails answers the one it names, and the other comes back after its wait.
- **With two questions without a reply the wait runs from the one sent last**, so the earlier question's own wait passes without a notice; when the later one ends without a reply, both are over.
- **An e-mail put into a case's `messages/replies/` by hand that names no message** counts as the answer to the latest question sent before the file came into the folder, by the time of the file itself (when it was last changed), and to no question asked after it. A file copied there with an older time of its own can so count for an earlier question than meant, or for none: the runner then brings the case back when the wait of the open question ends. A case left waiting with no question out comes back at the next pass (section 2.3).
- **A bounce is known by the words at the start of its subject or by its sender**, after what mail programs and gateways put before them (reply and forward prefixes, tags such as "[EXTERN]" or "***SPAM***", one word in capitals with a colon), and a delivery report in the standard form by what it says itself (a failure is a bounce; a delay or a delivery is an automatic message). A supplier's own reply whose subject begins with such words ("Niet bezorgd op 12 november: nieuwe leverdatum", "Onbestelbaar artikel ...") is taken for a bounce: the case comes back with "Bericht kwam terug", and you read the e-mail in your mailbox. A non-delivery notice in none of these forms comes to you as a reply ("De leverancier heeft geantwoord: lees het antwoord"), or, when its headers mark it as an automatic message, is filed as an automatic reply, and its question then comes back after the wait as not answered. One that names no e-mail and returns neither the message nor its headers is not filed at all, and its question also comes back after the wait, instead of at once as bounced. Whether the company's mail system names the returned message in its non-delivery reports is tried with a real one at the installation.
- **Who answered is not checked beyond the aid's own side.** An e-mail that names a question counts as the supplier's answer whoever sent it, except from the buyer's address, the aid's address and the work mailbox; you read the reply and decide.
- **A message whose hand-over was not confirmed counts as asked.** When it did not leave after all, the case comes back after the wait as not answered, and you ask again. A draft of it may stay in the Drafts folder of the work mailbox.
- **A message written as a file (`send_via` "file") is sent by no one**: the page says so, and the case comes back after the wait.
- **The wait of the settings counts for the questions already out.** After a change of "Wachttijd op een antwoord", the deadline of an open question moves with it.

## 5. Troubleshooting

**A case failed.** It is in the digest under "Mislukt" with the first line of its error, and not on the review page. Look in the case folder: `steps/08_result.json` (status `failed`, field `error`, which names the change it failed at, if any), `audit.json` (`errors`, with the step and, for an unexpected error, the traceback), and the last step file in `steps/`, which shows how far it got. A confirmation whose layout no template fits fails at reading. Fix the cause, then use the batch to try the document again (section 4.4).

**A document was not recognised.** See the "Overgeslagen" block of the digest and `cases/review_items.jsonl`. The class comes from keywords on the first page: "orderbevestiging", "order confirmation", "auftragsbestätigung" and similar make it a confirmation; a title line beginning with "Inkooporder", "Purchase order" or "Bestellung" a PO; "pakbon", "factuur", "offerte" and similar the other classes. With `po_layout` set, a PDF that fits the layout is a PO before any keyword counts, with the two exceptions of section 3.12. Without a keyword the document is "other"; without a text layer it is "no text layer; class unknown" (with `"reader": "llm"` Copilot is asked to class those from the file name and the e-mail's text). The PO number comes from `po_number_pattern` in the e-mail and on the first page. Two or more different numbers: the one the PO store holds with one of its order's item codes on the first page counts; when none of them counts, or more than one, "more than one PO number", skipped and listed. None: a number the store holds that stands on the first page with one of its order's item codes counts, then the one order that holds two of the document's item codes; else the case is opened without a PO, which goes to review as "no version selected" for the buyer to decide (section 3.17 says when the runner still links it by itself). A case linked by a held number or by its lines comes to the buyer with the reason "Kijk na of dit de goede inkooporder is: de assistent vond haar niet aan het nummer achter een van de woorden van de nummerherkenning." (section 2.4): when one supplier's confirmations keep coming with it, add that supplier's word to the number recognition. If the suppliers print the number after other words, or the company's PO numbers have another number of digits (out of the box 6 to 8 after one of five words; a longer number is not taken at all), set the words and the digits at the end of part 1 of "Koppeling met Exact" on the tab "Instellingen" (section 3.17). A file that raised an error stays in the inbox for 30 days, is tried again at every pass and is logged once in `runner.log` as `scan <file>`; the tab "Bestanden" shows it once, with "Kon niet worden gelezen", "Inkooporder niet opgenomen: de assistent heeft deze versie al uit een ander bestand" or the line of a refused export (section 2.7). After 30 days it moves to `inbox/processed/` and must be given again on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7).

**A case waits for its purchase order.** The tab "Beoordelen" says above its documents which order, or which version of it, the aid does not know yet ("Inkooporder 250101 kent de assistent nog niet. ..."), and "Zaken die op hun inkooporder wachten" under "Stand van de koppeling" counts it. Nothing needs deciding: when that order or version is filed, the runner checks the confirmation again by itself (section 3.17). At the first pass after the runner starts every order of the store counts as filed, so a restart checks it again only when its order, or the version it quotes, is in the store already. If the order stays away, give its PDF, or Exact's e-mail with it, on the tab "Bestanden" (section 2.7), whose line then says what became of it; with the mailbox connected, also check that Exact's e-mails reach the folder the aid reads (an e-mail that lay there before the mailbox was connected must be dragged out of the folder and in again, section 3.3). Look at "Stand van de koppeling" too: a PO PDF that does not fit the layout stays in `inbox/` as a file the aid could not read (section 3.12), and the tab "Bestanden" says "Kon niet worden gelezen" for it. A case that quoted a version of its order waits for that version. When the number in the hint is not one of the company's order numbers, the aid took another number of the confirmation for the order number: decide on the case on the review page, and set the number recognition (section 3.17). A changed recognition counts for documents that come in after the change; it links no case that came in before it. A case on which a decision was recorded is not checked again: decide on it on the review page.

**A settings file with `exact_base_url` set.** The runner, the review page, the capture server and the launcher end at their start with exit code 2 and the sentence "exact_base_url is set, but this version of the aid writes nothing in Exact: every change is prepared for the tab "In Exact invoeren". Remove exact_base_url from the settings file." Take the key out of the settings file, or set it to `null`, and start again (section 3.10). `AID_EXACT_TOKEN` is not used.

**A write was refused, or did not come back clean.** Only with a program's write route, which no installation has and no command builds (section 3.10): this entry is for the reader of the records of the route's tests. What the case shows depends on what happened to the write, by the four causes the writer of `aid/execution/exact_api.py` names. *No token*: nothing was sent, and after two attempts the change is prepared. *A status that is not 2xx*: on the read before the write, or a 4xx on the write itself (Exact refused it), the change is prepared; a status that is neither 2xx nor 4xx on the write (a 502 or a 503 of something in between) leaves it unknown whether Exact made the change, so it is "not sure" unless a later read settles it; on the read after a write that Exact accepted, the change is "made, not verified". *A value that reads back different*: the value from before means that Exact ignored the write, and the change is prepared; any other value is "made, not verified". *No connection or no answer*: a connection that was never made (refused, a connect time-out, a TLS or a proxy failure) sent nothing, and the change is prepared; no answer before the write: prepared; no answer at the write, over a connection that stood: "not sure"; after a write that Exact accepted it is "made, not verified". A prepared change is in `prepared_changes.csv`, its write-log entry has the `error`, and the case is in review with "prepared change: apply by hand", every later change of that case "not applied: an earlier write failed": check the token, the route and the endpoints; the buyer enters the prepared change from the tab "In Exact invoeren" (section 2.6) and closes the case. A change made and not verified, or not sure, is prepared nowhere: its write-log entry has status "done" with the failed check, or what happened to the write, in `error` (and `"outcome": "not known"` in its evidence when not sure), the case is in review with "write made, not verified: check in Exact" or "write outcome not known: check in Exact", and every later change of that case is "not applied: an earlier change waits for a look in Exact". The buyer looks in Exact and decides the item (section 2.4); check the route for what kept the answer or the read away.

**A question was not sent.** The case is in review with "Geen e-mailadres van de leverancier" (add the supplier's address on the settings tab under "Leveranciers", section 2.8, or in `supplier_emails`) or "Bericht niet verzonden". For a question of the runner's own check: the mail server refused it, or, through the work mailbox, the Microsoft sign-in has lapsed; sign in again on the tab "Instellingen", in the part "Koppelingen: het Microsoft-postvak en de AI-dienst" at its bottom, then ask the supplier from the page with "Leverancier vragen" on that item, or send the message by hand; see the case's `audit.json`, `messages`. For a question asked on the review page the item has no line, and after the close the page said "Een bericht is niet verzonden; de zaak blijft ter beoordeling." with the kind of cause (section 2.2 lists the sentences, section 3.7 the states behind them): the sign-in lapsed, the mailbox or the server not reached or refusing, no mailbox connected, the mail server's password not set, the settings file or the case's document not readable at that moment. Nothing is recorded as asked, and the record is in the case's `steps/09_close.json` (`messages`, with `status` "not sent (...)", `cause` and, for a failure of the mailbox or the server, `error`). Put the cause right and close the case again: the question is sent then, and the item is settled. If messages appear as files in `cases/outbox/` instead of being sent, either `send_via` is `"file"`, or nothing is connected for the runner, which then writes its own messages as files: no folder of the work mailbox with an account signed in, and no SMTP server with `AID_MAIL_PASSWORD` in its environment (the runner reads the variable once at its start, the review page at each close). The review page writes a file only with `send_via` `"file"`, and says so after the close: "Het bericht is niet verstuurd maar als bestand klaargezet (cases/outbox): de instellingen laten berichten als bestand schrijven."

**A reply was not filed.** A reply is filed only when its `In-Reply-To` or `References` header names the Message-ID of a question the aid sent (recorded in the case's `messages/sent.jsonl`); a delivery report without those headers is filed when the message it returns, or the headers of it, carry such a Message-ID. A reply written as a new e-mail, or a forward that drops these headers, is not filed: it is handled like any other e-mail. The mailbox rule must save the reply as a whole `.eml` file. A reply to a re-approval request is never filed to a case. An e-mail from the buyer's own address, the aid's address or the work mailbox that names a question is filed with its case under `messages/own/` and is no answer (the tab "Bestanden" says "Uw eigen bericht bij de zaak gelegd; dit telt niet als antwoord van de leverancier"), and a notice of a delay or a delivery under `messages/auto/`.

**A case waits although the supplier answered.** A reply answers the question it names and no other (section 2.3). When the case had two questions out, it waits until the other has a reply of its own, and the page says "Vraag aan de leverancier staat open." after the close; when that wait ends, the case comes back with "Geen antwoord binnen de wachttijd". The case's `audit.json` lists each filed e-mail under `answers` with the folder it was filed under (`replies`, `bounces`, `auto` or `own`) and the `message_id` of the question it names, `messages/sent.jsonl` holds the questions, and `messages/escalated.jsonl` the questions whose wait ended. `runner.log` names a case as `unreadable: PermissionError` (or another class) at a pass at which one of its filed replies could not be read; the next pass judges it again.

**The page shows no cases.** The page lists only cases in review and cases with a change made in the system that is not undone (whatever their status). Check that the page was started with the same settings file as the runner, press "Lijst vernieuwen", and look at the digest: cases that wait for a supplier, closed cases and failed cases are not listed unless they hold such a change.

**The digest did not come.** The daily digest is made at the first pass at or after `digest_time` (host time), once a day. If `last_sent` in `cases/digest_state.json` is today, it was made already. Look for `cases/digests/<day>.html` and, without SMTP, an `.eml` in `cases/outbox/`. A failed digest is logged in `runner.log` as `digest` and made again at the next pass. In `per_case` mode a pass without cases or skipped files makes no digest. The runner must be running at that time.

**"Postvak niet verbonden" in the digest.** The runner could not read the work mailbox because the Microsoft sign-in has lapsed or the account was signed out; `runner.log` says `mailbox: not connected` at most once an hour. The digest's line ends "onderaan bij Koppelingen": the buttons are at the bottom of the tab "Instellingen", in the part "Koppelingen: het Microsoft-postvak en de AI-dienst", which opens with a click on its heading. That part still names the account, and says "Aanmelding van ... verlopen" when you press "Verbinding testen" or "Mappen ophalen". Meanwhile the runner handles `inbox/` as usual, a digest it cannot send through the mailbox is written into `cases/outbox/`, and a supplier question or re-approval request is not sent: a case of the runner comes to the review page with "Bericht niet verzonden", and a close on the page says "Een bericht is niet verzonden; de zaak blijft ter beoordeling." (no other way is tried, section 3.14). Open the tab "Instellingen" of the review page on the PC where the runner runs, as the runner's Windows user, open "Koppelingen" at its bottom and press "Aanmelden bij Microsoft" (or "Aanmelden met een code"). At its next pass the runner reads the mailbox again from where it stopped and logs `mailbox: signed in again`, its next messages go through the mailbox again, and the line is gone from the next digest. If the line stays however often you sign in, the runner runs as another Windows user than the page (section 3.14). After "Afmelden" without "Opslaan" the line stays on purpose (section 3.14).
